CORAM: HONOURABLE MR. JUSTICE MIHIR KUMAR JHA
ORAL JUDGMENT
24.11.2015 All these writ petitions having identical issue of absorption in
the government service as teaching and/or non-teaching employees of
Sri Dhanwantari Ayurvedic College, Ahirauli, Buxar (hereinafter
referred to as 'the College') in terms of Section 6 of the Bihar Private
Medical (Indian System of Medicine) College (Taking Over) Act,
1985 (hereinafter referred to as 'the Act'), have been heard together
and are being disposed by this common judgment.
2. At the outset it must be recorded here that these writ
petitions are the aftermath of the judgment of this Court in the case of
Dr. Satyadeo Pandey & ors. v. the State of Bihar & ors., reported in
2005(2) PLJR 399, wherein the same issue with regard to absorption
of the services of the petitioners and others in the College had been

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 18
gone into and the writ petitions were disposed of with certain
observations and directions. Before this Court would examine as to
whether the observations and directions given in the case of Dr.
Satyadeo Pandey (supra) have been followed by the respondents in
letter and spirit, in course of taking its fresh decision and passing the
impugned order dated 17.10.2007 it would be absolutely necessary to
recaptituate of the basic facts has also been setout at length in the
exhaustive narration of events in the earlier judgment of this Court in
the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra).
3. In this regard what is very significant to be noted here is
that the College was established by the private individuals some time
in the year 1972 and was temporarily affiliated to Bihar University,
Muzaffarpur the basis of the decision of the State Government for the
academic sessions 1976-77 to 1980-81. Such temporary affiliation of
the College, however, was not extended beyond 1980-81 session and
consequently, there was ban on new admission in the college since
1981-82 session. After promulgation of the Act the College was
taken over by a notification dated 6.12.1986, with effect from
1.3.1983. Subsequently, as per judgment and order dated 12.7.1988 in
the writ petition bearing C.W.J.C.No. 992/1987 the date of taking
over of the college was modified and changed to 1.6.1986 in place of
1.3.1983.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 19
4. Section 6 of the Act, which has a direct bearing on the
result of these writ petitions, needs to be extracted and reproduced in
verbatim, which reads as follows:
" Determination of terms of the teaching staff and other
employees of the College.- (1) From the date of the notified
order, all the staff employed in the College shall cease to be
the employees of the college body.
 Provided that they shall continue to serve the College on ad
hoc basis till a decision under sub-section (3) and (4) of the
section is taken by the State Government.
 (2) The State Government will set up one or more
committees of experts and knowledgeable persons which will
examine the bio-data of each member of the teaching staff and
ascertain whether appointment, promotion or confirmation was
made in accordance with the Act, Statute or Regulations of the
University concerned and in keeping with the guidelines laid
down by the Indian Medical Council of India and take into the
consideration all other relevant materials including length of
the service in the College, and submit its report to the State
Government.
 (3) The State Government on receipt of the report of the
Committee or Committees, as the case may be, will decide in
respect of each member of teaching staff on the merits of each
case whether to absorb him in Government service or
terminate his service or to allow him to continue on an ad hoc
basis for a fixed term on contract and shall, where necessary,
re-determine the rank, pay, allowances and other conditions of
service.
 (4) The State Government shall similarly determine the term
of appointment and other conditions of service of other
categories of staff of the College on the basis of facts
ascertained either by a Committee or by an Officer or trusted
with the task and the provisions of sub-section (2) and (3) of
this section shall apply mutatis mutandis to such cases."
5. As would be evidenced from the aforesaid provisions and
in fact alike all Take Over Act of educational institutions in Bihar,
there was a provision even in the Act that from the date of takeover of
the College by the State Government teaching staff and other
employees of the College were only to continue by way of an adhoc
and interim arrangement till the State Government would take a

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 20
decision as with regard to their absorption in the Government service
or to terminate the service or even to allow them to continue on an
adhoc basis for a fixed term of contract. It was with this end in sight
that the State Government had by virtue of its notification dated 9th
December, 1986 initially taken over the College with certain
conditions, as would be better evidenced from the copy of the
notification dated 9th December, 1986, which reads as follows:

6. It would be also necessary to mention here that the date of
1.3.1983 in the aforementioned notification dated 9th December, 1986

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 21
got changed to 1.6.1986 under the direction given by this Court in the
judgment dated 12.7.1988 in C.W.J.C.No. 992/1987 and thereafter the
State Government had constituted a Screening Committee by its
notification dated 9.10.1990 and directed the Screening Committee to
submit its report to the State Government. It is an admitted fact that
such Screening Committee had submitted its report which was
published in Bihar Gazette on 15th January, 1992. On the basis of
such report submitted by the Screening Committee on 7th December,
1991 the State Government had taken a decision to absorb the services
of 103 teaching and non-teaching employees. Subsequently, a batch of
39 employees also had moved this Court and in view of the orders
passed in a contempt petition, M.J.C.No. 295/1985, 39 persons in
addition to 102 persons were also sought to be absorbed by a separate
order.
7. It was at this stage that a large number of other set of
persons claiming to be employees both teaching and non-teaching and
also claiming to be validly appointed employees of the College prior
to takeover with effect from 1.6.1986 had moved this Court with a
grievance that the Principal of the College had illegally omitted their
names from the list of teachers and non-teaching staff, as a result
whereof they could not be absorbed in the service of the College after
its takeover by the State. It in those writ petitions and in view of an

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 22
order dated 29.1.1992 passed by a Bench of this Court in the contempt
petition, bearing M.J.C.No. 529/1990 that the aforesaid writ petitions
were disposed of directing the respondents to constitute a Committee
of more than one responsible officer to look into the correctness or
otherwise of the allegations of the petitioners of those writ petitions
and whether they were entitled to continue on their posts after
takeover of the College. The Chief Secretary of the Government of
Bihar was directed to constitute a Committee in accordance with the
order of the writ Court within three weeks and the State Government
was directed to take a decision on the basis of the report to be
submitted by the Committee in accordance with the provisions of the
Act within one month from the date of submission of the report.
8. This Court in fact had also directed that a fresh Screening
Committee was to be constituted by the State Government consisting
of Secretary cum Health Commissioner, Department of Health, and
one more I.A.S. Officer holding a responsible post under different
department of the State Government and any member of Central
Council of Indigenous Medicine (CCIM). This Committee was
directed to examine the cases of the employees of the College
appointed till 1.6.1986 and submit a report to the State Government
within a period of six months from the date of constitution of the
Committee.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 23
9. It has to be kept in mind that as this decision had gone
against 102+ 39 already absorbed employees, they had moved this
Court with a grievance that there was no need for the Government to
constitute a fresh Committee and subject them also to fresh screening
with a view to take a decision for their absorption. Such two writ
petitions, being C.W.J.C.No. 4486/1991 and C.W.J.C.No. 4212/1992,
were disposed of by the judgment and order dated 12.5.1995, wherein
it was held that there was no need to review/ recall the earlier
judgment of this Court with regard to constituting a fresh Committee
for screening the cases of all the employees appointed in the College
prior to 1.6.1986. This Court in fact had also gone to hold that there
had been piecemeal scrutiny of the teachers and non-teaching staff of
the College at different stages after consideration of cases of 102 and
39 persons and in fact cases of 84 persons were also considered
subsequently, who had filed different writ petitions. In such
circumstances, this Court had held in its judgment dated 12.5.1995
that it was only just and proper that fresh scrutiny is made and a fresh
decision is taken regarding absorption or otherwise of all the
employees.
10. Thus, the State Government with a view to comply the
orders of this Court passed in the writ petition/ contempt petition had
resolved by its resolution dated 7.8.1998 to constitute a three

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 24
members Screening Committee (hereinafter referred to as the Second
Screening Committee) to scrutinize the cases of teaching and non-teaching employees appointed till 1.6.1986. The said Second
Screening Committee submitted its report dated 18.6.1999 and in the
light of the said report the concerned department of the State
Government issued notices to the concerned employees
communicating them the comments of the Second Screening
Committee relevant for the concerned employees and inviting
explanation. Thereafter fresh show cause notices were issued asking
the employees as to whey their services be not terminated and on
receipt of such show cause notices teaching and non-teaching
employees including the petitioners of these cases had submitted their
show cause reply. After examining such show cause reply by a
Committee constituted by the State Government, which had
considered the report of the Second Screening Committee, it had
accepted recommendation of the Committee for absorption of 23
teaching employees out of 84 and no non-teaching employees out of
167, whose cases were considered by the Second Screening
Committee, were found fit for absorption.
11. The State Government had finally issued an order dated
29.8.2003 containing the list of 229 persons whose services were not
found to be fit for absorption and they were accordingly terminated

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 25
from the service of the college. The petitioners to the exception of Dr.
Shiv Shankar Rai and four others being the petitoners of CWJC NO.
6102 of 2003, CWJC No. 7456 of 2003 and CWJC NO. 14980 of
2013 of these cases are also covered by the list of 229 persons
enclosed with the Government order dated 29.8.2003.
12. The case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey and its 11 analogous
cases in fact basically was filed assailing the aforementioned
Government order dated 29.8.2003 declaring 229 persons including
most of the petitioners of these cases not found fit for their absorption
and their consequential termination of their adhoc service in the
college. It is very very significant to note here that thisi Court, in the
case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra), did not interfere with the
impugned order dated 29.8.2003 terminating the services of adhoc
employees of the college including the petitioners and others, in all
229 persons, also declaring them to be not found fit for absorption and
had only remitted the matter back to the State Government with
certain observations and directions as would be borne out from the
following passages of the judgment of the case of Dr. Satyadeo
Pandey (supra):
"25. On a careful consideration of rival contentions on the
issue whether the Screening Committee took into consideration
yardsticks and criteria beyond the scope of Section-6 of the
Act while determining the validity of appointments of persons
claiming to be employees of the college and on consideration
of relevant facts noticed earlier, this court finds that to and
extent the Screening Committee committed error of law and
exceeded its jurisdiction in laying down several criteria beyond

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 26
law. The Screening Committee found that while the college
was under private control the management was not wholly
upto the mark, the college was not in healthy financial
condition and a number of illegal appointments were made
without proper advertisement in prominent newspaper of
Bihar. The Screening Committee has, on that account and on
account of alleged violation of the Employment Exchange
(Compulsory Notification of Vacancy) Act, 1959 and on
account of certain irregularities in the constitution and
functioning of the Governing Body in context of provisions of
Bihar Indigenous Medical Educational Institutions
(Regulations and Control) Act, 1982 hold most of
appointments of teaching and non-teaching employees to be
illegal.
26. On going through the report of the Screening Committee
in Annexure-c and through the alleged irregularities and
provisions of various Acts noticed above this Court of the
considered view that the Screening Committee failed to see
that Memo of such provisions and irregularities by the
erstwhile management and the governing body of the private
affiliated college could not rendered the appointments illegal
and void ab initio so as to disable the appointees from being
treated as employee of the college on the cut of date i.e.
1.6.1986. There is merit in the submissions of the petitioner
that in absence of any statutory obligation the governing body
of the private college had the power to make appointments
even without resorting to newspaper advertisements. But as
would appear from later discussions the governing body itself
chose to create an obligation by a valid and subsisting
resolution dated 25.3.1984 requiring newspaper advertisement
for further appointments. It is also found that the obligation of
notifying the vacancy to the Employment Exchange under the
relevant Act and Rules framed thereunder was not applicable
to the college when it was under private management. Unless
the irregularities of the governing body are found to be of such
a nature so as to amount to fraud in making appointments by
violating the basis norms set by itself in earlier resolutions or
by ignoring the requirement of basis qualification, the
irregularities would not affect the validity of appointment of
employees who continued in service of the college till its take
over .
27. The aforesaid view, however, requires further
clarification in the light of facts emerging from the report of
the Screening Committee and in the light of the submissions
advanced on behalf of the State. The Screening Committee had
found in paragraphs 3 and 5 of its report contained in
Annexure-c that the proceeding book after 24.4.1984 was
never signed by the President and he never attended the
subsequent meetings in which another person acted President
without any resolution of the governing body. The minutes of
subsequent meeting was found to have been recorded on the
orders of the Secretary as admitted by a clerk of the college.
The resolution of the governing body dated 25.3.1984 lay
down the requirement of future appointments only after
advertisement in specified Daily Newspapers which was not
followed. Photocopies of advertisement produced by claimants
appointed after 25.3.1984 were found to be forged. Obviously

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 27
appointments made after 25.3.1984 have been found, on proper
consideration of relevant facts not only to be against resolution
of the Governing Body but also tainted with fraud. Such
appointments have rightly been treated as invalid for the
purpose of absorption of employees by the State Government.
Obviously such illegal appointments made after 25.3.1984
when the issuance of Ordinance No. 1/85
was imminent or already done led to unusual increase in the
number of persons claiming to be employees of the college for
the purpose of absorption of their services under t he State
Government. The whole transaction of appointments after
25.3.1984 appears to be mala fide, for extraneous reasons and
tainted with fraud. This Court is not persuaded to issue any
Writ/Order or direction to the respondents for reconsideration
of cases of petitioners appointed after 25.3.1984. Their writ
petitions are, therefore, dismissed.
28. So far as petitioners who were appointed prior to
25.3.1984 are concerned, this Court finds that on account of
erroneous approach of the Screening Committee which took
into consideration certain irrelevant materials and provisions of
law as noticed earlier, beyond the permissible limits under law,
there cases require reconsideration by State Government. Since
the facts collected by the Screening Committee can be safely
used by the State Government or any Review Committee
which may be appointed by it for the purpose, this Court is of
the view that no useful purpose will be served by remitting the
matter in respect of employees appointed prior to 25.3.1984 for
re-examination by the Screening Committee. The report
submitted by the Screening Committee shall be used by the
State Government or any Review Committee that may be
constituted by the State Government to help it to come to a
proper decision, by keeping in mind the findings of this Court
recorded earlier in this judgment pointing out the illegalities in
the approach of the Screening Committee. Such illegalities
must be avoided by treating that the offending parts of the
report of the Screening Committee in respect of such
employees has been passed by this Court. Only the balance
part of the report of the Screening Committee shall be used by
the State Government or the Review Committed for coming to
a just and proper decision in respect of absorption of
employees of the college appointed prior to 25.3.1984. For this
purpose the cases of all such petitioners is remitted back to the
State Government. It can look into validity of appointment in
any particular case on the ground of fraud or appointment
being void ab initio for lack of basic academic qualification or
into facts showing termination/cessation of service before the
date 25.3.1984 or the cut off date etc. But none of the criteria
disapproved earlier by this order shall be applied for this
purpose.
29. Since large number of persons have joined together as
petitioners in one or the other writ petitions under
consideration hence this Court is faced with great difficulty in
examining their individual grievances of the nature that:-
(i) They must be absorbed against the posts which they held
before the takeover because they have the requisite
qualification for such posts, and
(ii) They have the requisite experience for absorption on the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 28
post to which they were appointed/ promoted by the Governing
Body. Such grievances of individual employees which may
have been raised by them in their comments to the show cause
notice should be considered by the concerned authority in
accordance with law. For the purpose of general guidance in
this matter on account of submissions advanced in some of the
writ petitions it is indicated that for the purpose of
qualification and experience of teachers guidance should be
taken from the guidelines laid down by the CCIM which have
been annexed with the supplementary counter affidavit of the
State. Even the earlier guidelines in this matter have been
already noticed and set out in paragraph 9 of the judgment and
order dated 12.5.1995 contained in Annexure B. These
qualifications for teaching staff is applicable even to the basic
post of Demonstrator which was then in existence for the
higher post of Professor, Reader and Lecturer the admitted
teaching experience required is laid down as 10 years, 5 years
and 3 years respectively.
37. Reverting back to general discussions, as noticed earlier,
the Screening Committee has not considered an important
aspect as to how many sanctioned posts were available in the
College. The notification of take over in Annexure 1 contains
an important condition that before absorption of employee of
the College the State Government shall create admissible
gazetted and non-gazetted posts as per yardsticks of CCIM. It
has also been noticed that there is no material to indicate that
such exercise has been done by the State Government. In order
to avoid any delay and legal complications in future, the State
Government is hereby directed to first complete the exercise of
creating the admissible posts as per condition (Gha) of
Annexure 1 before notifying its final decision in respect of
absorption of the petitioners in accordance with this judgment
and order. In case the number of post is more than number of
employees ultimately found eligible for absorption then there
shall be no problem but if the case is reverse than absorption in
various posts shall normally be on the basis of length of
service of the eligible employee or such similar relevant
consideration depending upon relevant facts as appears to have
been done in other two taken-over Colleges. The rank and
other conditions of the service of eligible employees may be
suitably altered if such re-designation can accommodate
eligible employees for absorption as per their qualification and
experience against available vacancy created by the
Government as per CCIM norms. Such course of action shall
be in accordance with discretion vested in the State
Government by section 6(3) of the Act. After remand the entire
exercise should be completed by the State Government at an
early date preferably within four months and in any case
within six months because the matter has become quite old."

13. After the judgment of the learned Single Judge in the
case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra), it also became final on account

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 29
of the Division Bench judgment of this Court refusing to interfere
with the observations and directions given by the learned Single Judge
in L.P.A.No. 1155/2005 and its analogous cases, wherein all that was
held by the Division Bench in its order dated 1.11.2006 reads as
follows:
" So far as the merits of these appeals are concerned, they are,
also, considered at the admission stage after being addressed
by learned counsel for the Government.
 The learned Single Judge has remanded the case of the
concerned original writ petitioners for reconsideration of the
matter in the light of the observations. The learned counsel for
the State submitted that the remand is not questioned. Then,
obviously, the remand order is only to the extent of
reconsideration of the case and if remand order is not
questioned, the question of these appeals being entertained
would not arise under clause 10 of the Letters Patent.
 These appeals shall, accordingly, stand dismissed at the
admission stage."
14. Thus, after the judgment of the learned Single Judge in
the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) acquired finality, the State
Government by an order dated 23.7.2007 in keeping with the
observations made in the judgment of this Court in the case of Dr.
Satyadeo Pandey (supra) had constituted a Review Committee, which
had examined the qualification of 165 teaching and non-teaching
employees and in its report dated 23.7.2007 had recommended for
absorption of 24 teaching employees and 42 non-teaching employees
and rest 99 were not found fit for absorption. The petitioners of these
cases are among those 99 persons except Dr. Shiva Shankar Rai the
sole petitioner in C.W.J.C. No. 6102 of 2003 and three more persons

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 30
who are petitioners in C.W.J.C. no. 7456 of 2003 as well as sole
petitioner in CWJC No. 14980 of 2013. As a mater of fact, save and
except, Dr. Shiva Shankar Rai, none amongst the petitoners are in
service of the college for more than last 12 years.
15. To complete the narration of events which took place
after the judgment of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) and its affirmance
by the Division Bench in L.P.A.No.1155/2005 and its analogous cases
by an order dated 9.11.2006 it has to be mentioned here that the State
Government by the notification dated 11.4.2007 had constituted a
Review Committee of five officials, which reads as follows:

recommending absorption of only 24 teaching and Gazetted
employees and 42 non-teaching employees. Such report being sheet
anchor of the Government decision and having a direct bearing on the
result of these cases is also reproduced hereinbelow:

17. It is very significant to record here that after the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 35
aforesaid report of the Review Committee was submitted that the
Padwarg Samiti of the State Government, the competent body to
create and sanction post on a proposal submitted by the Health
Department had recommended for creation and sanction of 191 posts
for the college in keeping with the norms of Central Commercial
Ingigenous Medicine (CCIM) for teaching of 50 students in the
degree course of Ayurveda and 100 beded hospital. The
recommendation of Padwarg Samiti as was duly notified and
circulated is also reproduced hereinbelow:

18. What is more significant to be noted here is that the
Government however in view of the report of Review Committee had
taken a decision to sanction only 66 posts for those persons who were
found fit to be absorbed by the Review Committee. The Government

order dated 16.10.2007 as communicated to the Accountant General
in this regard reads as follows:

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 41

Kki la[;k& 16@,3&12@07&902ºns0fp0½@iVuk@ fnukad 17 vDVwcj 2007**
20. Thus this Court would go into the correctness of the
aforesaid impugned order, it must make it clear that the scope of the
writ petitions in view of the orders and directions given in the
judgment of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra), which has become final
and binding between the parties and cannot be reopened, is very very
limited and has to remain confined to determination of the issues
remitted by this Court in the light of the following findings,
observation and directions, namely:
(i) In order to give effect to the provisions of Section
6(2)(3)(4) of the Act, the State Government, before
taking a final decision in the matter of absorption,
must create posts with appropriate pay and
emoluments against which only employees can be
absorbed in the service of the State Government as a
result of take over their services. This exercise was
necessary to avoid legal complications and undue
burden upon the State in case number of employees
eligible for absorption becomes larger than the
number of posts which could be sanctioned on the
basis of facts existing on the date of take over in

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 42
accordance with the yardstick of Central Council of
Indian Medicine of India (hereinafter referred to as
the CCIM”). Since, such exercise had not been done
by the State Government, a direction was issued to
the State Government to first complete the exercise of
creating the admissible posts as per condition (Gh) of
the notification of the take over dated 09.12.1986
before notifying its final decision in respect of
absorption of the petitioners and others in according
with the findings and the directions in the case of Dr.
Satyadeo Pandey (supra).
(ii) It was also observed that in case of number of
employees found eligible for absorption were more
than number of sanctioned posts, absorption on
various posts has to be made on the basis of length of
service of the eligible employee or such similar
consideration depending upon relevant facts as was
also done in the case of two other taken over
colleges.
(iii) The rank and other condition of service of eligible
employee could be suitably altered if such
redesignation could accommodate eligible employees

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 43
for absorption as per their qualification and
experience against available vacancies created by the
Government as per CCIM norms inasmuch as such
course of action would be in accordance with
discretion vested in the State Government by Section
6(3) of the Act.
(iv) The validity of the appointment, promotion or
confirmation of the persons seeking absorption had to
be made in accordance with University Act/Statutes
or Regulations and in keeping with the guidelines laid
down by the Medical Council of India. In this regard,
it was held that the word “verify” used in clause (Ga)
of Notification dated 09.12.1986 would make it
obligatory for the Review Committee to satisfy itself
regarding validity of the appointment of the claimant
employee and that he was still in service on the cut
off date and in the process of verification of the
persons seeking absorption, the Screening Committee
and the State Government would take into account
minimum eligibility and teaching qualification of
individual employee.
(v) The requirement of publication of vacancies for

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 44
appointment in the newspaper had to be examined in
the light of statutory obligation and/or practice
followed by the college, but then obligation of
notifying the vacancies to the Employment Exchange
(Compulsory Notification of Vacancy) Act, 1959 and
Rules framed thereunder being not applicable to the
college when it was under private management, could
not be made the criteria for rejecting the claim of
absorption of the teaching and non-teaching
employees. In this regard, it was also held that the
irregularities of the Governing Body should be of
such a nature so as to amount fraud in making
appointment by violating the basic norms set by itself
in earlier resolution or by ignoring the requirement of
basis qualification.
(vi) Appointments made in the college after 25.03.1984
being tainted with fraud, mala fide and for extraneous
reasons, no reconsideration was required of such
persons including the persons who were appointed
after 25.03.1984.
(vii) Persons appointed prior to 25.03.1984 and continuing
in service on the cut off date i.e., 1986, were to be

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 45
considered for their absorption on the basis of facts
collected by the Second Screening Committee which
could be used by the State Government or any
Review Committee that could be appointed by it.
(viii) The State Government/ Review Committee must
avoid the infirmities committed by the earlier
Screening Committee in rejecting the case of
absorption on such norms which were held to be
illegal such as applicaton of Employment Exchange
(Compulsory Notificaton of Vacancy) Act, 1959 or
irregularity in constitution and functioning of the
Governing Body or management of the college to be
not up to mark or poor financial condition of the
college before take over or appointments made by the
Governing Body without advertisement in leading
newspaper.
(ix) While remitting the matter to the State Government,
it was clarified that the State Government could look
into the validity of appointment in any particular case
on account of fraud or appointment being void ab
initio for lack of basis academic qualification or into
the facts showing termination/cessation of service

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 46
before 25.03.1984 or on the cut off date i.e.,
01.06.1986.
(x) For the purposes of qualification and experience of
teachers, guidance should be taken from the guideline
laid down by CCIM and in keeping in view para-9 of
the judgment and order dated 12.05.1995 in CWJC
No. 4486 of 1992 (Dr. Vijay Shankar Pandey & Ors.
Vs. The State of Bihar & Ors.). Para-9 whereof reads
as under:
“9. As a matter of fact, as noticed above, the
petitioners are themselves aggrieved with the
screening of their cases vide report dated 2.1.92.
They have been found eligible for the absorption
but for the post of Medical Officer, a lower post
than the one claimed by them. It is submitted
that the petitioners possess the qualification laid
down by the Central Council of Indian Medicine
under Section 36 of the Indian Medicine Central
Council Act, 1970 for the post of Demonstrator
(Dr. Yamuna Pd. Mishra is said to be eligible for
the still higher posts of Lecturer, Reader and
Professor) and, therefore, the finding of the
Screening Committee and the decision of the
State Government to absorb them on the post of
Medical Officer is arbitrary. The petitioners
have referred to clause-18 of the Brochure of the
Central Council laying down, inter alia, the
qualifications for teaching staff in Ayurvedic
Degree Colleges. The relevant clause of the
Brochure runs as follows:
“18. Qualifications prescribed for Teaching
Staff:
I. ESSENTIAL:
a. Degree Diploma in Ayurved from a
University established by law or a
statutory Board/Faculty/Examining
Body of Indian Medicine and
equivalent.
Or
Ayurvediacharya of All India Ayurved
Vidyapeeth
Or
Other eminent Ayurvedic Scholars of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 47
established repute though not having
any degree/diploma, but fit for teaching
Ayurvedic subjects.
b. Teaching experience in any institution
for ten years, five years and three years
for the post of Professor, Reader and
Lecturer respectively.
c. Knowledge of Sanskrit.
II. DESIRABLE:
a. Post Graduate qualification in Ayurved
from a recognized institution/university
established by law.
b. Original published papers/books on the
subject.”
(xi) In case of validly appointed employees of the college,
the approval of appointment or confirmation from the
University would not go to the root of the validity of
service after he had completed the required years of
experience by the date of confirmation and in such
cases yardstick adopted in two other taken over
Ayurvedic colleges on the basis of experience as per
requirement of the post on the date of take over was
permissible against the lower post or another post as
per qualification and experience till the cut off date as
decided by this Court in the order dated 22.06.2000
in CWJC NO 3469 of 1999 wherein it was held as
follows:
“ I may mention that the authorities are required
to screen the candidature of one or other
employee of the college strictly in accordance
with provision of section 6 of Take Over Act,
1985 without being influenced by any other
guidelines, except law, if any, laid down by the
Central Council of Indian Medicines and law
laid down by the court of law. They are also
required to screen the candidature of the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 48
employees of the college in similar manner as
made in respect of other similarly situated take
over colleges.”
(xii) For the post of Laboratory Technician, absorption
should be decided on the basis of that if the Bihar
University, at the relevant time, had laid down
qualification for the post in question, the same should
be made yardstick for deciding the claim of
Laboratory Technician otherwise the State
Government will be at liberty to apply the
qualification laid down by it if such decision was in
existence at the time of appointment.
(xiii) For the purpose of absorption, the Screening
Committee report with regard to act of omission and
commission of the conduct of the employee while
working in the college including passing examination
as an independent candidate not otherwise
permissible can be made a ground for rejecting the
case of absorption.
(xiv) Teaching experience gained by the employees after
the cut off date shall not be taken into consideration
for the purposes of claiming justification to the
promotion given by the Governing Body.
(xv) When a teacher had completed three years of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 49
teaching experience as Demonstrator prior to the cut
off date but had been promoted to the post of
Lecturer before acquiring such experience then he
should be absorbed as Lecturer as was done in the
case of other two taken over colleges and the same
principle shall be also kept in mind for deciding
claims for higher post of Reader or Professor and the
absorption should be made as regular teachers and
not on adhoc basis.
21. The impugned order dated 17.10.2007 observing the
challenge of the petitioners to the aforementioned Government
decision dated 17.10.2007 absorbing only 66 of the teaching and non-teaching employees in the college and leaving the petitioners and
others in all 99 of them, is based primarily on the following grounds:
(i) The directions given this Court in the judgment of Dr.
Satyadeo Pandey (supra), has not been followed in
letter and spirit inasmuch as the Review Committee
has again taken into consideration those very
infirmities which were held to be bad in the judgment
of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra). This aspect has been
developed by the learned counsel for the petitioners
in various forms such as the requirement of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 50
qualification and experience, the necessity of
advertisement, the power of appointment of the
Secretary vis-à-vis Governing Body and the norms
applied by the Review Committee in allowing or
refusing absorption.
(ii) On behalf of the teaching employees, it has been
emphasized that Statutes framed by the Bihar
University having been published in the year 1986 in
the Bihar University Calendar could not be made
applicable to the cases of the petitioners who were
appointed in the college on teaching post in between
1979-84.
(iii) The cut of date being 09.12.1986 as per the take over
notification dated 09.12.1986, the Review Committee
was not justified in examining the mode of
appointment of the petitioners and it ought to have
only examined the qualification and experience of the
petitioners as on 01.06.1986.
(iv) As with regard to non-teaching employees, it was
submitted that unlike the teaching employees for
whom there may have been provisions made in the
Statutes of the University or by CCIM laying down

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 51
qualification and experience, there was no prescribed
qualification either by the University or by the CCIM
and, therefore, absorption of the non-teaching
employees should not have been refused on the
ground of lack of qualification and experience.
(v) The State Government in fact ought to have
followed the same yardstick and policy in the matter
of absorption of the non-teaching employees of the
college as was done in the case of two other taken
over Ayurvedic Colleges of Darbhanga and
Bhagalpur wherein the services and experience of the
concerned employees were taken over on the basis of
their qualification and experience as on cut off date
of take over the college.
(vi) The Notification of Deobrat Narain Singh as a
member of the Screening Committee dated
07.08.1998 and again of the Review Committee dated
11.04.2007 was impermisslble in view of order dated
11.2.1999 passed in CWJC No. 1336 of 1999 and as
such the impugned order dated 17.10.2007 allowing
absorption of 66 persons and rejecting the case of 99
others including the petitioners is bad on this ground

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 52
alone.
(vii) The Review Committee had wrongly taken into
consideration the norms of CCIM framed in the year
2007 whereas it is the CCIM norms of the year 1986
which had to be taken into consideration and if that
was done, the absorption of the petitioners on the post
of Assistant Clerk was very much permissible.
(viii) The total number of post as per 1986 CCIM norms
admissible to the college was 191 which though
created under the recommendation of the Prashashi
Padvarg Samiti but the Government had confined it
to creation and sanction of only 66 posts for such
persons who were found fit by the Review
Committee and therefore, the petitioners were refused
absorption on a non est and non-existing ground.
(ix) The rejection of the case of absorption of the non-teaching employees on the post of Clerk on the
ground that they did not possession the knowledge of
typing was based on irrelevant and extraneous
grounds inasmuch as the State Government itself for
its own employees had issued the order dated
21.01.1984 wherein knowledge of typing for the post

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 53
On
26.11.2015
of Clerk was not an essential but only optional
qualification.
(x) The impugned decision dated 17.10.2007 passed on
the finding of the Second Screening Committee
report is bad because there was specific direction in
the judgment of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) to
ignore the report of the Second Screening Committee.
To be continued……….
22. It must be noted here that these submissions, part of
which are also borne out from the pleadings of the records and part of
which are by way of oral submission without any foundation of fact
laid down in the pleadings, were also not answered in the few counter
affidavits which have been filed in these cases which to say the least
is merely of an ornamental value. Thus, it became essential for this
Court to discover the facts from original sources.
23. This Court had accordingly called for the original
records of the State Government and the file No. 16/A3-12/2007,
produced by Mr. D.K.Sinha, learned AAG-2, which goes to show that
a Review Committee was constituted by the State Government on
11.04.2007 consisting of 05 members to comply the order of this
Court in the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra). The fact, which has
been duly recorded in the order sheet dated 18.04.2007 in MJC No.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 54
76 of 2006 (Dr. Kamta Prasad Mishra vs. the State of Bihar & Ors.),
wherein it was recorded that since the State Government had
constituted the Review Committee of 05 members, which had to
examine the case of 142 persons and accordingly 02 months time was
granted by the said order dated 18.04.2007 to complete the exercise
by the Review Committee and also to the fact to take a final decision
on the issue of absorption by the Government on receipt of the report
of the Review Committee.
24. From perusal of the aforesaid file, it would become clear
that the Review Committee, consisted of 04 members namely Sri
Amrendra Nraian Singh, in capacity of Chairman of the Committee, J
Ansari, Joint Secretary, Health Department, Dr. A.Mishra, Director
Indigenous Medicines and Dr. Deobrat Narain Singh, Superintendent,
Government Ayurvedic College, Patna in capacity of members, had
submitted the report on 23.07.2007. Whereafter a proposal for
creation of 191 posts involving expenditure of Rs. 2,90,91000/- was
submitted before the Prasashi Padvarg Samiti of the State
Government and on 23.08.2007, Prasashi Padvarg Samiti consisting
of Chief Secretary, Development Commissioner, Additional Finance
Commissioner and the Principal Secretary of the Personnel
Department had approved creation of 191 posts, which was
communicated by the Finance Department, vide Memo No. 6569

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 55
dated 12.09.2007.
25. The whole matter thereafter was placed before the
Council of Ministers vide a memorandum in Memo No. 808 dated
21.09.2007 for according of sanction of the Government for creation
of 191 posts as per the resolution of the Prasashi Padvarg Samiti. It
further transpires that on 9.10.2007, the Council of Ministers had
given sanction of creation of only 66 posts for 66 employees, who
were found fit for absorption in the report of the Review Committee
whereafter, the impugned order was passed in form of Notification
No. 902 dated 17.10.2007 absorbing the services of only 66 persons.
Thus, from the Government file, it becomes very clear that the
directions of this Court in the case of Satyadeo Pandey (supra) were
carried out in letter and spirit .
26. From perusal of the Government file, two of the main
submissions made by the learned counsel for the petitioners get very
easily answered. Firstly, that the directions of this Court in the case of
Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) with specific reference to paragraph 18
and 37 were carried out in the sense that firstly, the Review
Committee had analysed the service history of all the 191 persons
whose cases were examined after the order passed by this Court in
the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra). It has to be kept in mind that
the Review Committee has not rejected the case of any of the persons

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 56
on the ground of lack of sanction post and in fact when the report of
the Review Committee and the post sought to be created under the
recommended of Prashashi Padvarg Samiti, were placed before the
State Government for its decision. It had only concurred with the
report of the Review Committee for absorbing the services of 66
persons for the reasons given therein and in fact had made its own
independent exercise even in respect of some of the teaching
employees by accommodating them on equivalent post of Ayurvedic
Medical Officer. Thus, it cannot be said that the order of this Court
with regard to creation of post was not followed. The availability of
191 posts being there as per the recommendation of Prashashi
Padvarg Samiti , this Court in fact even now will have no difficulty in
allowing the absorption of some of the petitioners if they otherwise
fulfil the requirement in terms of Section 6(2)(3) & (4) of the Act.
27. Another submission with regard to nomination of Dr.
Deobrat Narain Singh, as a Member of the Screening Committee and
Review Committee, gets answered again from the Government file .
Firstly, when Dr. Deobrat Narain Singh was made Member of the
Screening Committee on 7.8.1998 and when on the basis of the said
report of the Screening Committee, the termination order of
petitioners and 223 others were passed on 29.8.2003, the same
became the subject matter of challenge in the case of Dr. Satyadeo

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 57
Pandey (supra) and others. In this regard, from the findings recorded
in the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra), it would become clear
that the report of the Screening Committee was not held to be bad on
account of participation of Dr. Deobrat Narain Singh. In fact, this
Court had only found certain criteria followed by the second
Screening Committee to be bad as such application of Employment
Exchange Act and other statutory provisions which were not
enforceable in the case of college, but the report of the second
Screening Committee was not held to be bad on account of
participation of Dr. Deobrat Narain Singh.
28. As a matter of fact, this Court would again read into the
order dated 18.04.2007 in M.J.C. No. 76 of 2006 (Dr. Kamta Prasad
Mishra vs. the State of Bihar & Ors.) and approval to the nomination
of Dr. Deobrat Narain Singh as a member of the Review Committee
inasmuch as this Court on 18.04.2007, on being produced the
notification constituting Review Committee consisting of Dr. Deobrat
Narain Singh had not only approved the same but also directed the
said Review Committee to complete its exercise within two months.
29. Added to all these, personal bias being raised again. Dr.
Deobrat Narain Singh, he has not been impleaded party to any of the
writ petition. The plain submission that there was an embargo in
CWJC No. 1336 of 1999 against Dr. Deobrat Narain Singh will also

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 58
be of no help and avail inasmuch as he himself as a member of the
Review Committee on 11.4.2007, had sought his refusal by filing an
application before the State Government on 26.6.2007, but the
Government had refused such request of Dr. Deobrat Narain Singh on
27.6.2007 after recording as follows:
