30. This Court would, in fact, find that Dr. Deobrat Narain
Singh was definitely not biased and prejudiced and in fact not a
single instance has been cited in the different writ petition filed on
this score. As a matter of fact, there was no question of Dr. Deobrat
Narain Singh being in any way personally interested in the non-absorption of any of the petitioners on the non-teaching or even
teaching post specially when he was a confirmed teacher of
Government Ayurvedic College and his seniority could not have been
questioned in any manner by anyone of these taken over colleges.
Thus, this Court also does not find any merit in the submission of
impugned order becoming bad on account of Dr. Deobrat Narain
Singh being a member of the Review Committee.
31. The next question on which the records were to be again
hunted this time from the office of the Hon’ble Chancellor, was with
regard to date of enforcement of Chapter XVI-B of the Statutes of the
Bihar University. It has to be kept in mind that this college was an
affiliated college to Bihar University and Bihar University had
already framed statutes for appointments of teaching employees in
Ayurvedic Medical College under Chapter XVI-B, with the heading
“Number, Grades, Qualifications, Pay Scales, Mode of Appointment
of Teachers and Officers in the Faculty of Ayurveda” Relevant
portion whereof reads as follows:

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 60
"1. The following shall be grades, pay scales and minimum
qualifications of teachers and officers in the faculty of
Ayurveda:-
(a) Director/ Principal.
 Pay Scales:- 1200-45-1515-EB-57-1800 plus rent free
quarter or 10% of pay in lieu thereof.
 Qualifications:- Degree in Ayurveda from a University or a
Board/ Council established by a State or Central Government
and recognized by the University, with at least 15 years of
teaching experience in a recognized Ayurvedic College of
which not less than 5 years should be as Head of a Department.
(b) Professor.
 Pay Scales:- 800-30-1100-45-1190-EB-52-1450
 Qualifications:- Degree in Ayurveda from a University or a
Board/ Council established by a State or Central Government
and recognized by the University with at least 10 years of
teaching experience in a recognized Ayurvedic College.
 or
 A Post-graduate degree in Ayurveda from a University or
from an institution established by a State or Central
Government and recognized by the University and the Central
Council of Indian Medicines, with at least 7 years of teaching
experience in a recognized Ayurvedic College.
(c) Reader.
 Pay Scales- 650-30-770-EB-35-875-EB-45-1100-EB-50-
1300.
 Qualifications- Degree in Ayurveda from a University or a
Board/ Council established by a State or Central Government
and recognized by the University with at least 7 years of
teaching experience in a recognized Ayurvedic College.
 or
 A Post-graduate degree in Ayurveda from a University or an
Institution established by a State or Central Government and
recognized by the University and the Central Council of Indian
Medicines, with at least 5 years of teaching experience in a
recognized Ayurvedic College.
(d) Lecturer
 Pay Scale- 610-20-670-30-940-EB-35-1155.
 Qualifications:- Degree in Ayurveda from a University or a
Board/ Council Established by a State or Central Government
and recognized by the University with at least 3 years of
teaching experience in a recognized Ayurvedic College, or
with at least 3 years experience as a Medical Officer in
Government/ University Dispensary.
 Provided further that a holder of M.B.B.S. degree from any
recognized University shall also be eligible for appointment in
Anatomy Physiology and Bio-Chemistry, Pathology,
Jurisprudence, Health and Hygiene and holder of M.Sc. degree
will be eligible for Basic Science in the subject concerned.
(e) Demonstrator.
 Pay Scale:- 550-20-610-30-670-E.B.-30-940.
 Qualifications:- Degree in Ayurveda from a recognized
University or Board/ Council established by a State or Central
Government and recognized by the University except that in
Science subjects (i.e. Physics, Chemistry and Biology) the
minimum qualification shall be a 2nd Class Master's degree of
a recognized University in the subject concerned.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 61
(f) Dy. Superintendent of Hospital.
 Pay Scale: 610-20-670-30-940-EB-35-1155.
 Qualifications:- Degree in Ayurveda from a University or a
Board/ Council established by a State or Central Government
and recognized by the University with at least 3 years of
teaching experience in a recognized Ayurvedic College or with
at least 3 years experience as a Medical Officer in
Government/ University Dispensary.
(g) Resident Medical Officer/ Inspector of Herbal garden/
Vaidya.
 Pay scale: 550-20-610-30-670-EB-30-940
 Qualifications:- Degree in Ayurveda from a recognized
University or Board/ Council established by a State or Central
Government and recognized by the University.
2. In special cases appointment on any higher initial pay within
the above scale may be made.
3. Notwithstanding anything to the contrary as contained in the
Statutes, the qualification of a teacher or officer already in
service and confirmed before the date these Statutes came into
force, shall be considered equivalent to the minimum
qualification prescribed for the post he was then holding.
4. The number of posts in each category of teachers and
officers shall be determined by the Syndicate in consultation
with the Academic Council from time to time keeping in view
the number of students in each subject in the college/ Institute
and the required number of posts shall be created by the
Statutes.
5. The posts of Director/ Principal and Professor shall be filled
up by open advertisement on all India basis. In the categories
of Readers and Lecturers one-third of the posts shall be filled
up by promotion according to the roster (i.e. first post by
promotion, second and third posts by advertisement, fourth
post by promotion and so on), provided sufficient number of
suitable persons are eligible for promotion. All other posts
shall be filled up by direct recruitment.
6. (1) All appointments (except temporary appointment not
exceeding six months) shall be made by the Governing Body
of the college concerned on the recommendation of the Bihar
College Service Commission. Every appointment is subject to
the approval of the Syndicate. No such appointment shall be
approved unless, the post existed at the time of the
commencement of the Statutes of this Chapter or has been
created after that date with the sanction of the Syndicate.
(2) Within a fortnight of the appointment of any teacher or
teachers made by the Governing Body, the Secretary of the
college shall forward to the University, all relevant papers
alongwith the resolution of the Governing Body regarding the
appointment made for the approval of the University.
7. The Syndicate shall, after consideration and enquiry, if any,
communicate its approval or disapproval of any or all such
appointments to the Secretary concerned. No teacher shall be
allowed to join his substantive post unless his appointment has
been previously approved by the Syndicate.
8. Notwithstanding anything contained in the Statutes, it shall
be permissible for the G.B. of any admitted college to make an
appointment on a temporary basis for a period not exceeding
six months to any post and to permit the person so appointed to

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 62
join his post, provided that a report of such appointment shall
be made to the Syndicate immediately thereafter."
32. The main argument however with regard to
aforementioned Statutes was that since it was published in the
Calendar (Compilation of Statutes) of the Bihar University in a book
form in the year 1986, the same must be held to be framed but also
approved by the Chancellors for its enforcement in the year 1986 and
as such the cases of the petitioners appointed in between year 1979 to
25.03.1984 would not be covered by the same. It was very difficult
for this Court to accept the submission s for a simple reason because
the same book i.e., Calendar (Compilation of Statute) of Bihar
University though published in 1986 had also contained the statutes
under Chapter XVI-A with heading “Condition of service of non-teaching employees of the affiliated colleges” which was assented to
by the Chancellor on 18.08.1972. This Court however in order to be
sure of the date of enforcement of Statutes contained in Chapter
XVIB had passed an order on 27.07.2015, relevant portion whereof
reads as follows:
“Heard learned counsel for the parties at
length in respect of C.W.J.C.No. 10417/2008 wherein
one of the issues raised by the learned Senior counsel
(Mr. Shivendra Kishore) is that since the petitioner
Dharamdeo Pathak was appointed on 13.8.1976 on the
post of Lecturer in Science despite having a qualification
of only Graduation (B.Sc.Hons.) and that he was
assigned teaching duty in the Department of Darvagun
on 26.1.1981 as also made Garden Manager, whereafter
he became a permanent Lecturer on 25.2.1981 and
eventually earned promotion on the post of Reader in the
department of Darvagun on 25.1.1984 as also on the post
of Professor in the department of Darvagun on 5.7.1985,

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 63
he will be entitled for being absorbed on teaching post
keeping in view the requirement laid down in the
Statutes III under Chapter XVI-B of the Statutes
(Number, Grades, Qualifications, Pay Scales, Mode of
Appointment of Teachers and Officers in the Faculty of
Ayurveda), published in Bihar University Calendar in
1986, which lays down that any person who has already
been appointed prior to coming into force of the Statute
will be deemed to have qualified for the post held by
him. This argument will eventually bring this Court to
find out the date of framing of the Statutes.
The submission of Mr. Shivendra Kishore is that
since it has been incorporated in the Bihar University
Calendar published in the year 1986 it will be deemed to
have been framed only in the year 1986.
 This Court, however, wants to be assured about
framing of the date of the Statutes as contained in
Chapter XVI-B of the Bihar University Calendar Vol.II.
This Court has, accordingly, requested Mr.
Y.V.Giri, learned Senior counsel appearing on behalf of
the Chancellor as also Mr. Vikash Ratan Bharti, learned
counsel for the University as well as Mr. D.K.Sinha,
learned Senior counsel for the State, to come out with
the specific case of the date when the Statute, as
contained in Chapter XVI-B came into force after
getting the consent of the State Government and assent
of the Hon’ble Chancellor.
33. The aforesaid issue of enforcement of statutes contained
in Chapter XVI-B had ultimately got answered by an affidavit filed on
behalf of the learned Chancellor, sworn by his Officer on Special
Duty in CWJC No. 6464 of 2008 clearly stating that the date of
assentation of the Chancellor on the Statute under Chapter XVI B was
07.08.1979. The relevant portion of the affidavit filed by the learned
Chancellor’s office in this regard reads as follows:
 "3. That present affidavit is being filed in pursuant to
the direction of this Hon'ble Court dated 08.15, to bring
on record the date on which the learned Chancellor of
the Universities of Bihar was pleased to accord his
assent to the Statute as described in Chapter XVI-B in
the Bihar University Calendar, Volume II (Statutes)
printed in the year 1986, which relates to the 'name,
grades, qualification, pay scales and mode of
appointment of teachers and officers of the University in
the faculty of Ayurveda'.
4. That it is respectfully and humbly submitted that the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 64
Inter University Board (hereinafter referred to as the
IUB) in its meeting held on 9.2.79 vide resolution no.13
had appointed a committee consisting of the Deputy
Chairman, IUB, the Vice Chancellor, Bihar University
and the Vice Chancellor, Sanskrit University to examine
the draft statutes relating to Ayurvedic and Homeopathic
Colleges of Bihar University, Muzaffarpur and Sanskrit
University, Darbhanga. It was also decide that on receipt
of the recommendation of the committee, the approval of
the Education Department and Health Department may
be obtained and thereafter the Chairman of the IUB may
approve the recommendation on behalf of the IUB. A
meeting of the Committee was held on 9.3.79 in the
Bihar University, Muzaffarpur. The Committee finalized
the draft statutes relating to (i) admission and exclusion
of colleges in the faculty of Ayurveda, (ii) number,
grades, qualifications, pay scales etc. of teachers and
officers of the University in faculty of Ayurveda; (iii)
admission and exclusion of homeopathic colleges and
(iv) qualification for the teachers of homeopathic
colleges. These drafts sent to the Education Department
and Health Department for approval. A meeting of the
Committee was again held in the IUB office on 29.7.79
to consider the draft statutes in the light of the
suggestions made by the Education Department and
Health Department. Accordingly, the Deputy Chairman
of the IUB, vide his letter dated 20.7.79 requested the
learned Chancellor to accord assent to the Statutes
mentioned in the aforesaid letter. The letters received
from the Education Department bearing Memo No. K/M
10147/75 E479 dated 17.3.79 and the letter received
from the Health Department bearing memo no.
INDAME(H) M319/78424 (I.M.) dated 17.3.79 were
also annexed with the letter of the Deputy Chairman,
IUB.
5. That Deputy Secretary to the Governor of Bihar,
vide his letter dated 20.8.79 informed the Vice
Chancellor, K.S.D.Sanskrit University, Darbhanga, that
learned Chancellor was pleased to accord his assent to
the Statutes on 7.8.79. The letter of the Deputy Secretary
to the Governor of Bihar was enclosed with the copies of
the draft Statutes which were referred to as the
Statement marked as 'A', Statement marked as 'B' and
Statement marked as 'C'."
34. If the said statutes are taken into consideration, none of the
petitioners barring Dr. Sheo Shankar Rai, would pass the test as per
the requirement of the statutes of the qualification laid down for the
post of Professor/Reader/Lecturer/Demonstrator. It must be kept in
mind that the CCIM Norms was in fact prevalent prior to 07.08.1979

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 65
in view of provisions made in the CCIM Act, 1970 prescribing the
following qualification for the teaching staffs:-
"13. Qualifications prescribed for Teaching Staff:
Essential:
(a) Degree (diploma in Ayurved from a University
established by law or a statutory Board/ Faculty/
Examining Body of Indian Medicine or equivalent.
 OR
 Ayurvedacharya of All India Ayurved Vidyapeeth.
 OR
Other eminent Ayurvedic Scholars of established repute
though not having any degree/ diploma, but fit for
teaching Ayurvedic subjects.
(b) Teaching experience in any institution for ten years,
five years and three years for the post of Professor,
Reader and Lecturer respectively.
(c) Knowledge of Sanskrit.
 Desirable:
(a) Post/graduate qualification in Ayurved from a
recognized institution/ university established by law.
(b) Original published papers/ books as the subject."
35. Thus, there would be no difficulty in coming to the
conclusion that for the post of Lecturer, there was always a
requirement for not only qualification of degree in Ayurveda but also
a minimum three years teaching experience in any Ayurvedic
Medical College. As a matter of fact, the issue that the qualification
laid down under Chapter XVI-B of the Statutes of the Bihar
University and the aforesaid CCIM norms were one and the same,
has already been answered by this Court in CWJC No. 4486 of 1992
wherein it was held as follows:
“…………. the petitioners have referred to Clause-18 of
the Brochure of the Central Council laying down, inter
alia, the qualifications for teaching staff in Ayurvedic
Degree Colleges. The relevant clause of the Brochure
runs as follows:
"18. Qualifications prescribed for Teaching Staff:
I. ESSENTIAL:
(a) Degree diploma in Ayurved from a University

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 66
established by law or a statutory Board/ Faculty/
Examining Body of Indian Medicine or equivalent.
 OR
 Ayurvedacharya of All India Ayurved Vidyapeeth.
 OR
Other eminent Ayurvedic Scholars of established repute
though not having any degree/ diploma, but fit for
teaching Ayurvedic subjects.
(b) Teaching experience in any institution for ten years,
five years and three years for the post of Professor,
Reader and Lecturer respectively.
(c) Knowledge of Sanskrit.
II. DESIRABLE:
(a) Post/graduate qualification in Ayurved from a
recognized institution/ university established by law.
(b) Original published papers/ books as the subject."
 It is pointed out that same academic qualifications
have been prescribed for the post of Professor, Reader
and Lecturer, the only distinguishing feature being that
for appointment to the said posts the person has to have
teaching experience of ten years, five years and three
years, respectively. My attention was also drawn to the
letter of the Central Council dated 10.09.1992 informing
Dr. Vijay Shankar Pandey, petitioner no. 1 in CWJC No.
4486 of 1992 stating that as per the notification
published in the Gazette of India for July 1989 there is
no teaching post of Demonstrator. Prior to 1989 there
was provision for the post of Demonstrator and as per
the curriculum prescribed there was no requirement of
any teaching experience. Reference has also been made
to Chapter XVI B of the Statute of the Bihar University
laying down inter lia qualifications for di9fferent posts
of teachers and officers in the faculty of Ayurved. The
qualifications for the post of Demonstrator have been
prescribed as degree in Ayurved from a recognized
University or Board/Council established by a State or
Central Government and recognized by the University
except that in Science subjects (i.e. Physics, Chemistry
and ;Biology), the minimum qualification shall be
second Class Master Degree of a recognized University
in the subject concerned.”
36. Once, this hurdle was crossed and it was as clear as
day light that none of the petitioners claiming absorption on the post
of Lecturer had the prescribed qualification laid down either by the
Bihar University under Chapter XVI-B of the Statute or under the
CCIM norms and thus, the reasoning given by the Review
Committee in its report to be valid for refusing absorption to such of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 67
the petitioners claiming absorption on the post of Lecturer, an
alternative argument was developed that since it was a private
affiliated college in the year 1972 to 1986, the requirement of
Statutes if not followed, would not vitiate the appointment of the
petitioners claiming absorption on the post of Lecturers inasmuch as
the Governing Body at the relevant point of time had approved the
appointment of the petitioners which was initially made by the
Secretary to the Governing Body. This led to the issue of power of
the Governing Body and its Secretary which again was prescribed
under Chapter XIII of the Statutes and incorporated in the Calendar
of Bihar University. Relevant portion whereof reads as follows:
Functions of Governing Body-“Clause 5. Except in the
case of colleges owned and maintained by the
Government, the Governing Body of an admitted college
shall:
(a) Pass with or without modification the annual
accounts and annual budget estimates prepared by the
Principal.
(b)subject to the provisions of the Act make
appointment against sanctioned posts of teachers.
(c) make appointment of non-teaching staff, other than
inferior staff, within the budget provisions against
sanctioned posts.
(d) on the application being recommended or forwarded
by the Principal, grant all kinds of leave except casual
and quarantine leave, which will be granted by the
Principal or confirm other kinds of leave granted by the
Secretary.
(e) constitute selection committee/committees for
appointment of various categories of staff excluding
inferior staff.
(f) be responsible for compliance with the orders and
directions of all the lawful authorities.
(g) be responsible for the enforcement and observance of
the laws and directions of the University.
(h) consider, examine and sanction all proposals or
schemes for improvement and expansion of the college.
(i) exercise general supervision over all financial and
administrative matters relating to the college.
President- 6. (a) The President shall preside at the meetings

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 68
of the Governing Body and either record the proceedings
of the meeting of the Governing Body himself or get
them recorded and sign the same, provided that when he
is absent, the members of the Governing Body may elect
a Chairman for the meeting from amongst themselves.
(b) The President may, when he considers it necessary in the
interest of the college, direct the Secretary to convene a
meeting of the Governing Body and the Secretary shall
comply with the same.
Secretary 7. The Secretary of the Governing Body of
every admitted college shall be agent and executive
officer of the Governing Body and shall:
(a) convene meetings of the Governing Body, prepare
agenda thereof including items suggested by the
Principal and the President.
 Provided that if the Secretary of the Governing
Body dopes not convene a meeting of the Governing
Body according to the provisions made in Article 3(viii)
of this chapter of the Statutes, the Vice Chancellor may
direct that the meeting of the Governing Body may be
held within a fortnight from the date of receipt of
communication from the University to this effect and if
the Secretary fails to comply with the direction, the
meeting shall be convened by the University
representative on the said Governing Body on such date
as may be fixed by him in consultation with the
President of the said Governing Body and he shall
prepare agenda for that meeting in consultation with the
Principal and the President.
(b) carry on correspondence with the University and the
Government and others with regard to the decisions of
the Governing Body.
(c) on application being forwarded or recommended by
the Principal, grant all other kinds of leave to the staff
according to the laws of the University in anticipation of
the sanction of the Governing Body, where sanction is
needed.
(d) be responsible for taking such action on behalf of the
Governing Body as he may be authorized to take or
which may become necessary in view of any decision of
the Governing Body.
(e) grant to the Principal, casual or quarantine leave or
permission to leave the station.
(f) be responsible for the compliance of the orders and
directions of all lawful authorities.
(g) be responsible for the enforcement and observance of
all the laws and directions of the University.
 Provided that, if for any reason, the post of Secretary
of the Governing Body falls vacant, the University
representative on the Governing Body shall function as
Secretary till election of the new Secretary.
Principal 8. In every admitted college, the Principal shall
be the head of the college and shall:-
(a) assign duties (teaching, administrative and extra
curricular) to the staff of the college and supervise the
performance of their duties;
(b) be responsible for the maintenance of discipline in all

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 69
its aspects in his college and, for that purpose, shall have
power to take such action as may be necessary, subject
to the laws of the University.
(c) have powers to make appointment of inferior staff
against sanctioned posts within the budget provisions;
(d) grant casual; or quarantine leave, or permission to
leave the station to the members of staff of the college;
(e) be the custodian of all records of the college;
(f) shall carry on all correspondence, except those
mentioned in Clause (b) of Article 7 of this chapter.
(g)subject to the control and direction of the Syndicate
and the Academic Council, have powers to make rules,
not inconsistent with the laws of the University, relating
to departments, clubs, societies, staff and students’ union
of his college; and a copy thereof shall be submitted to
the Governing Body and the University for information.
(h) be responsible for the enforcement and observance of
the laws and directions of the University.
(i) be responsible for compliance of the orders and
directions of all the lawful authorities.”
37. Since, appointment of all the petitioners invariably
were made by the Secretary to the College who had been vested
with no such powers under the statutes inasmuch as the power of
appointment of teaching and non-teaching employees except
inferior services were vested exclusively in Governing Body. This
Court thus had felt the necessity to look into the proceedings of the
Governing Body. It became more essential because the Second
Screening Committee had given its reasoning for deciding the case
of absorption of the petitioners and others on the basis of
proceedings of the Governing Body. Thus, once again the hunt has
made for the Proceeding Book of the Governing Body and this
Court was informed that in a criminal case lodged against the
principal of the College, the CBI had taken away the entire
Proceeding Book of the Governing Body and as such the same

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 70
could not be produced. This Court had accordingly passed a
detailed order on 30.09.2015 which reads as follows:
”After the learned counsel for the petitioners have
concluded their submissions and Mr. D.K.Sinha, learned
AAGII, has also started making his submissions this
Court would find that in the common counter affidavit,
which have been filed on behalf of State Government,
there is no supporting document to substantiate the
findings recorded by the Review Committee in its report
dated 23.7.2007. In absence of them, it would be very
difficult for this Court to finally decide the issue as to
whether refusal of absorption of the petitioners is based
on valid consideration as per direction given in the
earlier judgment in the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey &
ors. v. the State of Bihar & ors., reported in 2005(2)
PLJR 399.
2. Apart from the above this Court would also like to
know in the cases of non-teaching employees from the
State Government as to what was the relevant criteria
and qualification laid down either by the Bihar
University to which the College was affiliated or by the
State Government in respect of non-teaching posts under
consideration in these writ petitions even for the
Government Ayurvedic Medical College and Hospital,
Patna.
 3. As with regard to availability of the proceedings of
the meetings of the Governing Body of the College this
Court would direct the Principal Secretary of the Health
Department to firstly find out as to whether those
proceedings are available in the Government records as
copiously referred to in the report of review committee
dated 23.7.2007 or still with the Central Bureau of
Investigation in connection with a criminal case lodged
against Mr. Radha Mohan Rai, the then Principal of the
College. If the records of the Governing Body are with
the C.B.I., efforts should be made at the level of Chief
Secretary and Principal Secretary of Education
Department to obtain them from C.B.I. inasmuch as its
perusal would be very helpful in final adjudication of
these cases.
 4. For the purposes of filing a compact affidavit on
behalf of the State Government in the aforesaid terms
specially on the issues indicated above, the records of
Health Department handed over to this Court is directed
to be returned with to Mr. D.K.Sinha but such records
once again should be made available to this Court on the
next day of hearing.
 5. Let it be noted that the Bihar University is a party in
one of the writ petitions, C.W.J.C.No. 12064/2008, and a
direction was given under the order dated 4.2.2010 to the
University to file counter affidavit. This order having
been not complied the Vice Chancellor and the Registrar
of the Bihar University are clearly in contempt.
6. This Court would, accordingly, direct both the Vice

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 71
Chancellor and the Registrar of Bihar University to
ensure that the qualification and the criteria for
appointment of non-teaching post in the Ayurvedic
Medical Colleges, if any laid down, must be brought on
record or alternatively the criteria which has been
followed by the University in making similar
appointment on Class III post should be also placed on
record. While doing so, the University shall also clarify
in its affidavit as to whether the Statute relating to
condition of service of non-teaching employees of the
affiliated College assented to by the Vice Chancellor on
18.8.1972, as contained in Chapter XVI-A of the Bihar
University Calendar Vol.II, Law of University (Statutes)
was made applicable for the College in question also
affiliated to Bihar University since 1972 or whether any
other Statutes was in force laying down the qualification,
terms and conditions of the appointment and the service
condition of non-teaching employees of the College in
question. 7. Additionally, the Registrar of the University
is also directed to produce the original records of the
College from the stage of its affiliation in the year 1972
till it was taken over by the State Government in 1986.
38. It was with great difficulty that this Court could trace
the records of the Governing Body through the communication of the
Superintendent of Police, CBI, Delhi, who had taken a stand that all
such records had been handed over to the Vigilance Department way
back on 16.03.2002.
39. On 05.11.2015, learned senior counsel appearing on
behalf of the Vigilance Department had produced a photocopy of the
proceedings of the Governing Body of the college which was taken
on record on 05.11.2015 and on 06.11.2015. The photocopy of the
charge sheet in the criminal case filed against the Principal of the
college namely Radha Mohan Rai and one another person Sheo
Charan Ram had been produced. From perusal of the chargesheet of
the Vigilance P.S.Case No. 18 of 2004, it appears that F.I.R. was

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 72
lodged against Radha Mohan Rai, the then Principal of the College
for offence under Sections 420, 465, 467, 468, 471, 477A of the
Indian Penal Code and Section 13(2) read with Section 13(1(c) (d) of
the Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988 on 11.11.2004 and a
chargesheet was submitted on 24.11.2005. From perusal of the charge
sheet, it also clear that the criminal case against Dr. Radha Mohan
Rai, the Acting Principal was lodged in view of direction given by
this Court in CWJC No. 4821 of 1999 and CWJC No. 10201 of 1999.
Thus, from reading of the charge sheet, it becomes clear that both
Siddh Nath Tiwari, the then Secretary and Acting Principal Radha
Mohan Rai had indulged into making a large number of illegal
appointments.
40.What really is however important for this Court that it
could discover on the basis of production of the photocopy of the
Proceeding Book of the Governing Body of the college for the period
17.06.1979 to 14.09.1985 is that the modus operandi of the
appointment was that the Secretary of the College was making
indiscriminate appointment and subsequently in some of them, post
facto approval of the Governing Body was being obtained. In this
regard, this Court has carefully perused the proceedings of the
meeting of the Governing Body dated 24.06.1979 wherein under
Resolution No. 2, appointment of (1) Brahmeshwar Nath Tiwari on

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 73
the post of Lecturer in the Sharir Kriya Vigyan Vibhag, (2) Kamta
Prasad Mishra as Lecturer in Shalya Vibhag, (3) Mod Nath Mishra as
Lecturer in Charak General Vibhag and appointment of (4) Vijay
Kuamr Tiwari on the post of Demonstrator in Vigyan Vibhag,
(5)Paras Nath Chaubey without any post of any department , (6)
Suryavansh Chaubey on the post of peon and (7) Sriniwas Upadhyay
as Clerk, (8) Nagendra Pandey as Clerk, (9) Bihari Singh as
Adeshpal, (10) Sheshnath Chaubey as Night Guard, (11) Dilip Kumar
as Night Guard, (12) Chaturbhuj Singh as Adeshpal and (13) Md.
Harun Khan on the post of Peon, was said to have been given post
facto approval.
41.Similarly, in the meeting held on 23.12.1979 on the
application filed by Mod Nath Mishra, Vijay Kumar Tiwari, Kamta
Prasad Mishra and Brahmeshwar Nath Tiwari their services were
temporarily approved with a decision that the Secretary and the
Principal should report about their performance of duty whereafter
their permanent appointment may be considered. It is very
significant to be noted here is that in the same meeting, the
Governing Body had postponed the approval and/or confirmation of
the appointment made by the Secretary on the recommendation of the
Principal and had directed for submission of a report with regard to
each of such adhoc appointed employee by the Secretary of the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 74
College.
42. In the meeting of the Governing Body held on
15.01.1980, there is a visible interpolation. Whereafter Item No. 5 of
the agenda relating to any other matter 5(a) (b)(c)(d) have been added
for the purposes of giving approval to the appointment of Anil Kumar
Mishra, who was earlier said to be appointed by the Secretary on the
post of Librarian of the College. As a matter of fact, the post of
Librarian was sought to be created in the same meeting and the
appointment of Anil Kumar Mishra, only a graduate, made by the
Secretary, was sought to be regularized. What is more reveling from
the reading of the Resolution of the Governing Body dated
15.01.1980 is that the Governing Body had postponed consideration
of other appointment and had authorized the Secretary of the College
to make purely temporary and adhoc appointment, which could be
later on placed for approval of the Governing Body.
43.The proceeding of the meeting of the Governing Body
dated 25.05.1980 goes to show that Vijay Kumar Tiwari, holding
simple qualification of Bachelor in Science and appointed as a
Demonstrator in the Science Department, was made Head of
Department of Pharmacy and in place of Vijay Kumar Tiwari, one
Paras Nath Chaubey was posted as Demonstrator, who were asked to
take over charge of their respective post on 1st July, 1980.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 75
44. In the next proceeding of the Governing Body is in the
date of 16.8.1980 wherein under Resolution No. 5 (Ga), (1) Ramji
Tiwari, (2) Tarkeshwar Nath Ojha, (3) Motishwari Devi, (4)
Raghubansh Pandey, (5) Uma Yadav, (6) Gupteshwr Yadav and (7)
Ramdayal Yadav were sought to be appointed as a Class-IV
employee for the safety, security and functioning of the college.
45.In the next proceeding of the Governing Body held on
25.12.1980, there is no indication of any appointment or approval of
appointment made by the Secretary. In the next meeting held on
22.02.1981, services of Vidyapati Chaturbedi and Rajendra Prasad
Dubey were sought to be confirmed and permanent on the post of
Principal and Vice Principal. Whereas Dr. A.N.Pandey, Dharmdeo
Pathak and Yamuna Ojha were made permanent teachers.
46. In the next proceeding of the Governing Body held on
23.04.1981 vide Resolution No. 4, Brahmeshwar Nath Tiwari, Kamta
Prasad Mishra, Modnath Mishra, Vijay Kumar Tiwari, Dr. Jag Narain
Pandey, Dr. Ajiteshwar Narain, Dr. Harishchandra Srivastava in
modification to the earlier Resolution No. 3 and 4 dated 23.12.1979,
were sought to be appointed and confirmed afresh on their respective
post with effect from 23.04.1981. In the meeting, Paras Nath
Choubey was sought to be appointed on the post of Demonstrator
whereas on Shiv Shankar Pandey on the post of Vaidya and

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 76
Suryavansh Chaubey as Assistant Vaidya. One Anil Kumar Mishra
was appointed on the post of Librarian and Kanhaiya Chaubey on the
post of Head Clerk/Accountant whereas Satya Narain Chaubey,
Srinivas Upadhyay, Nagendra Pandey and Viplavi Kumar Upadhyay
were sought to be appointed on the post of Clerk. Rameshwar Ram
and Raghupati Yadav on the post of Night Guard and Peon
respectively. Vide Resolution No. 5 of the same meeting, Shashi
Bhushan Tiwari and Tarkeshwar Ojha were sought to be appointed as
Laboratory Assistant whereas Bhagwan Tiwari, Ram Nath Singh,
Radhe Shyam Tiwari, Raghuvansh Pandey, Kamla Kant Mishra,
Krishna Kant Mishra on the post of Clerk. Likewise, Uma Shankar
Yadav was appointed on the post of Peon and Gupteshwar Yadav,
Ram Dayal Yadav and Lal Bachan Paswan were sought to be
appointed on the post of Night Guard.
47. What is however very important to note at this stage is
that the Governing Body in the meeting held on 23.04.1981 had
resolved that the newly created post shall be filled up not only by way
of publication of advertisement in the newspaper but also with the
approval of the Bihar University.
48.In the meeting held on 15.05.1981 by the Governing
Body, there is no decision with regard to appointment of any teaching
or non-teaching employee but then a clear resolution was passed that

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 77
appointment in the college would be made as per the provisions made
in the norms of CCIM and as per the requirement laid down in the
Statutes. In the proceeding of the Governing Body held on
30.08.1981 also, there is no decision taken with regard to
appointment of any teaching/non-teaching employee.

on the basis of advertisement made in Aryabrat newspaper on

23. Dr. Deendra Nath Pandey Demonstrator Shalya Vibhag
54.Under Resolution No. 5 in the same meeting, promotion
of 15 demonstrators on the post of Lecturer was made with effect
from 01.07.1982.
1. Sri Purushottam Dwivedi
2. Sri Shashi Bhushan Jha
3. Sri Chaturbedi Kamendra Deo
Narain

55.In the meeting held on 29.03.1983, Dharamdeo Pathak
was promoted on the post of Reader w.e.f. 29.03.1983 in the subject
of Udwiz Pratyang Vigyan. It has to be noted that such promotion
was given on an application of Dharamdeo Pathak who was a
Member of the Governing Body in capacity of teachers’
representative. Vide Resolution No. 4, Vinod Kumar Chaubey was
appointed as a Ward Body on adhoc basis. Similarly, vide Resolution

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 82
No. 5, promotion of Dr. J.N.Pandey was made on the post of Reader
in Swasth Vrit Vibhag. Under Resolution No. 6, promotion of 14
teachers in difference to the decision dated 15.06.1982 was confirmed
and for rest, their earlier promotion was cancelled.
1. Sri Purushottam Dwivedi

Narain

56.A bare comparison of Resolution No. 5 of the meeting
dated 15.06.1982 would go to show that name of two persons namely
Satish Chandra Pandey and Radha Mohan Rai were sought to be
introduced by way of interpolation and likewise in the proceeding no.
6, the name of Ramesha Nand Jha was sought to be added by way of
interpolation.
57.In the meeting held on 11.09.1983, while Ramesha Nand
Jha was still shown to be Demonstrator in the Department of
Swasthya Vrit Vibhag and sought to be transferred to Prashuti Tantra
Vibhag, Dr. Sarada Pandey working as nursing sister in the
Department of Antaran Vibhag was sought to be promoted on the
post of Demonstrator w.e.f. 11.09.1983.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 83
58.In the same meeting, the promotion given to Dr.
J.N.Pandey on the post of Reader was cancelled due to lack of his
qualification. Resolution No. 9, the appointment of Dr. A.N. Pandey,
HOD of Anatomy was made permanent giving him the seniority
w.e.f. 13.3.1978. Vide Resolution no. 10, Sri Ram Nivas Upadhyay,
holding qualification of Bachelor in Science and working as a Head
Clerk in the Antrang Vibhag was sought to be promoted on the post
of Demonstrator in Maulik Siddhant Vibhag and one Bhagwan
Tiwari was made the Head Clerk. Ram Nivas Upadhyay was given
the teaching assignment of Modern Physics. Likewise, Paras Nath
Chaubey, Demonstrator in Maulik Siddhant Vibhag was shifted to
Vikriti Vigyan (Pathology Department). In the same meeting, Shanti
Ojha, Nursing Sister was promoted on the post of typist/Nursing
Sister.
59.In the proceeding of the Governing Body dated
13.10.1983, services of 16 persons including Sri Nivas Upadhyay on
the post of Clerk, Rama Shankar Chaubey on the post of
Demonstrator, Dinanath Upadhyay on the post of Demonstrator, Om
Prakash Ojha on Demonstrator as well as Avinash Kumar Siknha,
Suman Jee Pandey, Kamlesh Kumar Tiwari, Rajendra Tiwari, Nand
Bihari Ojha, Hiralal Upadhyay, Chitraketur Pandey, Kamlesh Pathak
and Smt. Krishna Murari, Bhrigunath Prasad, Ramjee Mishra and

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 84
Johan Pandey were terminated on account of their power
performance.
60.Vide Resolution No. 4, Dr. Yamuna OJha, Ajiteshwar
Naraindhar Dubey, Brahmeshwar Nath Tiwari and Kamta Prasad
Mishra were sought to be promoted on the post of Reader whereas
one Pradeep Kumar Jaiswal was promoted from post of Demonstrator
to the Lecturer.
61.The aforesaid proceeding dated 13.10.1983 also goes to
show that after expiry of the temporary affiliation of the college in
the year 1981, no fresh admission of the students was taken. Under
Resolution No. 9, promotion was given to Dr. Vishwesh Kumar on
the post of Reader in the department of Eye & ENT.
62.In the meeting of Governing Body dated 25.03.1984,
though no appointment was made but the decision was taken to create
teaching and non-teaching posts and what is very revealing is that
column of number of vacancies was unfilled and with patent
overwriting as also by interpolating the signature of the president of
the Governing Body who was non-else but the Cabinet Minster
namely Jag Narain Trivedi. It is on account of this, that the cut off
date in the judgment of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) has been
25.03.1984.
63. This Court, in view of aforesaid startling revelation from

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 85
the proceeding of the Governing Body, had accordingly directed the
University to produce the records relating to the college. From the
file of the University with regard to College in question, it transpires
that an inquiry in the affairs of the college was directed to be
conducted by Dr. D.N.Mallik on 12.10.1979 and when he had visited
the college, the Principal of the college had given in writing that only
three teachers namely Dr. Ayodhya Prasad, Dr. B.B.Pandey and Dr.
J.N.Dubey, having the qualification, were working in the college. In
fact, from the report of Dr. D.N.Mallik, it becomes clear that 05
teachers namely Dr. Rajendra Prasad Dubey, Dr. A.N.Pandey, Dr.
D.D. Pathak, Dr. Yamuna Prasad Ojha and Sri S.S.Pandey were the
rival teachers. The report of Dr. Mallik has only this much of
relevance for the purpose of this case that as on the date of inspection
by the officials of the Bihar University, there were only 9 to 10
teachers in the college. It must be kept in mind that the Statutes
framed by the Hon’ble Chancellor had soon come into force w.e.f.
07.08.1979 and therefore, so far the teaching employees are
concerned, there would not be much difficulty to understand that for
the rest of the appointment, the University Statutes laying down the
qualification for the teaching post had to be followed.
64. What is more important is existence of a letter of the
Principal of the college dated 10.10.1982 addressed to the officers on

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 86
Special Duty of the Bihar Government that the Government through
the Director, Indigenous Medicine vide letter no. 778 dated
01.02.1982 had informed the college that the Government was
contemplating to take over the college and as such the details of the
employees and other necessary details should be sent to the
Government. Thus, from 01.10.1982, each and every appointment
made was with a view to impending take over the services by the
State Government. From the University records, it is also apparent
that the college was never given affiliation for its temporary
affiliation and had expired in year 1981 and as such there was no
admission in the college from 1982 therein onwards a fact which also
stands admitted that only 06 batches of the college from 1977 had
appeared in the university examination whereafter the students were
not allowed to be either admitted or appeared for the university
examination for any academic session after the year 1982. It is in this
backdrop that the massive recruitment shown in the records of the
Governing Body will have to be strictly examined keeping in view
the minimum skeleton staff which was required from 1981 onwards,
when the college had no affiliation and was also not in a position to
impart teaching to the fresh students on accounts of ban on admission
under the orders of the State Government. As a matter of fact, the
University file also goes to reveal that there were two colleges under

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 87
the same name at Buxar which were claiming to be the college sought
to be affiliated with the approval of the State Government one at the
Station Road, Buxar and other at Ahrauli, Buxar.
65. Finally, this Court had to also discover as with regard to
qualification in respect of non-teaching post and particularly the post
of Laboratory Technician in view of observation and direction given
in the judgment of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (Supra). It was in this
backdrop, the cases were heard an order was passed for producing the
qualification, relevant portion whereof reads as follows:
when these cases have been heard today it is found that
the assistance of Bihar University is absolutely necessary,
inasmuch as there is a lot of controversy as with regard to
the qualification in respect of non-teaching post and
particularly for the post of Laboratory Technician.
 In that view of the matter, this Court would direct the
Registrar of Bihar University to appear alongwith the
Sectional Head of Ayurvedic College to explain to this
Court the qualification for non-teaching post in the affiliated
Ayurvedic Colleges of Bihar University as it was in the year
1976 onwards. The Registrar of the University shall also
produce the relevant Statutes with regard to the appointment
on teaching/ non-teaching post of the affiliated Ayurvedic
Colleges or general Statutes which were made applicable in
case of non-teaching employees of Ayurvedic Colleges.
This Court would make it clear that if any of the counsel
wants to file written statements that would also be done by
the next Monday. Learned counsel for the State in addition
to their written statement, if any, may also file a compact
chart in respect of all the petitioners vis-à-vis persons who
have been absorbed in Buxar Ayurvedic College.
66. The University thereafter had not only produced the
relevant records but also made it clear that no specific qualification
was prescribed by the university for appointment on the Class-3 and
Class-4 post in any of its affiliated Ayurvedic College. To that extent,
these facts were also recorded in the order dated 10.11.2015, relevant

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 88
portion whereof reads as follows:
 Pursuant to the earlier order of this Court, Mr. Mrigank
Mauli, learned counsel for the Bihar University, having taken
instruction, in presence of the Registrar of the University, says
that no qualification has been prescribed by the University for
appointment on Class-3 and Class-4 post in all its affiliated
Ayurvedic Colleges. In this regard, he has also explained that a
communication sent by the University to the State Government
on 21.1.2011 as with regard to the qualification for the post of
Laboratory Technician in the Ayurvedic College of being only
Intermediate in Science and its re-affirmance in the letter dated
11.10.2014 as contained in Annexure 22 & 23 Series to CWJC
No. 12064 of 2008 was based on a letter of the Government
bearing no. 9690 dated 17.8.1979 which actually was for the
post of Laboratory In-charge. Thus, Mr. Mrigank Mauli,
learned counsel for the University, on being instructed by the
University, submits that even for the post of Laboratory
Technician, no qualification was laid down by the University
and whatever communication was made in the aforesaid two
letters by the University to the State Government was based on
an erroneous impression with regard to post of Laboratory In-charge and Laboratory Technician, being the same.
 Let it be kept in mind that this Court had to find out the
qualification of Laboratory Technician from the University
because as per the earlier judgment, if the University has
prescribed any qualification for the post of Laboratory
Technician, that had to be made basis of absorption in absence
whereof the prescribed qualification for the post of Laboratory
Technical to be taken into consideration was to be as
prescribed by the State Government.
 Thus, having regard to the aforesaid categorical stand
taken by the University, this Court would direct the Registrar
of the University, present in Court, to swear and file an
affidavit to this extent in course of the day and serve a copy
thereof to Mr. Prabhakar Dwivedi, learned counsel in CWJC
No. 12064 of 2008 and Mr. Awadhesh Kumar Mishra, learned
counsel appearing in CWJC No. 15019 of 2008.
 Having regard to the aforesaid stand taken by Mr.
Mrigank Mauli on behalf of the Bihar University, this Court
would direct Mr. D.K. Sinha, learned AAG-2 to produce the
government decision with regard to the qualification of
Laboratory Technician being not only Intermediate in Science
but also having necessary two years training known as Medical
Laboratory Technician (MLT). He assures this Court that the
same will be brought on record on the date of reopening that is
on 23.11.2015.
67. Thereafter, the affidavit having been filed and the
learned A.A.G. produced the qualification for the post of Laboratory
Technician in the Government Medical College, this Court had

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 89
proceeded to hear the parties and ultimately in the light of these
submissions as well as the materials brought on record and thus
collected with the endeavours of the Court, these writ petitions are
being disposed of.
68. Thus, on the basis of the aforesaid documents, this
Court could get from the original sources, it became very clear that
there was a college established in the year 1972 for which affiliation
was granted by the Government in year 1975 and the University had
allowed the privilege of affiliated college only from the year 1977
which continued only up to 1981. From 1981, there was a dispute in
the college and on account of enactment made by the Bihar
Government with regard to ban on the Ayurvedic Colleges, the
University did not extend its privilege of affiliation nor it allowed
admission in the college after the year 1981. In this background, if
unabated the spate of appointment is taken into consideration has been
disclosed from the proceedings of the Governing Body it becomes
more clear that on account of the college having a sitting cabinet
minister namely Dr. Jag Narain Trivedi as the President of the
Governing Body, who was instrumental in getting the college taken
over by the State Government, things were being managed in the
records of the Governing Body to appoint as many employees as
possible. It is really strange that a college running in the rented

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 90
premises as was found by Dr. D.N.Mallik in his inspection report in
the month of October, 1979 could accommodate more than 500
employees as they claimed initially before this Court for the
appointments made up to 01.06.1986. In fact, it was only after the
judgment in the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) that sizeable
number got axed as there appointment was made after 25.03.1984
when they were marked and clear interpolation in the proceeding of
the meeting of the Governing Body.
69. As is often said, those who live by sword also must
perish by sword. In these cases, the petitioners are claiming that for
the Governing Body, there was no restriction for making appointment
and also follow the procedure of advertisement published in
newspaper but then there are clear evidence to show that either in the
year 1978 or even in the year 1981, the Governing Body had followed
the procedure of appointment by only making advertisement in the
newspaper. IN this regard, this Court from the pleadings of CWJC
No. 8089 of 2008, has found that his claims his appointment on the
basis of advertisement which was published in the newspaper “Indian
Nation”, relevant portion whereof reads as follows:
"THE INDIAN NATION, THURSDAY, MARCH, 9TH,
1978
SITUATIONS VACANT
WANTED
Principal and Lecturers in Shri Dhanwantri Ayurvedic
Mahavidyalaya, Buxar in Anatomy, Nidan Ras
Pharmacy Materia M. Hygiene Science Sanskrit
Grammar, Botany, Physicas, History of Ay. Vaidyakiya

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 91
subhasitani qualifications and emoluments as per Bihar
University Rules. Candidate with experience preferred.
Apply to the Secretary alongwith testimonials on or
before 10 A.M. on 12.3.1978 and come for interview on
12 A.M. on 12.3.1978 in the college premises. No T.A.
allowed.
 Dr. S.N. Tiwari
 Secretary
EIC 506-1-1"
70. Similarly, from the proceeding book of the Governing
Body and in particular from the reading of the decision taken in the
meeting of 23.04.1981, it becomes very clear that the Governing Body
had resolved that the appointments was not only be made after
publication of advertisement in the newspaper but also with the
approval of the Bihar University. The relevant portion of the said
resolution reads as follows:

71. Thus, when there was a complete cessation of work in
the college on account of its affiliation being not extended to 1981
and there was a decision of the Governing Body of making
appointment after publication of vacancies in newspaper, a practice

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 92
which was also in vogue even earlier in the year 1978, as found in the
case of Dr. Harishankar Prasad Srivastava, this Court must hold that
Review Committee did not commit any error in following the
direction in the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) in paragraph no.
26, already qaoted above, as with regard to violating the norms set up
by the Governing Body and that the publication of vacancies in the
newspaper was not followed by the Secretary of the college for
making fraudulent appointment.
72. As a matter of fact, so far the teaching post are
concerned, the absorption of those posts have to be strictly abide by
the provisions made in the statutes and the CCIM norms which had
contemplated temporary appointment by the Governing Body of a
teaching staff for a fixed period of six months whereafter the
appointment was to be made only with concurrence of university
service Commission/Bihar College Service Commission. Thus, none
of the petitioners claiming direct appointment on Lecturers unlike the
Demonstrators for whom there was no requirement of either the post
being filled up with the concurrence of Bihar College Service
Commission or the requirement of teaching experience of three years,
their appointment by the Secretary, even without advertisement and
selection process without fulfilling the minimum eligibility condition
of qualification will actually amount to fraud. The State Government

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 93
definitely had a power to discover these aspects at the time of taking a
decision for absorption through the Screening Committee and Review
Committee and if that has been done while rejecting the cases of
absorption of the petitioners claiming absorption on the posts of
Lecturer/Reader that cannot be faulted either on facts or in law.
73. This Court would find it also necessary to clarify that
whenever a statute itself lays down qualification by way of both
academic degree and post qualification experience, the same cannot
be equated as a desirable or optional qualification. This aspect of the
matter has been well settled in a series of cases by the Supreme Court
in the case of Bhagirathdan vs. State of Rajasthan & Ors. Reported as
(1992) 4 SCC 64, wherein it was held as follows:
“10. It was contended before us by learned counsel for
the appellants that it was clear from the said Entry 6 of
the Schedule that there was no necessity of five years’
experience of drilling or of maintenance of drilling
machines in the case of degree-holders such as the
appellants. The experience of five years that was
prescribed was only in respect of diploma-holders. As
we read the said Entry 6 of the Schedule, the
qualification that an Assistant Drilling Engineer or an
Assistant Mechanical Engineer had to possess for being
promoted to the post of Deputy Drilling Engineer was
(a) a diploma in Mechanical Engineer and (b) five years’
experience of drilling or of maintenance of drilling
machines. The qualification prescribed was thus, two-fold: educational and practical experience. A better
educational qualification does not obviate the need for
the prescribed practical experience.
74. The same view was again reiterated by the Apex Court
in the case of Indian Airlines Limited & Ors. Vs. S.Gopalakrishnan
reported as (2001) 2 SCC 362, wherein it was held as follows:-
 “5. When in addition to qualification, experience is

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 94
prescribed, it would only mean acquiring experience
after obtaining the necessary qualification and not before
obtaining such qualification. In the case of the
respondent, he obtained the ITI certificate in the year
1994 and, therefore, did not possess five years of
experience as required under the relevant rule. If his
qualification as a diploma-holder in Mechanical
Engineering is taken note of, he has not completed three
years of experience as he got the same in April 1996 and
on the relevant date he did not possess such
qualification. Indeed in prescribing qualification and
experience it is also made clear in the general
information instruction at Item 6 that “experience will be
computed after the date of acquiring the necessary
qualifications”. Therefore, when this requirement was
made very clear that he should have experience only
after acquiring the qualification, the view taken by the
High Court to the contrary either by the learned single
Judge or the Division Bench does not stand to reason.”
75. The Apex Court again in the case of Ashok Kumar
Sonkar Vs. Union of India and Ors. Reporte din (2007) 4 SCC 54 had
held that prescribed qualification and experience cannot be waived or
completed after appointment.
76. Thus, the appointments of those petititioners claiming
direct appointment on the post of Lecturer without having the
requisinte post qualification of three years teaching experience in
Ayurvedic College, will actually amount to its being ab initio void as
has been also explined in the judgment of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey
(supra) specially when all these appointments were by the Secretary
of the college despite the practice of the post being advertised in the
newspaper either in the year 1978 or even in the year 1981.
77. The strict test of fulfilling the qualification by the
petitioners claiming appointment and absorption on the post of
Lecturers has been in fact well settled by the Apex Court in the case

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 95
of take over of Lalit Narain Mishra Institute of Economic
Development etc. vs. State of Bihar & Ors. Reported as 1988 PLJR 45
SC wherein issue of take over of the aforesaid Institution had led to
also consideration of absorption of the services of the teaching and
non-teaching employees. In that regard, the Apex Court in the case of
L.N.M. Institute (supra) had after noticing almost same and in fact
identical provisions as in the case of Section 6(1)(2)(3) & (4) of the
present take over Act, had held as follows:
“39. Now we may deal with the case of Dr. Jagadanand
Jha, Registrar of the Institute. The first Ordinance,
namely Ordinance No. 15 of 1986 was promulgated by
the Government on April 19, 1986 and the services of
Dr. Jha were terminated by an order dated April 21,
1986 which is extracted below:-
“The Governor of Bihar in exercise of power under
section 6 and sub-se4ctions (2),(3) & (4) of Bihar Private
Educational Institutions (Take Over) Ordinance, 1986
and Bihar Ordinance No. 15 of 1986 and Education
Department Notification No. 99/C has considered the
report of the Committee and has come to the conclusion
that appointment and promotion of officers and workers
not done as per rules of the University, nor in
accordance with Government directions and
notifications and their stay in the Institute was not in the
interest of the said Institute. Therefore, the Services of
the following persons are dispensed with immediate
effect:
1. Dr. Jagannath Mishra-Chairman-cum-Director General
2. Sri Jagadanand Jha-Registrar
40. Paragraph 6 of the Ordinance, which is verbatim the
same as section 6 of the Act, reads as follows:-
“6. Determination of terms of services of the teaching
staff and other employees of the institution.-(1) As from
the date of the notified order, all the staff employed in
the institution shall cease to be the employee of the
institution:
Provided that they shall continue to serve the institution
on an ad hoc basis till a decision under sub-sections (3)
& (4) is taken by the State Government.
(2) The State Government will set up one or more
Committees of experts and knowledgeable persons
which will examine the bio-data of each member of the
teaching staff and ascertain whether appointment,

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 96
promotion or confirmation was made in accordance with
the University Regulation or Government
direction/circular and take into consideration all other
relevant materials, such as qualification, experience
research degree etc. and submit its report to the State
Government.
(3) The State Government on receipt of the report of the
Committee or Committees, as the case may be, will
decide in respect of each member of teaching staff on the
merits of each case, whether to absorb him in
Government service or whether to terminate his service
or to allow him to continue on an ad-hoc basis for a
fixed term or on contract and shall, whether necessary,
redetermine the rank, pay, allowance and other
conditions of service.
(4) The State Government shall similarly determine the
term of appointment and other conditions of service of
other categories of staff of the Institution on the basis of
facts to be ascertained either by a committee or by an
officer entrusted with the task and the provisions of sub-sections (2) & 3) apply mutatis mutandis to such case.”
41. Under paragraph 6(1), all the staff employed in the
institution shall cease to be the employees of the
Institute from the date of the notified order. Under the
proviso, however, such employees will continue on an
ad hoc basis till a decision under sub-paragraphs (3) &
(4) is taken by the Government. Under sub-paragraph (2)
of paragraph 6, the State Government is to set up one or
more committees of experts and knowledgeable persons
for the purpose of ascertaining whether or not
appointment, promotion or confirmation of each member
of the teaching staff was made in accordance with the
University Regulation or Government direction/circular
and to submit its report to the State Government. Sub-paragraph (3) of paragraph 6 enjoins the State
Government to decide in respect of each member of
teaching staff on the merits of each case whether to
absorb him in Government service or to terminate his
service or to allow him to continue on an adhoc basis for
a fixed term or on contract etc. sub-paragraphs (2) & (3)
relate to the members of teaching staff of the concerned
institution. Sub-paragraph (4) of paragraph 6 of the
Ordinance deals with the cases of other categories of
staff of the institution. It provides that the State
Government similarly determine the term of
appointment and other conditions of service of other
categories of staff of the institution. It is clear that there
is some distinction between sub-paragraph (3) and sub-paragraph (4). While under sub-paragraph (3) , the State
Government is to decide, among other things, whether
the service of a member of teaching staff will be
terminated or not, under sub-paragraph (4), the State
Government has not been enjoined to decide whether the
service of any member of a non-teaching staff will be
terminated or not. All that has been directed to be
decided by the State Government under sub-paragraph

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 97
(4) relates to the terms of appointment and other
conditions of service.
42. Admittedly, the petitioner Dr. Jagadanand Jha was
not a member of the teaching staff, but as noticed
already, he was the Registrar of the Institute, which
comes within the expression “other categories of Staff”
under sub-paragraph (4). It is true that under sub-paragraph (4) it has been provided that sub-paragraphs
(2) & (3) shall apply mutatis mutandis but, such
application will be limited to the term of appointment
and other conditions of service of a member of non-teaching staff of the institution . In other words, the State
Government may appoint a committee for the purpose of
considering the term of appointment and other
conditions of service of the members of the non-teaching
staff and the State Government has to decide
accordingly.
78. As a matter of fact in the lot of the petitioners and in
fact in the lot of 99 persons, who have been refused absorption on
various grounds as per the recommendation of the Review Committee
and its acceptance by the State Government, there are two separate
groups, one of teaching employees and the other of non-teaching
employees. Section 6 of the Act also clearly differentiates evaluation
of cases of teaching employees and non-teaching employees,
inasmuch as Section 6(2) and (3) of the Act puts more stringent
condition in case of absorption of teaching employees in comparison
to non-teaching employees, who are covered by the provisions of
Section 6(4) of the Act.
79. In the considered opinion of this Court in the case of
teaching employees the State Government for the purposes of
absorption had to ascertain whether their appointment, promotion or
confirmation was made in accordance with the Act/ Statutes or

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 98
Regulation of the University concerned and in keeping with the
guidelines laid down by the Central Council of Indigenous Medicine
(CCIM), whereas no similar requirement was laid down in respect of
non-teaching employees. This aspect of the matter with regard to
marked distinction in the manner of absorption of teaching and non-teaching employees in case of an exactly similar provision with regard
to takeover of L.N. Mithila Institute, Patna stands settled in the
judgment of the Apex Court in the cases of Lalit Narain Mishra
Institute of Economic Development (supra).
80. On the basis of the aforesaid discussions, this Court
would go to hold that under the provisions of the take over Act, while
there was a strict test to be applied for the teaching employees, not so
strict test was to be applied in the case of non-teaching employees. In
the case of non-teaching employees as was also held earlier in the
case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) , the basic qualification and the
manner of appointment was to be examined with a view to find out
any fraud in the appointment. Thus, in their cases, the reasons given
by the Review Committee will have to be scrutinized individually.
81. For the sake of repetition, it however, must be made
clear that none of the persons absorbed on the post of teaching
employees were initially appointed as Lecturer and in fact all of them
were appointed on the post of Demonstrator in whose cases

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 99
On
27.11.2015
requirement of three years teaching experience or to continue for a
period of six months only or the recommendation of Bihar College
Service Commission prescribed under the Statutes was not required.
82. On the other hand having regard to the statutory
provisions, this Court will have no difficulty in rejecting the cases of
absorption of the petitioners teaching employees claiming direct
appointment on the post of Lecturer as none of them had three years
teaching experience on the date of their appointment nor their
appointment was as prescribed both, in the Statutes and CCIM norms
nor their appointments were approved by the Syndicate of the
University nor even their continuation beyond a period of six months
was permissible without the recommendation of the College Service
Commission.
83. This would become now more clear in course individual
analysis of the facts of each and every petitioner claiming absorption
on the teaching post.
 To be continued -------------
Re.: C.W.J.C. No. 10417 of 2008
84. The prayer of the petitioner, namely, Dharamdeo
Pathak, in this writ application reads as follows:
"1. … for modification of the order contained in Memo no. 902
dated 17.10.2007 by which the Respondents has left out the
petitioner and thereby has not absorbed the petitioner on the post

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 100
of Teacher/Demonstrator in Rajkiya Dhanwantari Ayrvedic
College, Buxar, Ahiroulli (hereinafter referred to as "College" for
the sake of brevity) as the respondents have not considered the case
of these petitioners in terms of the judgment of this Hon'ble Court
passed in CWJC No. 9757 of 2003 and analogous cases which they
were duty bound to follow for absorption of the services of these
petitioner."
85. Learned counsel for the petitioner, in support of the
aforementioned prayed, has submitted that the petitioner should be
absorbed on the post of Reader on which post he was working on
09.12.1986 when the college was taken over in terms of Bihar Private
Medical (Indian System of Medicine) College (Taking Over) Act,
1985 (hereinafter referred to as "the Taking Over Act") vide a
Notification dated 09.12.1986.
86. It is the further case of the petitioner that on
13.08.1976, he was appointed as a Lecturer in Science (Botany,
Chemistry, Physics) on approved pay scale of the university and that
subsequently on 26.01.1981, he was also assigned the teaching work
of Lecturer in Dravyagun Department of the college apart from giving
him charge of the post of Garden Manager for maintaining the garden
of the college. The petitioner has also come out to say that on
25.02.1981, the Governing Body of the college had made his
appointment permanent on the post of Lecturer. According to the
petitioner, he was promoted on the post of Reader by an order of the
Secretary to the college dated 25.01.1984 with effect from 29.03.1983
in accordance with the resolution passed in the meeting of the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 101
Governing Body of the college on 29.03.1983.
87. In fact, the petitioner has also enclosed an order dated
05.07.1985 to show that the Governing Body, on 01.06.1985, had held
its meeting for promotion of the petitioner on the post of Garden
Manager -cum-Professor (Udyan Prabandhak -Sah-Pradhyapak). The
petitioner also has sought to support all these developments in his
career in the college right from the post of Lecturer in Science to the
post of Garden Manager-cum-Professor on the basis of the different
orders issued by the Secretary of the college.
88. The Government, however, on 29.8.2003 while
terminating his service, had rejected his claim of absorption of the
petitioner on the post of Professor or Reader or even Lecturer on the
ground that the petitioner did not possess the requisite qualification as
laid down under the Statutes. In this regard, the Screening Committee
had found the following infirmities in his appointment on the post of
lecturer in the college:-

89. Thereafter services of this petitioner was terminated by
a common order dated 29.8.2003 which has assailed by him and
others in a number of writ applications which were heard together and
were disposed of by a common judgment dated 11.3.2005 in the case
of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra). In view of the direction given by this
Court, the Review Committee dated 11.4.2007 had again considered
his case and had found him unfit for absorption. The Review
Committee in fact had considered in all the cases of 165 persons and
had recommended for absorption of 24 persons holding teaching post
and 42 persons holding non-teaching post and rest 99 of them were
found unfit for absorption and the petitioner is also among those 99
persons. Though the order dated 17.10.2007 does not give any
indication as with regard to the reasons for finding the petitioner unfit
but the report of the Review Committee brought on record indicates
that the petitioner in the list of 18 teaching employees was found unfit
on account of lack of prescribed qualification/experience as recorded
in the report of the second Screening Committee.
90. The Review Committee dated 11.4.2007 after
examining the earlier aforesaid report of Screening Committee, had

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 103
again found the petitioner unfit along with 17 other persons claiming
absorption on teaching posts by recording as follows:-
