91. Mr. Shivendra Kishore, learned senior counsel
appearing on behalf of the petitioner, while assailing the
aforementioned reasoning of the State Government, has initially tried
to wriggle out of the Statutes by staking a claim that such Statutes
though included in the Bihar University Calendar was meant only for
Kameshwar Singh Darbhanga Sanskrit University. He had also come
out to say that when the petitioner was appointed in the year 1976 on
the post of Lecturer in the college, neither Bihar State University Act,
1976 had not come into force nor even the Statues framed by the
Hon'ble Chancellor had been notified.
92. According to Mr. Shivendra Kishore, the petitioner,
though a graduate in the subject of Botany, was entitled to be
appointed as a Lecturer in the Department of Science in the year 1976
and, therefore, the subsequent promotion earned by the petitioner,
either on the post of Reader and/or Professor, could not have been

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 104
questioned specially for the purposes of absorption of his services in
the college. He has, in this regard, also submitted that assuming that
the petitioner does not have a prescribed qualification for holding the
post of a Lecturer/ Reader/ Professor, on account of his having no
qualification in the subject of Ayurved, his services could still be
absorbed on the post of Garden Manager.
93. In this regard, he has also submitted that in the given
facts and circumstances of the case, the petitioner would be satisfied
that if his absorption was made even on the post of Laboratory
Technician for which he was earlier screened and recommended in the
first screening committee report. In this regard, he has also sought
help from the provision of Section 6(3) of the Taking Over Act
which, according to him, empowers the Government to recommend
the rank/ pay of the employee to be absorbed in the college. Finally,
he has also raised a plea of discrimination by pointing out that when
other Ayurvedic Colleges were taken over, such strict and rigorous
norms for absorbing the services of the teachers was not followed.
94. Mr. D.K.Sinha, learned A.A.G. 2 appearing on behalf
of the State, on the other hand, has submitted that there would be no
question of absorption of the petitioner on any post in the college
inasmuch as the college was an affiliated college to the Bihar
University and, therefore, at all point of time, its appointment on any

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 105
teaching post had to be as per the norms prescribed by the Bihar
University. In this regard, he has relied on the provision of Bihar State
University Act, 1960 and Bihar State University Act, 1976 to contend
that even in affiliated college, appointment of a Lecturer could never
be made beyond the period of six months unless there was a
concurrence given by the University Service Commission, which was
later on replaced by the College Service Commission.
95. According to Mr. Sinha, the Government was fully
entitled to look into the service history as also the mode and manner
of appointment of the employee claiming absorption including that of
the petitioner and if, in course of the same, it got discovered that the
petitioner, being only a graduate in Botany, was claiming to be
Professor of the college, in question, the same would not only
jeopardize his case but also the case of all other persons, who were
claiming absorption on similar ground.
96. In the considered opinion of this Court, an Ayurvedic
College, having an hospital for imparting teaching up to degree in
Ayurveda, has to be governed by the Provision of Central Council of
Indian Medicine (hereinafter referred to as 'the CCIM') norms. Right
from the inception, the qualification, as prescribed by the CCIM
norms, for the teaching staff, was laid down as has been noticed by
this Court in the case of Vijay Shankar Pandey vs. State of Bihar and

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 106
Ors. (C.W.J.C. No. 4486 of 1992) disposed of on 12.05.1995. The
aforesaid case of Vijay Shankar Pandey (supra) was in relation to this
very college and the judgment enclosed with the writ application, vide
Annexure-11 in para-9, will go to show that Clause 18 of the
Brochure of the CCIM had laid down the qualification for teaching
staff of the Ayurvedic Degree College to the following effect:
"18. Qualifications prescribed for Teaching Staff:
I. ESSENTIAL :
a. Degree Diploma in Ayurved from a university established by
law or a statutory Board/Faculty/Examianing Body of Indian
Medicine and equivalent.
 Or
Ayurvedacharya of All India Ayurved Vidyapeeth.
 Or
Other eminent Ayurvedic scholars of established repute though
not having any degree/diploma, but fit for teaching Ayurvedic
subjects.
b. Teaching experience in any institution for ten years, five years
and three years for the post of Professor, Reader and Lecturer
respectively.
c. Knowledge of Sanskrit.
II. DESIRABLE:
a. Post Graduate qualification in Ayurved from a recognized
institution/University established by law.
b. Original published papers/books on the subject."
97. Coupled with the aforementioned prescribed
qualification, this Court has also found that Chapter XVI-B of the
Bihar University Calendar, the learned Chancellor had framed
Statutes laying down the "number, grades, qualifications, pay scale,
mode of appointment of teachers and officers in the faculty of
Ayurved." Clause-1 of the said Statutes laying down the qualification
of teachers and officers, being relevant for the purpose of this Court,
is quoted herein below:
1. The following shall be grades, pay scales and minimum

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 107
qualifications of teachers and officers in the faculty of
Ayurveda:-
(a) Director/Principal.
 Pay scales:- 1200-15-1515-EB-57-1800 plus rent free
quarter or 10% of pay in lieu thereof.
 Qualification:- Degree in Ayurveda from a University
or a Board/Council established by a State or Central Government
and recognized by the University, with atleast 18 years of
teaching experience in a recognized Ayurvedic College of which
not less than 8 years should be liend of a Department.
(b) Professor:
 Pay scale: - 800-10-1100-45-1190-EB-62-1450.
 Qualification:- Degree in Ayurveda from a University
or a Board/Council established by a State or Central Government
and recognized by the University, with atleast 10 years of
teaching experience in a recognized Ayurvedic College.
Or
 A Post-graduate degree in Ayurveda from a University
or from an institution established by a State or Central
Government and recognized by the University and the Central
Council of Indian Medicines with atleast 7 years of teaching
experience in a recognized Ayurvedic College.
(c) Reader
 Pay scale:- 650-10-770-EB-35-875-EB-45-1100-EB-50-1300.
 Qualification:- Degree in Ayurveda from a University
or a Board/Council established by a State or Central Government
and recognized by the University, with atleast 7 years of
teaching experience in a recognized Ayurvedic College.
Or
 A Post-graduate degree in Ayurveda from a University
or from an institution established by a State or Central
Government and recognized by the University and the Central
Council of Indian Medicines with atleast 5 years of teaching
experience in a recognized Ayurvedic College.
(d) Lecturer
 Pay scale: - 610-20-670-30-940-EB-35-1155.
 Qualification:- Degree in Ayurveda from a University
or a Board/Council established by a State or Central Government
and recognized by the University, with atleast 3 years of
teaching experience in a recognized Ayurvedic College or with
atleast 3 years experience as a Medical Officer in
Government/University Dispensary.
 Provided further that a holder of M.B.B.S. degree from
any recognized University shall also be eligible for appointment
in Anatomis Physiology and Bio-Chemistry, Pathology,
Jurisprudence, Health and Hygiene and holder of MSc. Degree
will be eligible for Basic Science in the subject concerned.
(e) Demonstrator.
 Pay scale: - 550-20-610-30-670-EB-30-940.
 Qualification:- Degree in Ayurveda from a University
or a Board/Council established by a State or Central Government
and recognized by the University except that in Science subject
(i.e. Physics, Chemistry and Biology) the minimum qualification
shall be a 2nd class Master's degree of a recognized University in
the subject concerned.
Or

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 108
 A Post-graduate degree in Ayurveda from a University
or from an institution established by a State or Central
Government and recognized by the University and the Central
Council of Indian Medicines with atleast 7 years of teaching
experience in a recognized Ayurvedic College.

98. A question would, therefore, arise that as to whether
the petitioner, being a simple graduate in the subject of Botany,
whether he could have been absorbed in terms of Section 6(2) and (3)
of the Act on the basis of his alleged appointment on the post of
Lecturer in any subject in the college.
99. As noted above, the petitioner did not hold any
qualification in Ayurveda and as such he could not have been
appointed on the post of Lecturer in the subject of Ayurveda. The
submission that the appointment of the petitioner was made for
teaching science subject i.e. Physics, Chemistry and Botany, first of
all is not envisaged either under the CCIM norms or under the
Statutes, but even then, if this Court goes to the proviso of the
qualification laid down for the post of Lecturer, the basic degree of
M.Sc. in pay scale in Science was the minimum requirement.
100. It would become more clear if the qualification for
the post of Demonstrator is taken into consideration inasmuch as even
for holding the post of Demonstrator, the requirement would be
degree in Ayurveda from a recognized university or having second
class Master's degree in the subject concerned, namely, Physics,
Chemistry and Biology. Thus, having regard to the lack of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 109
qualification of the petitioner, his claim for absorption on the post of
Professor-cum-Garden Manager or Reader or Lecturer or even Garden
Manager cannot be allowed.
101. In fairness to the submission of Mr. Shivendra
Kishore, learned senior counsel of the petitioner, this Court also must
note that though he had tried to refer to CCIM norms for showing that
there was a post of Garden Superintendent or Assistant Garden
Superintendent, he had miserably failed to point out anything from the
CCIM norms as to whether the post of Garden
Superintendent/Assistant Garden Superintendent could be made
available to a person like the petitioner who was initially appointed as
a Lecturer in the basic Science subject assigned to teaching work of a
subject of Ayurveda, namely, Dravyagun and thereafter he was also
made Garden Manager.
102. This Court has again looked into the sanction of post
made by the Padwarg Samiti of the State Government under the
CCIM norms and in this regard, it would find that there is no
sanctioned post of Garden Manager so as to allow the petitioner to
even stake his claim against the said post. The submission that the
petitioner’s case ought to have been considered for a non-teaching
post of Garden Manager as the said post was existing in the norms of
C.C.I.M. One has to only go through the number of sanctioned post

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 110
by Padwarg Samitti, 191 in all and there is no post of Garden
Manager on which the petitioner’s services could have been absorbed.
In any event as on 01.06.1986, the petitioner was claiming to be
functioning as a Professor-cum-Garden Manager in the College and
once the Screening Committee and the government had found that the
petitioner did not hold the qualification for any teaching post, his
alternative absorption on a non-teaching post of Garden Manager was
permissible.
103. The claim of the petitioner of his being absorbed on
the post of Laboratory Technician in exercise of power under Section
6(3) of the Taking Over Act is equally misconceived. First of all, for
the post of Laboratory Technician, it has already come on record that
the requirement was not only of being Intermediate in the Science
subject but also having a specialized training known as MLT, an
aspect which will be also clear from reading of the judgment in the
case of those petitioners claiming absorption on the post of
Laboratory Technician. Suffice it to say that the petitioner did not
possess the qualification also for the post of Laboratory Technician.
104. As a matter of fact, the over stretched argument that
a person can also be even absorbed on the post of Garden Manager in
exercise of power under Section 6(3) of the Taking Over Act does not
seem to be correct either on fact or in law. What really Section 6(3) of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 111
the Taking Over Act seeks to convey is that the Government has to
take a decision as to whether an absorption has to be made in
Government service or the services of the person has to be terminated
or even has to be allowed to be continued on an ad-hoc basis for a
fixed term on contract and the expression "shall, where necessary,
redetermine the rank, pay allowances and other conditions of service"
is clearly referable to only such of the persons whose services are
absorbed . Thus, what Section 6 seeks to convey is that the
Government shall have power to determine the terms and conditions
of the teaching staff and other employees of the college, but it never
intends that if a person does not have a qualification for teaching post
and claims to be promoted on the post of Professor, he can ultimately
be absorbed on the post of Peon. That would be really stretching the
matter too far. Strictly speaking, the claim of absorption has already
been rejected earlier by this Court while partly modifying the taking
over Notification dated 9th December 1986 in which the cut off date
was first of March 1983.
105. This Court also does not agree with the submission of
learned Senior Counsel that Section 6 (3) of the Act envisages a
person claiming absorption against a teaching post to be absorbed
against a non-teaching post. As a matter of fact the spirit of Section
6(3) of the Act is that once the Screening Committee had submitted its

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 112
report after ascertaining the process of appointment, promotion and
confirmation in terms of the Act, Statutes and Regulation of the
University concerned as well as in keeping with the guideline laid
down by the Central Council of Indigenous Medicine, the
Government had to consider and decide the merit of each case
whether to absorb or terminate the service of such person or allow
him to continue on adhoc basis. The question of re-determination
under section 6(3) of the act is only meant for the purpose of such
persons who are to be absorbed. To cite an example if a working
teacher was working in the College as on 1.6.1986 on the post of
Professor and was found to be not fulfilling the requisite qualification
or his appointment on the post of Professor was contrary to the
provisions of the Act, Statutes, Regulation of the University
concerned and/or not in keeping with the guideline of the CCIM and
he could have been re-designated on the post of Lecturer, if he had
otherwise qualified on the post of Lecturer. Section 6(3) of the Act
does not mean that a person claiming absorption on the post of
Professor could be absorbed even on the post of Peon of the College
by the State Government in exercise of power under section 6(3) of
the Act.
106. Thus, it has to be essentially held that on 09.12.1986
when this college was taken over with the cut off date of 1.06.1986,

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 113
the petitioner Dharamdeo Pathak did not possess the requisite
qualification of the post of Professor-cum-Garden Manager and,
therefore, there is no error in rejecting the claim of his absorption.
107. This Court would however fail in its duty if it does
not take into notice the plea of discrimination which has been raised
by the petitioner in para no. 22 of the writ application which is quoted
herein below:
"22. That it is stated as per the Section 6(2) of the said Act the
employees were required to be considered whether they were
having requisite qualification and experience on the cut off date or
not, but in the notification dated 07.08.1998 (Annexure-9) the
government fixed the criteria beyond the section 6(2) of the Act
which was not permissible. It is also stated that other colleges
which were taken over by the same Act and their employees were
considered for absorption the Government considered only the
qualification and experience on the cut off date. The notification
dated 07.08.1998 (Annexure-9) was discriminatory in nature as the
separate criteria was fixed."
108. There cannot be a better vague pleading on the plea of
discrimination. This Court is not made aware as to who and whose
services were taken over by the Government by a different criteria and
the manner in which the plea of discrimination has been raised leaves
nothing for this Court but to hold that the same is completely lacking
in details so as to uphold the decision of refusing absorption of the
petitioner being bad on account of its being discriminatory in nature.
109. Additinally, this plea of discrimination in the matter
of absorption in the college was directly gone into the earlier
judgment of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) wherein the same was
clearly negatived vide paragraph nos. 22 to 28. Therein while

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 114
remitting the matter back to the State Government keeping in view
that there was no such massive appointment made in two colleges
where the number of total employees was only 13 and 26 respectively.
110. Thus, as with regard to the petitioner’s plea of
discrimination all that need to be noted here is that a similar argument
was also made in the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) for
automatic absorption by citing example of earlier takeover of the two
other Ayurvedic medical colleges at Darbhanga and Bhagalpur, but
they were rejected on the ground of the provisions made under
Section 6 of the Takeover Act. That judgment has already become
final. The petitioner cannot now re-agitate the same issued and in fact
this Court does not find any infirmity in reexamining of credentials of
the petitioners by the State Government for the purposes of absorption
in the college in view of specific direction of this Court in the case of
Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra).
111. This Court therefore will have no difficulty in
rejecting the case of the petitioner Dharmdeo Pathak for more than
one reason. Appointment in any affiliated college at any point of time
was permissible by the Governing Body only for a maximum period
of six months. Beyond the period of six months, appointment was to
be made only with the recommendation of the University Service
Commission/ College Service Commission which had to advertise the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 115
post in newspaper and also recommend the candidates only after
following the selection process through interview. Such provision was
already existing even in Bihar State Universities Act, 1960 which was
in force on the date of appointment of the petitioner Dharmdeo Pathak
and eventually was also incorporated in Bihar State Universities Act
which came into force on 31.12.1976.
 112. In any event it will be absurd to hold that though the
petitioner had no qualification for holding any teaching post, much
less on the post of Lecturer and Reader in Ayurvedic Medical
Colleges, yet his absorption should be made on a teaching post of
Reader which he was holding on the cut off date i.e. 1.6.1986. As has
been noted above, for appointment on the post of Lecturer the CCIM
had already prescribed the qualification under which the
degree/diploma in Ayurveda from a University established by law was
a condition precedent. Thus, either the appointment of the petitioner
as a Lecturer on 13.8.1976 or his promotion on the post of Reader on
25.1.1984 as well as Garden Manager-cum-Professor on 5.7.1985 was
contrary to the provisions made in the statutes, inasmuch as he did not
have any qualification of the subject of Ayurveda and as such,
appointment/promotion had also never received approval of Syndicate
in terms of the Statutes in Chapter XVI-B, where for the post of
Professor apart from holding degree in Ayurveda one had to be

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 116
backed by ten years of teaching experience in a recognize Ayurvedic
College.
 113. In any event in the appointment and continuation of
the petitioner as a Lecturer and his eventual promotion as a Reader in
the year 1984 (on 25.1.1984) and his promotion on the post of
Professor on 5.7.1985 there was a clear infringement of mandatory
provision of Clause 6, 7 and 8 of the statutes under Chapter XVI B
inasmuch as his appointment/promotion had never received approval
of the Syndicate of the University and that he continued beyond a
period of six months without recommendation of the Bihar College
Service Commission. Judged in this background, this Court will have
no difficulty in holding that the petitioner’s absorption on a teaching
post was wholly impermissible as he lacked the basic qualification for
holding the post of teacher in the College which have been termed to
be ab initio void in paragraph 28 of the judgment of Dr. Satyadeo
Pandey (supra) wherin it was held as follows:-
“For this purpose the cases of all such petitioners is
remitted back to the State Government. It can look into
validity of appointment in a particular case on ground of
fraud or appointment being ab initio void for lack of basic
academic qualification or into facts showing
termination/cessation before 25.3.1984 or the cut off date
etc.”
 114. Thus, this Court will have no difficulty in rejecting
the case of the petitioner Dharmdeo Pathak for his absorption on any
post much less on a teaching post.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 117
 115. For the reasons indicated above, this writ
application must fail and is, accordingly, dismissed.
Re.: C.W.J.C. No. 6464 of 2008
 116. In this writ application, four petitioners have sought
the same relief as sought by the petitioner of C.W.J.C. No. 10417 of
2008 i.e. for absorption on the post of teachers. In this regard, the case
of the petitioner no. 1, namely, Dr. Kamta Prasad Mishra, is that he,
being a graduate in Ayurved, was directly appointed on the post of
Lecturer in the Department of Salakya and was latter on transferred to
the Department of Chikitsa on 25.09.1981 and ultimately he was
promoted on the post of Reader on 13.10.1983 and on the post of
Professor on 07.06.1985. The petitioner though has not enclosed any
document in support of the aforementioned claim but relied on first
screening report.

120. The petitioner no. 4, namely, Dr. Ajiteshwar

found him unfit by giving the following reasons:-

 121. Thereafter services of all the four petitioners were
terminated by a common order dated 29.8.2003 which was assailed by
them and others in a number of writ applications which were heard
together and were disposed of by a common judgment dated
11.3.2005 in the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra). In view of the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 121
direction given by this Court, the Review Committee dated 11.4.2007
had again considered their case and had found them unfit for
absorption. The Review Committee in fact had considered all cases of
165 persons and had recommended for absorption of 24 persons
holding teaching post and 42 persons holding non-teaching post and
rest 99 of them were found unfit for absorption and the petitioners are
also among those 99 persons. Though the order dated 17.10.2007 does
not give any indication as with regard to the reasons for finding the
petitioners unfit but the report of the Review Committee brought on
record by the petitioners as Annexure-23 at page 236 indicates that the
petitioners in the list of 18 teaching employees were found unfit on
account of lack of prescribed qualification/experience as recorded in
the report of the second Screening Committee.
 122. The Review Committee as with regard to absorption
of teachers including the petitoiers had recorded as follows:-

;ksX; ugha ik;k x;kA**
 123. The submission of Mr. Gyanendra Roy, learned
counsel appearing on behalf of the petitioners, that consideration of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 122
the case should have been made only on the basis of facts existing on
the cut-off date i.e. 1.6.1986 and his specific reference in this regard
to the notification dated 9.12.1986 is also to be noted for its being
rejected. The cut-off date is with regard persons continuing in the
services of the College and when Section 6 talks of absorption to be
based on screening to be made as to whether the appointment,
promotion or confirmation was made in accordance with Act,
Statutes, Regulation of the University concerned and in keeping with
the guideline made by the CCIM, it necessarily leads to an enquiry in
initial appointment and the continuation of the person concerned in
the College after cut-off date. Since none of the petitioners had the
qualification itself at the time of their appointment by way of three
years teaching experience in a recognized Ayurvedic College, the
Government was absolutely justified in holding that their appointment
was contrary to the provisions made in the Statutes, which, as noted
above, was well in force on the date of appointment of each and every
petitioner.
 124. Mr. D.K.Sinha, learned AAG-2, appearing on behalf
of the State, has rightly submitted that even these four petitioners
would not fulfil the requisite qualifications for the teaching post of
Lecturer inasmuch as both under the Central Council of Indian
Medicine (hereinafter referred to as 'the CCIM') norms and the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 123
Statutes. As referred above, the minimum eligibility criteria was not
only for possessing the qualification of Graduation in Ayurvedic
subject, but also backed by three years post qualification experience
but none of the four petitioners had possessed the same. He has also
correctly submitted that the appointment of the petitioners, beyond the
period of six months, was not permissible either under the terms of the
Bihar State University Act or the Statutes framed thereudner because
even if the college was an affiliated college to Bihar University, it was
required to advertise the post of Lecturer and got the selection made
through the Bihar College Service Commission. According to Mr.
Sinha, the claim of promotion of the year 1985-86 being beyond the
cut of date of 25.03.1984 will have no meaning especially when even
for such promotion the norms prescribed by the university in Chapter
XVI B of the Statutes were not followed.
 125. In the case of these four petitioners, this Court
would find that they had the educational qualification for the post of
Lecturer, but then they were not fulfilling the minimum eligibility
criteria of the required three years post qualification experience. The
requirement of three years post qualification experience for the post of
Lecturer was part of the mandatory qualification. This experience for
the post of Lecturer, therefore, cannot be said to be acquired by the
petitioners. This aspect of the matter stands settled by the judgment of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 124
the Apex Court in the case of Indian Airlines (supra).
 126. As noted above, none of the petitioners have
brought their certificates of passing the Ayurvedic Degree course nor
have even sought to claim that they were backed by any teaching
experience on the date of their appointment on the post of Lecturer in
the college. Thus, if they did not possess the requisite qualification
and their appointment on the post of Lecturer, beyond the period of
six months, was not even permissible as per the Act and Statues, their
appointment in terms of paragraph no.28 of the judgment of Dr.
Satyadeo Pandey (supra) was ab initio void. The Government,
therefore, cannot be said to have committed any error in not absorbing
petitioners on the teaching posts.
 127. That apart appointment of the petitioners was in
teeth of the Statutes not only in relation to qualification but also in
terms of Clause 6, 7 and 8 under which any person could be appointed
by the Governing Body/ Managing Committee of an affiliated College
only for a maximum period of six months, whereafter such
appointment could be continued only with the approval of the Bihar
College Service Commission as well as the approval of the Syndicate.
The appointment and continuation of the petitioners being in teeth of
Clause 6, 7 and 8 of the Statutes under Chapter XVI B, no flaw can be
said to have been committed by the 2nd Screening Committee and

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 125
Review Committee in holding that the petitioners were not fit to be
absorbed in the services of the College.
 128. Mr. Roy alike Mr. Shivendra Kishore had made an
alternative submission that even if the petitioners are held not fit to be
absorbed on the post of Lecturer, there being nine post of Laboratory
Technician created by the Padwarg Samiti and since no one was
absorbed against those post in the order of the State Government
dated 17.10.2007, the petitioners could be absorbed against non-teaching post of Laboratory Technician.
 129. In the considered opinion of this Court, the issue of
absorbing teaching employees against non-teaching post could arise
only when they had given up their claim for appointment on the post
of teaching post. As a matter of fact there are eleven petitioners in the
group of non-teaching employees, who are also claiming their
absorption on the post of Laboratory Technician and therefore,
automatic absorption of the petitioners on the post of Laboratory
Technician was neither permissible nor can be directed by this Court
specially when they too alike the petitioner Dharmadeo Pathak of
preceding case in CWJC No. 10417 of 2008 did not possess the
qualification of one year specialized training, namely, M.C.T. Thus,
for the reasons already explained earlier while deciding the case of
Dharmadeo Pathak, the claim of the petitioners for their absorption on

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 126
the post of Laboratory Technician is also rejected.
 130. Thus, the case of all the four petitioners of C.W.J.C.
No. 6464/2008 as with regard to their absorption in the services of the
College must fail and their writ application is also, accordingly,
dismissed.
Re. : C.W.J.C.No. 7650/2008
 131. The sole petitioner Pramod Kumar while seeking
his absorption has claimed that his appointment was made on the post
of Demonstrator in the Department of Science on 22.11.1981 and he
had jointed the post of Demonstrator on 1.12.1981. According to him,
he was subsequently promoted on the post of Lecturer vide resolution
of the Governing Body dated 16.1.1985.
132. After takeover of the college, the services of the
petitioner were subjected to Screening and in the gazette notification
dated 2.1.1992, the First Screening Committee had opined as
followed:-

 134. The services of this petitioner was also terminated
by the common order dated 29.8.2003 and when he too had moved
this Court, his writ application was also disposed of by the common
judgment in the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) remitting his
case as well for reconsideration by the State Government.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 128
 135. The Review Committee dated 11.4.2007 had also
considered his case along with other teaching employees and had
recorded as follows:-

 136. The State Government, thereafter, by its order dated
17.10.2007 had absorbed services of only twenty two teachers and did
not include the petitioner therein for lack of qualification for the post
of Demonstrator on which he was appointed in 1981 or promoted on
the post of Lecturer by the Governing Body on 16.1.1985 i.e. after
24.3.1984 a cut off date fixed by this Court in the judgment of Dr.
Satyadeo Pandey (supra).
 137. This Court would find that this petitioner also did
not possess the requisite qualification for the post of Demonstrator,
which under the Statutes under Chapter-XVI B was as follows:
" Qualifications:- Degree in Ayurveda from a recognized
University or Board/ Council established by a State or Central
Government and recognized by the University except that in
Science subjects (i.e. Physics, Chemistry and Biology) the
minimum qualification shall be a 2nd class Master's degree of a
recognized University in the subject concerned."
 138. As a matter of fact the petitioner does not hold any
degree in Ayurveda not he was a Post-graduate in any subject of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 129
Science and as such, his alleged appointment as a Demonstrator in the
College was void ab initio as explained in paragraph no.28 of the
judgment of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra). The petitioner as on the
date of his appointment on the post of Demonstrator was simply a
Graduate, inasmuch as he claims that till 4.11.1982 he was working in
the College only on the basis of his qualification of Graduation and
claimed that he had passed M.Sc. examination in the year 1983-84
from DAV College, Kanpur as a private candidate while still
continuing in service of the college. His case is, therefore, squarely
covered by paragraph no.34 of the judgment of CWJC No. 10043 of
2003 in the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) wherein it was held
as follows:-
"34. From the records of CWJC No. 10048/03 it appears that it
was dismissed as withdrawn on 20.1.05 since he wanted to pursue
his representation/appeal before the Chief Minister submitted
against order of termination dated 7.7.03. The impugned order of
termination in that writ petition shows that one of the charges
which was found correct was that there is no provision for taking
B.Sc. Examination as an independent candidate as claimed by the
petitioner and in the same year he also claimed to have passed the
professional degree of B.A.M.S. from another University which
was also not permissible. In these facts and circumstance, this
court finds no good ground to interfere with report of the
Screening Committee and the decision of the State Government in
respect of this petitioner. His writ petition is, therefore, dismissed."
 139. Be that as it may, once the petitioner came out with
his case of being a Demonstrator at the time of his appointment
without any qualification either of degree in Ayurveda or Post-graduate, his subsequent acquiring qualification of M.Sc., which is
also shrouded a mystery as per findings of the Screening Committee's

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 130
reports, he will not qualify for absorption on the post of Lecturer
which he was holding on the cut-off date i.e. 1.6.1986. As a matter of
fact the petitioner's initial appointment on the post of Demonstrator in
the subject of Science, a post which was never existing and his being
also not holding qualification for the post of Demonstrator as well as
the petitioner's alleged promotion on 16.1.1985 by the Governing
Body without mentioning any department, whereas no Lecturer could
be appointed in Ayurvedic College on the basis of even a Post-graduation qualification of Master in Science are sufficient to show
that the petitioner's appointment also was in teeth of the provisions
made under the Statutes and therefore, rejection of his case for
absorption on the post of Lecturer by the Government does not suffer
from any infirmity.
 140. Thus, for the reasons indicated above, this writ
application also must fail and is, accordingly, dismissed.
Re: C.W.J.C. No. 7880/2008
 141. The sole petitioner Shashi Bhushan Tiwary,
allegedly working as Nirman Vaidya on the cut-off date of 1.6.1986,
claims his absorption in the College on the basis that he had initially
entered in the College as Aushadhi Nirman Sahayak on the basis of
his qualification, a non-teaching post, on 1.7.1980 and thereafter he
was appointed afresh on 1.3.1981 as a Rasayanshala Sahayak and

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 131
ultimately was promoted on the post of Vaidya on 4.3.2003.
 142. The first Screening Committee in its report dated
2.1.1992 had recorded the following findings:-

 144. Based on the aforementioned findings of the second
Screening Committee, his services were terminated on 29.8.2003
whereafter his writ application along with others assailing the
common order of termination were disposed of on 11.3.2005 directing
review of the cases by a Review Committee with certain observations
and directions. The case of the petitioner was also rejected by the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 132
Government in view of report of 2nd Screening Committee and
review committee in terms of the order dated 17.10.2007.
 145. The Review Committee dated 11.4.2007 as with
regard to the petitioner allegedly appointed on the post of Ausadhi on
the post of Sahayak had held as follows:-

ds in ij dh x;h FkhA fdUrq buds ikl fu;qfDr ds le; rhu o"kksZa dk
'kS{kf.kd vuqHko izkIr ugha FkkA ftlds pyrs Ldzhuhax lfefr us
lek;kstu ds ;ksX; ugha ik;k Fkk vkSj leh{kk lfefr Hkh Ldzhuhax lfefr
ds izfrosnu ls lger gksrs gh bUgs a ljdkjh lsok es a lek;kstu ;ksX; ugha

 146. According to the learned counsel for the petitioner,
even if the petitioner may not have the prescribed qualification for the
post of Vaidya as per Statutes of the University, his absorption could
have been still made on a non-teaching post of Aushadhi Nirman
Sahayak.
 147. This Court would find it difficulty to accept such
submission for more than one reason. Firstly, on the cut-off date the
petitioner claimed that he was continued as Vaidya and his absorption
should also be made on the post of Vaidya. For the post of Vaidya
under the Statutes the qualification was degree in Ayurveda from
recognized University or the Board/ Council established by the State
or Central Government and recognized by the University. The
petitioner admittedly had passed only the examination of Sanskrit
course, namely, "Navinotar Madhyama". Such qualification of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 133
Sanskrit subject alone cannot qualify the petitioner to become Vaidya
as he was sought to be appointed by the Managing Committee on
4.3.2003. As a matter of fact the qualification of the petitioner of
Navinotar Madhyama would also not qualify him for the post of
Aushadhi Nirman Sahayak as he was not required to manufacture
medicine of Sanskrit.
 148. Be that as it may, the petitioner did not possess
qualification of holding a degree in Ayurveda and as such, his
absorption on the post of Vaidya, one which was holding on the cut-off date, was wholly impermissible and this Court does not find any
error in the decision taken by the authorities rejecting his case for
absorption in the services of the College.
 149. The petitioner's case for absorption on the post of
Ausadhi Nirman Sahayak also does not merit acceptance in view of
his having no qualification for the post and also because of tampering
of the record of proceeding of the Governing Body as noticed in the
report of 2nd Screening Committee.
 150. Thus for all the aforesaid reasons, this Court cannot
find any error in rejecting the case of this petitioner for absorption in
the service of the College.
 151. That being so, there is no merit in this writ
application and the same is, accordingly, dismissed.

 155. The services of this petitioner was terminated by
the common order dated 29.8.2003 which is said to have been assailed
by the petitioner in CWJC No. 11414 of 2003, which was disposed of
by the common judgment dated 11.3.2005 in the case of Dr. Sayadeo

Review Committee dated 11.4.2007 did not find the petitioner fit for

follows:-

 156. As noted above, the Government on reconsideration
of findings of 2nd Screening Committee report and Review
Committee report has rejected his case for absorption on the ground
that this petitioner also did not possess the requisite qualification as
prescribed in the Statutes, namely, three years post qualification
experience at the time of his appointment on 1.2.1978.
 157. Learned counsel for the petitioner has basically

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 136
concentrated on the aspect that in the advertisement, which was issued
in the newspaper for his appointment on 9th March, 1978, there was
no specific mention of post qualification teaching experience of three
years and therefore, his appointment made on the basis of such
advertisement cannot be faulted either on fact or in law, much less for
the purposes of rejection of his claim for absorption on teaching post
of Professor, which he was holding on the cut-off date i.e. 1.6.1986.
 158. This case in fact is the eye opener because at least
this petitioner has come out to say that in the year 1978 the Governing
Body of the College used to advertise the post for making
appointment but the advertisement, which has been annexed by him,
will itself go to show that the Secretary of the College did not
prescribe any qualification, rather had mentioned the qualification to
be as per the Bihar State University Rules. Such advertisement being
relevant not only for the purposes of this case but for other cases as
well is quoted hereinbelow:
"THE INDIAN NATION, THURSDAY, MARCH, 9TH, 1978
SITUATIONS VACANT
WANTED
Principal and Lecturers in Shri Dhanwantri Ayurvedic
Mahavidyalaya, Buxar in Anatomy, Nidan Ras Pharmacy
Materia M. Hygiene Science Sanskrit Grammar, Botany,
Physicas, History of Ay. Vaidyakiya subhasitani qualifications
and emoluments as per Bihar University Rules. Candidate with
experience preferred. Apply to the Secretary alongwith
testimonials on or before 10 A.M. on 12.3.1978 and come for
interview on 12 A.M. on 12.3.1978 in the college premises. No
T.A. allowed.
 Dr. S.N. Tiwari
 Secretary
EIC 506-1-1"

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 137
 159. As noted above, there was a Statutes of the
University which prescribed qualification for the post of Lecturer,
Reader and Professor and also the manner of appointment as well as
approval of such appointment only on the basis of recommendation of
Bihar College Service Commission and approval by the Syndicate of
the University. Thus, there would be no difficulty in holding that the
appointment of the petitioner on the post of Lecturer was again made
contrary to the provisions made in the Statutes as well as in CCIM
norms, both of whom are common as with regard to qualification on
the post of Lecturer requiring post qualification experience of three
years apart from the degree in Ayurveda.
 160. This Court in fact could have given the benefit of
doubt to the petitioner in view of Chancellor's affidavit that the
Statutes had come into force only with effect from 7.8.1979, wherein
his appointment was made on the post of Lecturer on 1.2.1978 but
then the petitioner will still not cross the hurdle because of norms of
CCIM of the year 1970 which again was part of the statutory
consideration under section 6 of the Act. Under CCIM norms, as
quoted above, the requirement of post qualification experience of
three years for the post of Lecturer was always there and therefore, no
exception could have been made in the case of the petitioner by the
State Government for the purposes of his absorption.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 138
 161. True it is that the petitioner had a degree in
Ayurveda on the date of his appointment on the post of Lecturer but
he had no post qualification teaching experience of three years on the
date of his appointment. Similarly, when he got promoted within five
years on the post of Reader and he did not have post qualification
teaching experience of seven years nor he had ten years required
qualification teaching experience on the date of promotion on the post
of Professor on 31.1.1985, it can be very well held that there is no
infirmity in rejecting the case of absorption of the petitioner.
 162. Thus, this Court will have no difficulty in coming to
the conclusion that the claim of petitioner's absorption on the post was
rightly rejected by the State Government.
 163. In the result, this application also fails and is,
accordingly, dismissed.
Re.: C.W.J.C.No. 8545/2008
164. The sole petitioner in this case is said to have been
appointed as Demonstrator on 11.11.1981 by the Governing Body of
the college and his service is said to have been terminated by the
college on 10.9.1985 under the order of the Principal of the college
whereafter he is said to have been reinstated in service in view of the
resolution passed by the Governing Body on 1.9.1986 on the basis of
which he is said to have started functioning with effect from 2.9.1986.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 139
165. When the college was taken-over, the services of
the petitioner were absorbed by an order dated 18.9.1995 but in course
of the second Screening, his services were not found to be fit for
being absorbed as he was not in service on the cut off date i.e.
1.6.1986 and to that extent, the report of the Review Committee in
support of the petitioner reads as follows:-

 166. The petitioner having been removed from service by
the common order dated 29.8.2003 is said to have moved this Court in
CWJC No. 10574 of 2003 and his case was also rejected refusing his
absorption in the college on the basis of the findings recorded in the
second Screening Committee report and the report of Review
Committee dated 11.4.2007 wherein it was held as follows:-

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 140
lek;kstu ds ;ksX; iuk;k x;k 'ks"k 18 dehZ ftugsa fu;qfDr ds le;

;ksX;rk@vgrkZ ds vHkko esa iwoZ dsa Ldzhuh ax lfefr dh vuq'kalk ls lger
gksrs gq, ljdkjh lsok esa lek;kstu ds ;ksX; ugha ik;k x;kA**
 167. This Court will have no difficulty in approving the
aforementioned reasons of the State Government for more than one
reason. Firstly, the petitioner as on 1.11.1981 was simply a Bachelor
in Science. For the post of Demonstrator the requirement under the
Statutes was a degree in Ayurveda or Post-graduation. Since the
petitioner was appointed as a Demonstrator not in the subject of
Science but in Kaya Chikitsha Vibhag, he had to necessarily possess
the qualification of degree in Ayurveda. The petitioner has never
brought any material on record the fact that he had passed his GAMS
course prior to his appointment on the post of Demonstrator on
1.11.1981.
 168. That apart, the petitioner was not in the service of
the College as on 1.6.1986 on account of termination of service on
10.9.1985. Thus, in view of the mandate of law under which take-over
was to be made, the petitioner was entitled to be absorbed in the
service of the college. From reading of Section 6 of the Act it is
absolutely clear that absorption was to be made only in respect of
staff, who were working in the College on the cut-off date as is clear
from section 6(1) of the Act. It is an admitted fact that the petitioner's
services were terminated on 10.9.1985 much before take-over
notification was issued on 9.12.1986 with effect from cut-off date of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 141
1.6.1986 as per direction of this Court. Admittedly on 1.6.1986 the
petitioner was not in service and therefore, if the petitioner claims that
the Governing Body had taken a decision on 1.9.1986 to take him
back in service, that may not come to his rescue because this Court
has straightway rejected the case of the persons, whose appointments
were made after 25.3.1984. In this regard, this Court, in the case of
Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) had clearly held that the proceedings of
the Governing Body after 25.3.1984 being tainted were not worth
reliance and had observed as follows:-
"28. ………, Since the facts collected by the Screeening
Committee can be safely used by the State Government or any
Review Committee which may be appointed by it for the
purpose, this Court is of the view that no useful purpose will be
served by remetting the matter in respect of employees
appointed prior to 25.03.1984 for reexamination by the
Screening Committee. The report submitted by the Screening
Committee shall be used by the State Government or any
Review Committee that may be constituted by the State
Government to help it to come to a proper decision, by keeping
in mind the findings of this court recorded earlier in this
judgment pointing out the illegalities in the approach of the
Screening Committee. Such illegalities must be avoided by
treating that the offending parts of the report of the Screening
Committee in respect of such employees has been quashed by
this Court. Only the balance part of the report of Screening
Committee shall be used by the State Govennment or the
Review Committee for coming to a just and proper decision in
respect of absorption of employees of the college appointed
prior to 25.03.1984. For this purpose the cases of all such
petitioners is remitted back to the State Government. It can look
into validity of appointment in any particular case on ground of
fraud or appointment being void ab initio for lack of basic
academic qualification or into facts showing
termination/cessation service before the date 25.03.1984 or the
cut off date etc. But none of the criterial disapproved earlier by
this order shall be applied for this purpose."
 169. This aspect of the matter becomes further clear
when this Court has ultimately been able to get the entire copy of the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 142
resolution of the Governing Body from the Vigilance Department and
from perusal of them it is found that the last meeting of the Governing
Body was held on 14.9.1985. Thus, if the services of the petitioner
were already terminated as on 10.9.1995 under the order of the
Principal of the College, this Court will have no difficulty in holding
that the alleged decision dated 1.9.1986 of the petitioner being taken
back in service is based on fitment of imagination of the petitioner.
There is nothing on record to show that the Governing Body had ever
held the meeting as on 1.9.1986 and in any event when the petitioner
himself has not brought on record the resolution of the Governing
Body by which he got reappointed on the terms and conditions that he
will not get any salary for the intervening period, his such
appointment will within the category of post 25.3.1984 appointee
whose cases have already been rejected by this Court in the case of
Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra).
 170. Thus, the case of the petitioner Shyam Narain
Upadhaya will also not merit any further consideration, inasmuch as
there is no flaw in rejecting his case of absorption both on account of
his being not qualified for the post of Demonstrator on the date of his
appointment on 1.11.1981 and his also not being in service of the
college on the cut off date i.e. 1.6.1986 on account of his termination
of service on 10.9.1985.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 143
 171. In the result, this application is also dismissed.
Re.: C.W.J.C.No. 9584/2008
 172. The sole petitioner Dr. Rangnath Pathak has claimed
absorption and in the process has assailed the impugned order of the
State Government primarily on the ground that his appointment on the
post of Demonstrator on 7.10.1981 in the Department of Shalakya did
not suffer from any infirmity, inasmuch as he had a valid Ayurvedic
qualification of having degree of Graduate in Ayurvedic Medicine and
Surgery (GAMS) and in fact his continuation in the College by way of
transfer from the post of Demonstrator to the post of Chikitsha
Padadhikari on 19.8.1985 and ultimately his promotion on the post of
Lecturer in the department of Sanskrit Sanghita on 15.11.1986 did not
suffer from any infirmity. As a matter of fact this Court has found that
the petitioner on the cut-off date of 1.6.1986 was working as
Chikitsha Padadhikari, which as per Statutes is a non-teaching post,
whereas the post of Demonstrator and Lecturer in addition to
Professor, Reader are teaching post.
173. In respect of this petitonier, it is found that after
takeover of the college, the first Screening Committee while
recommending him for absorption in the post of Medical Officer had
recorded the following findings:-

 175. His services having been terminated on
29.8.2003, he had filed CWJC No. 10254 of 2003 assailing the
report of the second Screening Committee and the consequential
order of termination dated 29.8.2003 which was disposed of by a
common judgment dated 11.3.2005 in the case of Dr. Satyadeo
Pandey (supra) remitting the matter back to the State Government
for reconsideration on the basis of findings of 2nd Screening
Committee Report and Review Committee. The Review Committee

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 145
did not find him fit for absorption while notifying its decision dated
17.10.2007 wherein it had recorded as follows:-
^^leh{kk lfefr }kjk ekuuh; mPp U;k;ky; }kjk ikfjr vkns'k

lger gksrs gq, ljdkjh l sok esa lek;kstu ds ;ksX; ugha ik;k x;kA**
 176. It is significant to mention here that the petitioner
has not enclosed the certificate of his passing GAMS examination
in November, 1979 to refute the findings of the Committee and had
enclosed only show-cause reply as contained in Annexure-9 to
show that the original certificate was issued to him on 20.9.1982
whereas his appointment has been made on 7.10.1981 in response
of which he had joined on 5.2.1982.
 177. Before this Court when the learned counsel for the
petitioner had tried to assail the reasons for his services being not
absorbed by the State Government, he had produced the original
certificate of G.A.M.S. of the petitioner but not the one which was
gone into at length by the Screening Committee. In fact the petitioner
in his writ application has produced two certificates by way of
Annexure 1, in which first one clearly shows that the name of the
College from which the petitioner is said to have completed study

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 146
starts with "Rajkiya" and then remains blank, whereas in the second
certificate "Patna Rajkiya Ayurvedic College". When this Court had
tried to know from the learned counsel for the petitioner about such
discrepancy, learned counsel for the petitioner had produced the
original certificate, which is kept on record but the same is one dated
20th December, 1982 and on the back side of it, it is mentioned to be
'duplicate'.
 178. The Second Screening Committee which had
examined into the certificate of the petitioner was one which was
original and its photocopy is Annexure 1, where, as noted above, the
name of the College in which the petitoiner had studed is definitely
missing. Thus, if now in this background the case of petitioner is
examined, it would be found that here is a case of appointment when
the petitioner is said to have been appointed on 7.10.1981 on the post
of Demonstrator with a condition that his joining will come into effect
with effect from 5.2.1982. Learned counsel for the petitioner does not
explain it as to when the result of GAMS examination of the
petitioner was declared. The examination as per the certificate was
held in November, 1979 and the petitioner in the writ application
claims that he had completed his GAMS course in the year 1979.
There is, therefore, much more to meet the eyes than what the
petitioner had sought to project in his writ application. The petitioner's

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 147
appointment on 7.10.1981, on a date on which he had not even passed
his GAMS examination nor had any certificate of degree of GAMS,
only goes to show that the private Governing Body had appointed the
petitioner even before his allegedly passing GAMS examination.
 179. In this regard it also remains unexplained as to how
the petitioner, who was appointed as a Demonstrator in Shalakya
Vibhag, got promoted on the post of Lecturer in Sanskrit Sanghita.
His all such explanation before this Court, which was also given by
him before the authorities in answer to the show cause notice, by itself
would expose his efforts to mislead this Court by producing a forged
certificate before this Court which was never produced by him before
the Second Screening Committee. As noted above, the petitioner
subsequently has obtained a duplicate certificate in which he has got
the name of the College filled up or created, whereas the original
certificate does not mention the name of the College. In such a
situation, the petitioner's claim absorption on the post of Lecturer that
he was holding on the cut-off date of 1.6.1986 or Demonstrator on
which he was appointed on 7.10.1981 without passing the G.A.M.S.
examination cannot qualify him for being absorbed in the services of
the College.
 180. The claim of the petitioner that he could have been
absorbed on the post of Chikitsha Padadhikari, the post he was

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 148
holding on the date of take over of the college inasmuch as, the
Prashashi Padbarg Samiti had created 15 posts of Resident Medical
Officer/ Clinical Registrar/ Ayurvedic Chikitsha Padadhikari/
Panchkarma Vaidhya, can also be of no avail, inasmuch as such
decision for transfer of the petitioner to the post of Medical Officer
was taken by the Governing Body to transfer him from the post of
Deminstrator on 10.8.1985, whereas this Court in the case of Dr.
Satyadeo Pandey (supra) has each and every decision of the
Governing Body after 25.3.1984 to be vitiated by fraud.
 181. Thus, to sum up, this Court must hold that the
petitioner has not approached this Court with clean hands. His claim
of appointment on the post of Demonstrator on 7.10.1981 at a point of
time he had not possessed the minimum prescribed qualification for
the Demonstrator as prescribed in Chaptere XVI B of the Statute and
his effort to make a claim for fresh appointment on the post of
Chikitsa Padakikari on 10.8.1985 in view of the discrepancy in his
certificate of G.A.M.S. in fact would amount to fraud as was
explained in paragraph no. 28 of the judgment of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey
(supra). In that view of the matter, this Court does not find any reason
to now direct the Government to reconsider the case of the petitioner
for his absorption even on the post of Chikitsha Padadhikari.
 182. Thus, for the reasons given above, this writ

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 149
application also fails and is, accordingly, dismissed.
Re.: C.W.J.C.No. 10047/2008
183. The sole petitioner in this case is said to have been
appointed on the post of Demonstrator on 1.2.1979 whereof he is said
to have been made permanent under the decision of the Governing
Body dated 1.5.1981 and subsequently in view of his qualification of
being B.Sc. as Ayurvedic Ratan, he was directed to discharge the duty
of Demonstrator as well as Lecturer in view of his order of promotion
dated 23.2.1982.
184. When the college was takenover on 9.12.1986 with
effect from 1.6.1986, the first Screening Committee had found him
unfit for the post of Lecturer and had recommended for his absorption
on the post of Laboratory Technician by recording as follows:-

ch-,l-lh- gSA vr% budh ;ksX;rk ds vuqlkj bUgs a ySc VSduhf'k;u ds in
ij ekuk tk ldrk gSA**
185. The petitioner in fact claims that he has continued
for the post of Laboratory Technician but his payment of salary was
abruptly stopped from the month of August 2003. Though his name
was neither included in the list of terminated employees dated
25.8.2003 nor in the list of the retained employees and is not being

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 150
paid his salary but a person similarly situated alike him, namely, Dr.
Shiv Shankar Rai who too was not terminated on 29.8.2003 has been
absorbed by an order dated 5.5.2005. There is no counter affidavit in
this case as with regard to the specific allegation.
 186. As noted above the petitioner's appointment is said
to have been made on 1.2.1979 on the post of Demonstrator in
Science Department under the order of the Secretary. It however no
repeatition that under the Statutes and even under the CCIM norms
not only the post of Deminstrator is a teaching post but the
qualification of Degree in Ayurveda or a post graduate in science
subject is a condition precedent.
 187. The story that Parasnath Choubey was made
permanent Demonstrator in Maulik Sidhyant on 1.5.1981 in view of
his qualification of being only a graduate in Science, in no view of the
matter would improve his case because even in Science Department
Post Graduation in Science subject was the minimum requirement for
the post of Demonstrator.
 188. The petitioner, who has died on 1.9.2015 and has
been substituted by his widow Chandrawati Devi, in view of the order
in I.A.No. 7998/2015, therefore, cannot be absorbed on the post of
Demonstrator.
 189. The further case of the petitioner that he was

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 151
promoted on the post of Lecturer on 23.2.1982 would make the case
worse because he did not possess the requisite qualification of Degree
in Ayurvedic science which was a condition precedent at all point of
time under the CCIM norms or under Chapter XVI-B of the Statutes.
 190. Thus, this Court will have no difficulty in rejecting
the case of late Parasnath Choubey, now sought to be substituted and
represented by her widow Smt. Chandrawati Devi and his sons and
his daughters.
 191. This writ application, therefore, being devoid of
any merit is, accordingly dismissed.
Re.: C.W.J.C.No. 14816/2008
 192. The petitioner being a widow of late Dr.
Ramashankar Choubey has claimed the absorption of service of her
husband stating that her husband Ramashankar Choubey was
appointed as a Demonstrator in the Department of Sarir Rachna on
1.8.1981 and his such appointment was also approved by the
Governing Body in the meeting held on 14.2.1982.
 193. According to the widow, petitioner, her husband
was holding the qualification of GAMS but his services were not
absorbed when the college was takenover with effect from 9.12.1986
and in fact he was given only a show-cause notice in view of the
findings of the second Screening Committee reading as follows:-

194. The husband of the petitioner in his show-cause
reply has claimed that though his services were terminated in the year
1983 but his services were reinstated on 17.1.1985 and as such, he
being a working employee on the cutoff date i.e. 1.6.1986 would be
entitled for his absorption. His such explanation was not accepted and
he too was removed from service by a common order dated 29.8.2003
and the husband of the petitioner had filed CWJC No. 9657 of 2003
which was disposed of in the batch of the writ application by a
common judgment dated 11.3.2005 whereafter in course of review,
the services of the husband of the petitioner were not absorbed and he
had in the meantime died on 11.8.2007. The widow, therefore, has

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 153
claimed absorption of the services of her husband by taking a plea that
since the college was takenover with effect from 1.6.1986, the
absorption of the service of her husband would give the benefit of the
payment of family pension as also salary of her husband.
195. In this writ application Shashi Kala Chaubey, the
widow of late Ramashankar Chaubey had claimed absorption of her
husband on the post of Lecturer.
196. From the perusal of the records it is found that late
Ramashankar Chaubey was appointed in the College by the Secretary
of the Governing Body on 1.8.1981 on the post of Demonstrator in
the department of Anatomy. It, however, appears that the services of
the husband of the petitioner were terminated on 13.10.1983 by the
Governing Body of the College. According to the case set out by the
petitioner, her husband was later on reappointed by an order of the
Secretary dated 2.6.1986 with effect from 1.6.1986.
197. As a matter of fact when reappointment is also said
to be made on 2.6.1986, that is also because beyond the cut-off date
on 1.6.1986 and in any event the Secretary's such decision on
2.6.1986 can have no sanctity in the eye of law, specially when the
power of appointment/ reappointment as per Statutes is vested in the
Governing Body and not in the Secretary. This Court had already
found from the records of Governning Body that no meeting of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 154
Governing Body was infact held on 1.6.1986 as has been discussed in
a similar case of 8545/2008 Shyam Narayan Upadhya Vs. State of
Bihar.
 198. Thus, the husband of the petitioner having been
now fallen in the category of post 25.3.1984 appointee, this Court
would find it difficult to issue any direction for his absorption,
inasmuch as any and every decision of the Governing Body of
appointment, reappointment etc. after 25.3.1984 has been held to be
null and void and vitiated by fraud. Thus, the prayer of the petitioner
for absorption of services of her husband has been rightly rejected by
the State Government in view of the specific observations with regard
to cessation of being an employee of the college either on 25.3.1984
or on the cut-off-date i.e. 1.6.1986 in paragraph no. 28 of the
judgment oin the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra).
 199. In view of the aforesaid reasons as also the reasons
given the similar case of Dr. Shyam Narayan Upadhya (C.W.J.C. No.
8545/2008) this writ application must fail and is accordingly,
dismissed.
Re.: C.W.J.C.No. 14980/2013
 200. The sole petitioner Rajendra Prasad Dubey has
assailed a separate order passed by the State Government dated
21.12.2010 rejecting his case for absorption on the post of Lecturer on

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 155
the ground that his appointment was not made as per norms of CCIM
and he did not possess the requisite qualification for the post of
Lecturer prescribed by the Bihar University.
 201. The case of the petitioner is that he was appointed
on the post of Professor (Pradhayapak) on 30.7.1973. This Court
however finding that the first appointment letter in fact does not
mention even department in which the petitioner was directly
appointed on the post of Professor. Surprisingly there is a second
appointment letter dated 29.8.1973 by which the petitioner was said to
have been appointed as In-charge Principal and in this appointment
letter the petitioner was shown to be a Professor in Nidan Bibhag.
There is a third appointment letter of the petitioner dated 13.3.1978 in
which as per resolution of the Governing Body he was shown to be
appointed as Lecturer in Nidan and Surgery Department and he was
asked to give his joining. The appointment letter dated 13.3.1978 also
mentions his appointment to be temporary and subject to approval of
the University. Surprisingly the confirmation of the petitioner as a
Lecturer by an order dated 25.2.2981 with effect from 13.3.1978 itself
was made in Rog Bibhag. The petitioner is also said to have been
promoted on the post of Reader in Rog Bibhag and Shalya by an order
dated 23.2.2982 with effect from 14.2.1982. The petitioner has also
enclosed an office order dated 7.1.1983 to show that the Governing

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 156
Body had transferred the petitioner on the post of Head of the
Department of Surgery (Shalya).
 202. According to the petitioner, he had superannuated
from the service of the College on 30.6.2002 and when his retirement
benefit was not paid he had moved this Court in C.W.J.C.No.
3718/2003, wherein the Government had taken a stand by filing a
counter affidavit that the services of the petitioner were not found fit
for absorption and as such, he was not entitled for any retirement
benefit. The Government thereafter had passed the common order of
termination dated 29.8.2003, wherein the services of the petitioner
were terminated. Both the writ applications C.W.J.C.No. 3718/2003,
naming retirement benefit by the petitioner and C.W.J.C.No.
10445/2005 were heard together and were disposed of by this Court
by a common judgment, wherein his claim for pecuniary benefits was
rejected and C.W.J.C.No. 3718/2003 was dismissed and his case for
absorption was remitted with the following observations and
directions:
 "…………the State Government is hereby simply directed
to get the case of the petitioner examined afresh by appropaite
committee or authority for consideration of his case for absorption
in the State Government service in accordance with law and in
terms of the provisions of the Act. it is needless to say that in case
the petitioner's services are ultimately found fit to be absorbed in
the State Government service, consequential notification shall be
issued with necessary orders for payment of all pecuniary benefits
to him in accordance with law. All the exercise in this respect shall
be completed by the respondents within three months from the date
of receipt/production of a copy of this order.
 In the result, C.W.J.C. No. 10445 of 2005 is disposed of in
terms of the observations and directions made hereinabove.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 157
 Since it has been held hereinabove that as long as the
petitioiner continued in the status of an ad hoc employee pending
screening of his case by the respondents in terms of section 6 of
the Act, he is not entitled to any pecuniary benefits on the basis of
his superannuation from service of the college, C.W.J.C. No. 3718
of 2003 fails and is hereby dismissed. "

 203. It is only thereafter that the State Government had
passed the impugned order. The State Government in order to comply
with the aforementioned directions in the case of the petitioner had
constituted a Committee and the Committee after detailed
consideration had found the petitioner not fit for absorption. Such
report of the Committee dated 24.10.2008 is reproduced hereinbelow:

 204. Thereafter the State Government on 21.12.2010 had
passed the impugned order, which reads as follows:

 205. In the considered opinion of this Court the
petitioner, who has already claimed to have retired from the College
in the year 1992 and who stands terminated in view of the order of the
State Government dated 29.8.2003 will not now require
reconsideration for his absorption only for the purposes of fixing
benefits, inasmuch as the petitioner as per his own showing in the
affidavit in the writ petition is above 71 years of age, his age being 69
years as on filing of the writ petition on 25.7.2013.
 206. This Court will have no difficulty in holding that the
whole claim of appointment of the petitioner initially on the post of
Professor in Nidan Department, his transfer to Shalya Department,

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 160
smacks of apparent forgery and illegality. First of all from five years
course of study prescribed by the Central Council of Indigenous
Medicine (CCIM) it would be found that there is no subject or
department known as Nidan Department, that is how the petitioner's
first appointment letter also does not mention any department when he
is said to be directly appointed as Professor on 13.7.1973. It is in the
second appointment letter of his becoming Acting Principal that he is
described as a Professor in Nidan department. Now there is no Nidan
department of the subject of Nidan. As such, in the prescribed five
years course as per course of study, there is no subject or department
of Nidan and to clarify this aspect this Court would extract the subjcts,
which are taught yearwise in an Ayurvedic College:
"Pre-Ayurvedic course
Subjects
1. Sanskrit
2. Prarambhik Padarth Vigyan
3. Udbhijjang Prratyang Vigyan
4. Prarambhik Rasashastra Parichaya
5. Ayurvedic Itihas and Ayurveda Parichaya
Main Ayurvedic course
Subjects
First Year
1. Padartha vigyan
2. Astang Sangraha
3. Sharirachana Vigyan
4. Sharirkriya Vigyan
5. Svasthavritta
Second year
1. Shartirschana Vigyan
2. Shartirakriya Vigyan
3. Svasthavritta
4. Drayyaguna Vigyan

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 161
5. Rasashaastra
6. Bhalshjyakalpana
7. Rogavigyanam & Vikritivigyan
Third Year
1. Dravyaguna Vigyan
2. Rasa Shastra
3. Bhaishajyakalpana
4. Roga Vigyan & Vikritivigyan
5. Agadatantra & Vyavaharayuveda
6. Prasutitantra & Striroga
Fourth year
1. Charaka Samhita
2. Prasutitantra & Stri Roga
3. Kaumarabhritya
4. Kayachikitsa
5. Shalayatantra
6. Shalakyatantra
Fifth year
1. Kayachikitsa
2. Shalakya tanta
3. Shalya tantra
4. Chraka Samhita
 207. It, thus, becomes clear that the story of appointment
in the year 1973 in Nidan Department is beyond the scope of course
of study in Ayurvedic College. In this background this Court finds
that in the appointment letter, which the petitioner got after five years
now for the post of Lecturer on 30.3.1978, his appointment was
shown to be as a Lecturer in Nidan where someone has added Surgery
with a sign of addition (+). The word Surgery has definitely been
written by someone else which gets confirmed even by the subsequent
appointment letter where he is being described as Lecturer in Rog
Vighyan and Surgery. Thus, there are sufficient materials to show that
the petitioner becoming Professor and then Lecturer having no

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 162
qualification for the post of Lecturer could not have been absorbed on
the post of Lecturer, inasmuch as under section 6 of the Act the
Government had to look into the initial appointment and his so called
claim of continuation as on the cut-off date on which day he was a
Professor in a non-existing Rog Vidhyan and Shalya.
 208. Be that as it may, this Court does not find any error
in consideration of the case of the petitioner by the Government which
had rejected separately the case of the petitioner for absorption while
complying the direction of this Court in his earlier aforementioned
writ petition.
 209. Thus, the case of petitioner also fails and is,
accordingly, dismissed.
Re.: C.W.J.C.No. 10802/2008
 210. In this case the prayer of the petitioner Purshottam
Dwivedi is absolutely different, inasmuch as he has been absorbed by
the order of the State Government on the post of Lecturer but then he
has prayed for the following relief:
"1. That by this petition the petitioner prays for consideration
of the case of the petitioner for absorption/ promotion to the
post of reader as well as Professor taking into account that
since the date of his absorption he is entitled for such
promotion under the government decision and the petitioner
also prays for all consequential benefits arising out of his due
promotion.
2. That the petitioner further prays for payment of salary from
29.3.2003 till 31.10.2007 to which the petitioner is entitled to
under the law and other relief/ reliefs for which the petitioner
is entitled."
211. The petitioner in this case claims to be appointed on

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 163
the post of Demonstrator on 12.11.1981 and promoted on the post of
Lecturer on 16.9.1983 as also on the post of Reader on 5.7.1985.
212. In the first Screening Committee report, he was
recommended for absorption on the post of Reader to the following
findings:-

213. Subsequently, the second Screening Committee did
not find him fit for absorption by recording as follows:-
