 214. In view of the above, when the services of the
petitioner were terminated on 29.8.2003 and the matter was remitted
back for reconsideration in view of the judgment dated 11.3.2005, the
petitioner’s case upon reconsideration for absorption was allowed by
absorbing on the post of Lecturer by the impugned order dated
17.10.2007.
 215. Learned counsel for the petitioner in support of the
aforesaid prayer had submitted that the petitioner's appointment on the
post of Demonstrator on 12.11.1981 and his promotion on the post of
Lecturer on 16.9.1983 having been now approved by the State
Government, he would qualify for promotion on the post of Reader
and Professor, specially when the Governing Body by the resolution
dated 5.7.1985 had already promoted him on the post of Reader and
therefore, on the cut-off date i.e. 1.6.1986 he was working as Reader.
He has also raised a dispute with regard to his date of birth, inasmuch
as according to him, his date of birth is 23.1.1949 but the Screening
Committee had recorded the date of birth as 31.1.1942.
 216. In the considered opinion of this Court the petitioner
actually does not possess the qualification for the post of Reader and
Professor, inasmuch as from the supplementary affidavit filed by him
it would be apparent that he passed his Prathama examination in

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 165
Sanskrit curriculum in 1961, Madhyama in 1967 and Shastri in 1969.
He is said to have passed course of Prachinacharya from Kameshwar
Singh Darbhanga Sanskrit University in 1971.
 217. Such course of Prachinacharya from Kameshwar
Singh Darbhanga Sanskrit University has not been recognized under
the Indian Medicine Central Council Act, 1970, which under 2nd
Schedule gives an exhaustive list of recognized medical Ayurvedic
Colleges granted by the University, Boards and other Institution of
India in terms of Section 14 of the aforesaid Act. As a matter of fact
for Kameshwar Singh Darbhanga Sanskrit University the course of
Prachinacharya has been taken to be equivalent to BAMS for the
period 1962 onwards, whereas the course of Ayurvedacharya has been
taken to be equivalent to Bachelor of Ayurvedic and Medicine
Surgery from 1981 onwards. The petitioner is definitely neither
Prachinacharya from Kameshwar Singh Darbhanga Sanskrit
University nor has completed the course of Ayurvedacharya and
therefore, this Court will find it difficult to hold him to have the
requisite qualification for the post of Reader and Professor.
 218. In this regard learned counsel for the petitioner
himself had referred to the communication of the University to the
CCIM and the Government of India, as contained in Annexures 26
and 27 dated 15.7.2000, 6.8.2000 and 28.8.2000, all of which

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 166
collectively go to show that in the 2nd Schedule of 1970 Act there
was a proposal for also including Prachinacharya as an equivalent
qualification but there is nothing on record to show that the Ministry
of Health and Family Welfare had ever made the amendment in the
Schedule. The petitioner in fact has also only annexed a draft
notification sent by the CCIM to the Government of India and
therefore, this Court will find it difficult to hold that the qualification
of the petitioner as Prachinacharya would qualify for his being even
absorbed on any teaching post much less for his being promoted on
the post of Reader and Professor.
 219. It must be noted here that the petitioner on the basis
of his date of birth on 31.1.1942 has already retired from the service
on 31.1.2009 and has been given his pension, gratuity and other
retirement benefit treating him to be Lecturer in view of the
Government order dated 17.10.2007. The petitioner in fact is fortunate
to be absorbed on the post of Lecturer but in no view of the matter this
Court can hold his promotion by the Governing Body on the post of
Reader under the resolution dated 5.7.1985 because all the resolutions
of the Governing Body after 24.3.1984 have been found to be vitiated
and not acceptable.
 220. It has also to be kept in mind that even if his
experience is taken into account from the date of his appointment as a

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 167
Demonstrator i.e. 12.11.1981 he did not complete the period of five
years of teaching experience as prescribed under the Statutes on the
date of his alleged promotion on 5.7.1985. As a matter of fact the
requirement of five years of teaching experience apart from being
Graduate in Ayurvedic Science is the prescribed minimum
qualification for the post of Reader and therefore, this Court must
hold that the petitioner should be satisfied with his absorption on the
post of Lecturer, inasmuch as the Government has already paid his
retirement benefit treating him to be absorbed on the post of Lecturer
and retired from service with effect from 31.1.2009.
 221. When such observations were made learned counsel
for the petitioner had sought permission to withdraw this application
in order to enable the petitioner to file his representation before the
State Government for the relief sought in this writ application.
 222. That being so, this writ application is permitted to
be withdrawn.
Re.: C.W.J.C.No. 6102/2003
 223. The prayer of the petitioner in this writ application
reads as follows:
" … to quash letter no. 321 (Deshi Chikitsa) dated 9.4.2003
whereby and whereunder the Deputy Secretary (respondent
no.4) has directed the Principal of the college to terminate the
services of the petitioner and other employees of the
Government Dhanwantari Ayurvedic College, Ahirauli at Buxar
(hereinafter referred to as the College) in view of the advise of
the Department of Finance and to quash the order dated
22.5.2003 vide memo no. 136 issued under the signature of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 168
Principal of College terminating the petitioners service."
 224. Mr. Ashok Kumar Singh, learned Senior counsel
appearing on behalf of the petitioner, in support of the aforementioned
prayer, had tried to make out a distinction between the other case and
this case by explaining that in other cases pursuant to the order passed
in the case of Dr Satyadeo Pandey (supra) a fresh decision has been
taken but in the case of the petitioner no such decision was taken
inasmuch as the writ application itself was filed in view of the earlier
order dated 09.04.2003 terminating the services of the petitioner. Mr.
Singh explains that the said order dated 09.04.2003 was not approved
in any manner in the batch of writ applications disposed of on
11.03.2005 in the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey & Ors. vs. the State of
Bihar & Ors. (supra) and as such the impugned order is fit to be
quashed on that ground alone.
 225. Mr. Singh has also submitted that the name of the
petitioner, in fact, is not mentioned in either of the category in the list
dated 29.08.2003 and, therefore a fresh consideration will be required
to be made at least in the case of the petitioner. According to Mr.
Singh, the petitioner does fulfil all the requisite qualification and also
other ingredients for holding of his appointment to be valid. In this
regard, he has submitted that the petitioner is graduate in Ayurveda
and his appointment in the college on 15.140.1981 was made on the
post of Demonstrator. Subsequently, on 01.06.1985, after the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 169
petitioner had completed three years of service as a Demonstrator and
also post qualification experience as an Ayurvedic graduate, his
promotion was made on the post of Lecturer by an order of promotion
dated 01.06.1985. He has also explains that in view of the first
Screening Committee the services of the petitioner was absorbed in
1991 and even the second Screening Committee in the year 1999 had
recommended for absorption of the petitioner.
 226. In this case, a counter affidavit has been filed
wherein it was sought to be explained that though the services of the
petitioner were screened by the Screening Committee but then
subsequently the Finance Department did not accord approval for
continuation of the teachers on ad hoc basis and as such when the
second Screening Committee had recommended only for absorption
of 22 persons whereas the services of the petitioner were sought to be
terminated by an order dated 09.04.2003 under the orders of the
Principal of the college primarily on the ground of there being no
Government sanction for continuation of the petitioner on the post of
Lecturer in the college.
 227. The petitioner, in fact, has filed reply to the counter
affidavit and has brought on record that after this writ application was
filed on 10.07.2003 and the operation of the impugned order dated
22.05.2003, so far it relates to termination of service of the petitioner,

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 170
was stayed, the petitioner has been continuing in service and, in fact,
has been transferred in the year 2009 to work as a Lecturer in the
Government Ayodhya Shivkumari Ayurved Mahavidyalaya,
Begusarai.
 228. On the strength of this notification dated
30.10.2009, Mr. Singh has, in fact, tried to canvas that the petitioner's
continuation in the college from 1981 till this date, i.e. for the period
of 34 years could not have been questioned much less disturbed
specially when he fulfils all the pre-requisite condition for absorption
of his services in terms of Section 6 of the Bihar Private Medical
(Indian System of Medicine) College (Taking Over) Act, 1985
(hereinafter referred to as "the Taking Over Act"). In this regard, Mr.
Singh has also led stress that alike the petitioner, the other 21
teachers, who were also appointed initially on the post of
Demonstrator, their services also have been absorbed on the post of
Lecturer and, therefore, there is no difference between the case of the
petitioner and the other 21 teachers who have been absorbed under the
decision taken pursuant to the directions of this Court in the case of
Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra).
 229. From the facts noted above, it becomes absolutely
clear that no decision with regard to termination of services of the
petitioner was taken either in the year 2003 pursuant to the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 171
finalization of the second Screening Committee report. In this regard,
this Court would find that the impugned order dated 09.04.2003, in
fact, was only by way of direction from the State Government to the
Principal of the college to take an appropriate action for terminating
the services of such persons whose cases were still pending for
consideration. The said order dated 09.04.2003 reads as follows:-

 230. The list enclosed therewith of as many as 229
persons was only by way of a direction by the State Government to
the Principal of the college to terminate the services of those persons
including the petitioner. As a matter of fact, the consequential order
dated 22.05.2003 passed by the Principal of the college directing 158
persons to hand over the charge of the post mentioned against their
names, was by way of an interim measure because the issue of
absorption, at that point of time, in terms of Section 6 of the Taking
Over Act, was still pending. This Court, in fact, would find that such
a decision was ultimately taken on 29.08.2003 and in the list though
there are names of 229 persons, the name of the petitioner is missing.
May be, that on account of the order of stay passed by this Court on
11.07.2003, the name of the petitioner was not included but then the
facts remain that the petitioner's services were never terminated after
the aforesaid order of the Principal Secretary which, as noted above,
was stayed by this Court.
 231. What still make the case of the petitioner different

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 173
and, in fact, fit for reconsideration by the State Government, would be
that there is no document brought by the respondents on record to
show that alike in the case of others, the Review Committee had ever
considered the case of the petitioner. As a matter of fact, this writ
application having remained pending for more than 11 years with an
interim order of stay, the respondents could have a justification in not
doing so, which could have amounted to interference in the interim
order.
 232. Thus, when this Court also finds that the order
passed by the Health Department, pursuant to the directions complied
in the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra), was issued and as many as
99 persons were found unfit for absorption, the name of the petitioner
also does not figure therein. What however is still tragic for the
petitioner is that neither he is in the list of terminated employees nor
he is in the list of retained employees because the list of retained
employees dated 17.10.2007 reverting names of 66 persons including
15 Lecturers but the case of the petitioners has not been considered
even that list.
 233. This Court is also mindful that there are available
vacant sanction post of teachers inasmuch as apart from approving the
services of 15 Lecturers in the order dated 17.10.2007, the Screening
Committee had approved the services of three more teachers namely

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 174
Dr. Purushottam Dwivedi, and Dr. Rajendra Prasad on the post of
Lecturer and Dr. Gopal Krishna Choube on the post of Ayurvedic
Medical Officer respectively. If the post sanctioned by the Prasashi
Padwarg Samiti in its backdrop is taken into account, it would be
found that there were 01 post of Principal, 06 of Professor, 14 of
Reader and 15 of Lecturer and therefore, the services of this petitioner
can be also absorbed on a teaching post.
 234. Thus, considering all these aspects, this Court would
direct that the case of the petitioner, namely, Shiv Shankar Rai for his
absorption should be considered by the Health Department and since
he had the requisite qualification for the post of Demonstrator at the
time of his initial appointment and his appointment on the post of
Lecturer also has been made in terms of the prescribed qualification of
having three years post qualification experience, this Court would not
find there to be any impediment in absorbing the services of the
petitioner Shiv Shankar Rai on a teaching post specially when he has
been working till date on the post of Lecturer in the Government
Ayodhya Shivkumari Ayurved Mahavidyalaya, Begusarai for more
than list six years.
 235. This Court hopes and believes that the case of the
petitioner shall be considered in accordance with law and till it is done
so, the interim order passed continuation of his service shall continue.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 175
 on
30.11.2015
In other words, the petitioner shall not be terminated from service till
a final decision is taken by the Government in terms of Section 6 (2)
and (3) of the Taking Over Act.
 236. With the aforementioned observation and
direction, this writ application is disposed of.
To be continued…………
237. Having thus dealt the cases of absorption of
teaching employees of the college, this Court would take the cases of
non-teaching employees with a clear caveat at the outset that in view
of judgment of the Apex Court in the case of L.N.Mishra Institute of
Economic Development (supra), the test will not be as redressed and
only three things will have to be found out;(i) as to whether they had
fulfilled the basic qualification ;(ii) as to whether they come within
the sanctioned strength of post created by CCIM and now by the
Prashashi Padvarg Samiti and (iii) as to whether their appointment
was vitiated by any fraud.
238. This test being applied by the Court is again in
keeping with the conscience of the observations and directions given
in paragraph 28 of the judgment of the Satyadeo Pandey(supra),
wherein direction was issued to State Government to decide the case
of absorption of the petitioners by examining into validity of
appointment either on the ground of fraud or appointment being void

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 176
ab initio for lac of basic academic qualification or into the facts
showing termination/secession of services as before the date
25.03.1984 or the cut off date etc.
Re.: C.W.J.C.No. 9727 of 2008
 239. In this case there are 15 petitioners and they claim
absorption on various non-teaching post. Petitioners no. 1 and 12,
namely, Binod Kumar Choubey and Yugal Kishore Pandey claim
their absorption on the post of Pharmacist while petitioner no.2 Smt.
Indu Kumari claims her absorption on the post of Nurse and petitioner
no.14 Dr. Sharda Pandey on the post of Nursing Sister. Petitioner
no.3 Arjun Pandey, Petitioner no.5 Avinash Kumar Sinha claim their
absorption on the post of Assistant Clerk/ Clerk while petitioner no.4
Arun Tiwary, Petitioner no.6 Ramashraya Singh, petitioner no.7
Dinanath Singh and petitioner no.13 Sri Bhagwan Tiwari claim their
absorption on the post of Clerk. Petitioner no.8 Vinod Kumar Gupta
claims his absorption on the post of Wardboy, whereas petitioner no.9
Dilip Kumar and petitioner no.10 Sri Rang Mishra as well as
petitioner no.15 Mohan Tiwari claim their absorption on the post of
Nightguard. Petitioner no.11 Lakheshri Devi claims absorption of her
husband Sheoji Yadav on the post of Cook.
 240. The petitioner Binod Kumar Choubey claims to be
working as a Pharmacist in the College when it was taken-over on

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 177
9.12.1986 and in fact in paragraph no.7 in the chart the post of
absorption claimed by him is that of the Pharmacist. The case of the
petitioner Binod Kumar Choubey is that he was appointed initially on
the post of Wardboy in the College on 24.2.1983 and subsequently, he
was promoted by the Governing Body on the post of Pharmacist on
31.1.1986. The qualification of Binod Kumar Choubey as per his own
showing is only Intermediate. He also claims that his promotion was
made on account of his having some experience in the field of
Pharmacy for the post of Pharmacist which is technically specialized
post. One has to necessarily the qualification of either degree or
diploma in Pharmacy. In that view of the matter, this Court would not
find the petitioner Binod Kumar Choubey fit for being absorbed on
the post of Pharmacist.
 241. Learned counsel for the petitioner, however, has
cited an example of Bipin Bihari Mishra who is said to have absorbed
on the post of Pharmacist despite his qualification also being of
simple Graduate and not backed by any technical qualification in the
field of Pharmacy. As a matter of fact this clear averment made by the
petitioner in paragraph no. 20 of the writ petition has not been
controverted by the respondents in the counter affidavit.
 242. Having thus regard to this aspect this Court would
deem it expedient in the ends of justice to direct the respondents to

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 178
consider the case of the petitioner for absorption on the post of
Wardboy, inasmuch as such appointment though made initially by the
Secretary to the Governing Body was approved by the Governing
body. Let it be kept in mind that Bipin Bihari Mishra has already died
in the year 2009 and his precedent cannot be now made a basis for
absorption of the petitioner on the post of Pharmacist but then having
regard to the scope of Section 6(4) of the Take-over Act, as discussed
earlier, this Court would find that there is a sanctioned available post
of Wardboy and the case of the petitioner Binod Kumar Choubey is fit
to be considered at least against the post of Wardboy.
 243. For doing so, this Court is also mindful of the fact
that the reason given in the impugned order of the Review Committee
for not absorbing the petitioner Binod Kumar Choubey is one and
only one that his initial appointment made by the Secretary was not
approved by the Managing Committee but now the fact has been
discovered from Resolution No.4 of the Governing Body dated
29.3.1983 that his initial appointment on the post of Wardboy made
earlier by the Secretary was approved by the Governing Body. Thus,
even if promotion of the petitioner Binod Kumar Choubey on the post
of Pharmacist due to lack of qualification is held to be bad, he in view
of his admitted continuance and working in the College till 2003
would be entitled for consideration of his case for absorption on the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 179
post of Wardboy, a Class IV post.
 244. Petitioner no.12 in C.W.J.C.No. 9727/2008, namely,
Sri Yugal Kishore Pandey claims to be appointed on 30.3.1982 on the
post of Pharmacist and subsequently promoted on the post of
Demonstrator on 14.9.1985.
 245. The qualification of the petitioner Yugal Kishore
Pandey is only GAMS i.e. degree in Ayurvedic medicine. It has to be,
however, taken into consideration that on the date of appointment i.e.
on 30.3.1982 the petitioner Yugal Kishore Pandey was not having
qualification of GAMS because he had passed such examination only
subsequently some time in the year 1983 in view of his result declared
of an examination held in the year 1981. Nonetheless the fact remains
that as on the date of his initial appointment he did not possess any
qualification for the post of Pharmacist. Subsequently his promotion
was made on the post of Demonstrator. The post of Demonstrator as
per Statutes had required the qualification of degree in Ayurveda from
a recognized University or the Board/ Council established by the State
or the Central Government and recognized by the University except
that in Science subjects i.e. Physics, Chemistry and Biology. The
minimum qualification prescribed was 2nd Class Masters degree of
recognized University in the subject concerned.
 246. The question, therefore, would be as to whether the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 180
initial appointment of the petitioner Yugal Kishora Pandey on the post
of Pharmacist without having any qualification as on 30.3.1982 much
less degree or diploma in Pharmacy or his subsequent promotion on
14.9.1985/ 12.10.1985 on the post of Demonstrator has to be made the
basis for deciding his claim of absorption on the post of
Demonstrator.
 247. Learned counsel for the petitioner has laid stress that
if the respondents could absorb Bipin Bihari Mishra on the post of
Pharmasist despite he also not having any requisite qualification for
the post of Pharmasist and in fact being a simple Graduate if he could
be ultimately absorbed on the post of Pharmacist, the petitioner Yugal
Kishore Pandey ought to have been absorbed at least on the post of
Pharmacist.
 248. There would be no difficulty in appreciating the
aforesaid submission but then what would straightway come in the
way of the petitioner Yugal Kishore Pandey to be equated with the
case of Bipin Bihari Mishra is that as on the date of take-over i.e.
9.10.1986 he was not working as a Pharmacist. He in fact claims to be
working on the post of Demonstrator by virtue of his order of
promotion. As noted above, the petitioner claims to have passed
GAMS examination and if such qualification can be held to be one
which had been prescribed under the Statutes he becomes entitled for

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 181
consideration of his case for absorption on the post of Demonstrator.
In the sanctioned of post that was made by Prashashi Padwar Samiti
there is not sanctioned post of Demonstrator and as such, his
absorption on the post of Demonstrator is not possible. It is here that
the State Government will have to invoke its power under section 6(4)
of the Act which empowers it to absorb on any other post.
 249. Considering the fact that the petitioner Yugal
Kishore Pandey is having degree of GAMS and that in such
prescribed course of study of curriculum of GAMS there is also a
paper of Pharmacy, he cannot be said to be totally disqualified at least
for holding the post of Pharmacist. As a matter of fact there are six
sanctioned post of Pharmacist in the decision taken by the Prashashi
Prawar Samiti and therefore, this Court would direct the respondents
to consider the case of the petitioner Yugal Kishore Pandey on the
post of Pharmasist by also keeping in mind the precedent of Bipin
Bihari Mishra.
 250. The petitioner no.14, Dr. Sharda Pandey has come
out with a claim of absorption on the post of either Lecturer or in
alternative on the post of Nursing Sister. Learned counsel for the
petitioner no.14 explains that it is true that on 20.2.1982 when the
petitioner Dr. Sharda Pandey got appointed on the post of Nursing
Sister under the orders of the Secretary to the Governing Body, she

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 182
did not possess the prescribed qualification for the post of Nursing
Sister i.e. diploma in Nursing under Bihar Nurses Registration
Council, but then she ultimately had her GAMS i.e. degree in
Ayurvedic Medicine in 1983 and was ultimately promoted from the
post of Nursing Sister to Demonstrator by the Governing Body on
11.9.1983 and thereafter promoted on the post of Lecturer on
31.12.1985.
 251. In the considered opinion of this Court, Dr. Sharda
Pandey, the petitioner no.14, cannot establish her claim of being
Lecturer on the date of taking over i.e. 9.12.1986 giving it
retrospective effect on 1.6.1986. The reason for the same will be that
this Court has already in the judgment of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra)
had fixed a cut off date of 25.3.1984 whereafter the decision of the
Governing Body has not been recognized and, therefore, if the
petitioner claims becoming a Lecturer by some decision of the
Governing Body dated 31.12.1985, that by itself will be a major
impediment for absorption of the petitioner Dr. Sharda Pandey on the
post of Lecturer. That in fact, will be the reason in not allowing the
absorption of Dr. Sharda Pandey on the post of Lecturer because there
is a requirement under the Statute framed by the Bihar University
laying down the post, qualification and experience of three before
becoming eligible for appointment on the post of Lecturer and

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 183
therefore, if the petitioner had passed GAMS examination in the year
1983 and at that point of time she was working as a Nursing Sister
followed by alleged resolution of the Governing Body of promotion
from Nursing Sister to the Demonstrator, this all will only lead to only
one and one conclusion that Dr. Sharda Pandey will not be entitled for
absorption on the post of Lecturer in the College.
 252. The alternative submission that at least she will be
absorbed on the post of which her initial appointment was made i.e.
post of Nursing Sister made on 20.2.1982 because there is at least one
post of Mid Wifery in the case of GAMS completed by the petitioner
in the year 1983, will also be of no avail. For the post of Nursing
Sister, there is a requirement of Diploma in Nursing or Diploma of
Nursing Attendant include one paper of Mid Wifery that apart for
holding of Nurse one has to be essentially registered with Bihar
Nursing Registration Council.
 253. As with regard to the case of petitioner no.2 Smt.
Indu Kumari and petitioner no.14 Dr. Sharda Pandey claiming to be
appointed on the post of Nurse/ Nursing Sister, this Court would find
that petitioner no.2 has no qualification for the post of Nurse.
According to her own case she was appointed on the post of Nurse on
23.3.1982. Her letter of appointment, however, is not on record and
her appointment has been made also by the Secretary, who under the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 184
Statutes has been vested with no power of making appointment on any
non-teaching post. To top it all, her so called confirmation of
appointment by the Governing Body is admittedly after cut-off date
under the judgment of this Court i.e. 26.3.1984. Thus, no sanctity can
be given even on the process of appointment, inasmuch as her
appointment was not made by the competent authority. Finally, this
Court would find that petitioner no.2 also does not possess requisite
qualification of Nurse for which not only passing of Nurses course
examination is compulsory but also registration by the Bihar Nurses
Registration Council is sine qua non. Thus, this Court will not find
any difficulty in rejecting the case of claim of absorption of petitioner
no.2 Smt. Indu Kumari.
 254. Reverting back to the case of petitioner no.14 Dr.
Sharda Pandey, this Court would find that her appointment was
allegedly made by the Secretary on 20.2.1982 on the post of Nursing
Sister. On the date of her appointment she was simply Matriculate and
therefore, when she alike Smt. Indu Kumari, petitioner no.2, did not
possess the requisite qualification for the post of Nurse/ Nursing
Sister, her alleged approval of appointment by the Governing Body on
11.9.1983 can also hardly make any difference. It is quite strange that
the petitioner no.14 also claims to have been promoted on the post of
Female Medical Officer in the Department of Gynecology and is said

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 185
to have submitted her joining on 14.7.1983 in view of an order of the
Secretary of the College dated 14.7.1983 promoting petitioner no.14
on the post of Female Medical Officer cum Demonstrator. The claim
of petitioner no.14 is that she had passed her GAMS examination in
the month of May, 1983 and therefore, she was promoted on the post
of Demonstrator, which is also not permitted under Chapter XVI-B of
the Statutes where the post of Demonstrator is meant only for direct
recruitment. The plea of her promotion, therefore, also does not find
any merit and in any event her claim that eventually she had got
promoted by the resolution of the Governing Body on 31.12.1985 on
the post of Lecturer will have no sanctity in the eye of law because
she did not possess the requisite qualification of three years teaching
experience prescribed for the post of Lecturer under the Statutes. This
Court, therefore, will find it difficult to issue any direction for
absorption of petitioner no.14.
 255. As with regard to the case of petitioners no. 3, 4, 5,
6, 7 and 13 claiming absorption on the post of Clerk, their absorption
has been refused by the Review Committee primarily on two grounds.
Firstly, that there was no post ever known as the post of Assistant
Clerk or that such appointment of the aforesaid petitioners was
approved by the Governing Body on or before 25.3.1984 being the cut
off date fixed by this Court in the earlier judgment in the case of Dr.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 186
Satyadeo Pandey (supra). Additionally, one more reason was also
given that these petitioners did not have the requisite qualification of
competence in typing.
 256. There are two aspects of the matter which would be
immediately gone into by this Court while considering the case of
absorption on the post of Clerk/ Assistant Clerk/ Typist. As per the
CCIM norms, there is no post of Assistant Clerk. In the CCIM norms,
there is also no post of Typist and yet the Secretary of the College had
made indiscriminate appointment on the post of Clerk/ Assistant
Clerk/ Typist. Secondly, the college was a medical college and not a
Secretariat where any number of Clerks/ Typists could have been
appointed. For a College, like the petitioners which had admittedly the
intake capacity of 50 students and/or having hundred beds hospital.
 257. For both, under the CCIM norms as also under the
order of the State Government, there is availability of 01 post of Head
Clerk and 08 posts of Clerks. Out of 06 posts, one post for the Head
Clerk and five posts for the Clerk has already been filled up by way of
absorption, leaving only three more posts of Clerk on which
absorption can be made.
 258. This Court, in absence of the pleading in particular
with regard to the fulfillment of the qualification of typing, is not in a
position to decide as to who would qualify for absorption, but then as

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 187
one of the reasons in the case of most of the petitioners, non-approval
of their appointment by the Governing Body has been found to be
incorrect by this Court, the interest of justice would be better served
by directing the respondents to consider the case of all the aforesaid
petitioners allegedly appointed and working on the post of Clerk/
Assistant Clerk/ Typist and if it is found that they were fulfilling both
the educational qualification and the typing qualification, three senior
most of them shall be absorbed to fill up the slot of the unfilled three
post of Clerk as sanctioned by the Committee.
 259. Learned counsel for the petitioners, in fact, have
also pointed out that three, out of six absorbed Head clerk/ Clerks
namely Shree Ram Niwas Upadhyay, Sudhir Kumar Pathak and Ram
Sagar Rai have already retired from service which would give scope
for absorption of three more persons. This Court would find it
difficult to accept such submission for a simple reason that absorption
of anyone, if allowed, would date back to the date of taking over i.e.
9.12.1986 and, therefore, the resultant vacancy which now have
emerged after the retirement of the absorbed employees of the
College, therefore, cannot be used for the purpose of absorption
inasmuch as that will not give any benefit for counting of past service
in absence of vacant sanctioned post.
 260. Thus, as with regard to the consideration of the case

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 188
of Clerks, only three slots at best can be filled up by way of
absorption by considering the three senior most eligible persons
fulfilling the educational qualification and the knowledge of typing
supported by the certificate of typing.
 261. For rest of the petitioners, namely, petitioners no. 8,
9, 10, 11 and 15, this Court would find that the post of Wardboy and
Nightguard are the post of the hospital for which though there is a
sanctioned post under the norms of CCIM also created by the
Prashashi Padbarg Samiti but no individual consideration has been
made in the cases of these Class IV employees. The Review
Committee in fact has rejected their cases on the ground that their
appointments were not approved by the Governing Body. As per the
Statutes the appointment of the Wardboy, Nightguard etc. being
inferior post the power of appointment is vested in the Principal of the
College and therefore, much cannot be said about the illegality in the
appointment of petitioners no. 8, 9, 10,11 and 15. More-over, as noted
above, there is no consideration at all by the Review Committee much
less by the State Government as with regard to these petitioners
holding Class IV post from the post creation order by Prashashi
Padbard Samiti. It would be found that a large number of Class IV
post were sanctioned, therefore, treating those post to be available for
consideration of the case of petitioners no. 8, 9, 10 and 15, their cases

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 189
should be considered against the post of Wardboy, Nightguard or
some other equivalent post. As with regard to the case of petitioner
no.11, who has already died, there would be no question of now fresh
consideration.
 262. With the aforementioned observations and
directions, this writ application, concerning 15 petitioners, in the
manner indicated above, is disposed of.
Re.: C.W.J.C. No. 7246 of 2008
263. In this case, there are three petitioners who have
claimed their absorption. Petitioner No. 1, namely Radheshyam
Tiwari claims to have been appointed on the post of Assistant Clerk
on 01.02.1980 on the basis of his qualification of Praveshika,
petitioner no. 2, namely, Krishna Kant Mishra, again is said to have
been appointed on the post of Assistant Clerk with similar
qualification of Praveshika and the petitioner no. 3, namely, Bipin
Bihari having qualification of Intermediate, is said to have been
appointed on 12.11.1981 on the post of Typist. It is the case of the
petitioners that their aforesaid appointment made by the Secretary to
the Governing Body of the College was later on confirmed by the
Governing Body in its Resolution No. 5 in the meeting held on
23.04.1981 and by resolution No. 4 dated 14.02.1982 in case of
petitioner no. 3. The petitioners, however, after the take over of the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 190
college, were terminated from service on 27.8.1999, in view of report
of the Screening Committee that there was no sanctioned post of
Assistant Clerk/Typist under CCIM Norms and their appointments
were not made after following the prescribed procedure. The
petitioners had assailed the aforesaid order of their termination dated
27.08.2003 and their case was also disposed of by this Court by the
common judgment in the case of Satyadeo Pandey(supra). Thereafter,
the Review Committee had considered the case of all the Clerks
including the petitioner nos. 1 and 2, in all 30 of them and had
recorded as follows:
 fyfid laoxZ esa dqy 30 dehZ ekuuh; U;k;ky; ds
