mudh fu;qfDr dk lEiqf"V 'kklh fudk; }kjk ugha fd;s tkus ds
dkj.k v;ksX; ekuk x;kA
264. Learned counsel for the petitioners had referred to
a number of documents to suggest that the post of Assistant Clerk
though was not sanctioned under the CCIM norms but then there were
provisions for the post of Clerk and if the Governing Body had
appointed them on an inferior post of Assistant Clerk on which they
had continued till the date of take over of the college, they cannot be
refused absorption due to non-availability of sanctioned post of
Assistant Clerk. Similarly, in respect of petitioner no. 3, it was

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 191
considered that there is no sanctioned post of Typist in any particular
department of the college, but then it was said that there were scope of
Typist in the office of the Principal of the college.
265. This Court, on careful perusal of the norms laid
down by CCIM either of the year 1986 or 2007, has found that there is
no provision for Assistant Clerk or Clerk and there was only a
provision for Typist. As per 1986 CCIM norms, there were only three
posts of Clerks and three posts of Assistant and the Prashashi Padvarg
Samiti also had created in all 08 sanctioned posts of Clerks.
266. As noted above, Prashashi Padvarg Samiti was
guided by CCIM norms keeping in view of the strength of the
students being 50 in one academic session and 100 bedded wards for
which it had created one post of Head Clerk and 08 posts of Typist-cum-Clerk/Clerk. Out of these 09 posts, 06 persons have already been
absorbed, namely, (1) Uday Narain Tiwari, (2) Shesh Nath Rai, (3)
Kamalakant Mishra, (4) Ram Nivas Upadhyay, (5) Sudhir Kumar
Pathak and (6) Ram Sagar Rai. Thus, there are only three vacancies
left on the post of Clerk-cum-Typist or Clerk and there are 30
claimants including the petitioners.
267. This Court, therefore, can, at best, direct the State
Government to examine as to whether the petitioners qualification of
Preveshika/Intermediate was the sufficient qualification for the post of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 192
Clerk-cum-Typist/Clerk and thereafter prepare a list of eligible clerks
to fill up the two remaining vacancies strictly in accordance with
seniority as was also directed by this Court in the case of Satyadeo
Pandey (supra) wherein it was held that if the number of post is less
than the number of employees claiming absorption, the Government
will have to adopt the basis of length of service of the eligible
employees or such similar relevant consideration keeping in view the
precedent of the two earlier taken over Ayurvedic Colleges.
268. As a matter of fact, the case of the petitioners
would require reconsideration because one of the reason mentioned in
the impugned order, apart from the absence of sanctioned post and the
lack of knowledge of typing, is absence of approval of appointment of
the petitioners by the Governing Body but then as this Court has been
able to get the copy of the proceedings of the Governing Body from
which it is established that their appointment made by the Secretary,
were later on approved by the Governing Body in the meeting held on
23.04.1981 in case of petitioner nos. 1 and 2 and on 14.02.1982 in
case of petitioner no. 3, their cases need to be reconsidered for their
absorption.
269. Thus, this writ petition is also disposed of in terms
of the obervations and directions given in paragraphs no. 255 to 260
in CWJC No. 9727 of 2008.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 193
270. The aforesaid direction given for the petitoners of
CWJC No. 9727 of 2008 and CWJC No. 7246 of 2008 shall also
govern the cases of following persons claiming absorption on the post
of clerks-cum-typists, namely:-
Sl.
No.
Case No. Name of petitioners
1. CWJC No. 7998/ 08 1. Nagendra Nath Pandey
2. Biplavi Kumar Upadhyaya
3. Raghubansh Pandey
2. CWJC No. 7360/08 1. Rambachan Tiwari
2. Sri Narayan Mishra
3. Birendra Pandey
3. CWJC No. 8799/08 Kanhaiya Chaubey
4. CWJC No. 9800/08 Shree Niwas Upadhyay
5. CWJC No. 9727/08 1. Arjun Pandey
2. Avinash Kumar Sinha
3. Arun Tiwari
4. Ramashraya Singh
5. Dinanath Singh
6. Shri Bhagwan Tiwari
6. CWJC No. 11003/08 Usha Devi, w/o Late Balmiki Pandey
7. CWJC No. 12366/ 08 Mostt. Raj Kishore Devi W/o late
Ravindra Kumar Pandey

Re.:C.W.J.C.No. 10354 of 2008
 271. In this case the sole petitioner Vidyavati Pathak
claims that on the basis of her qualification of only Madhyama, she
was appointed on 12.2.1982 by the Secretary to the Governing Body
of the College on the post of Nursing Sister and that though she does
not have qualification prescribed for the post of Nursing Sister, but
then she can be absorbed on the post of Dai (maid).
 272. The present case in fact will be one of the worst
cases of illegal appointment being made by the Secretary of the
College. For the post of Nursing in a medical college, the minimum

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 194
requirement would be the diploma in Nursing followed by registration
before Bihar Nursing Registration Council. Thus, the petitioner who
had only a qualification of Madhyama and thus having some an idea
of the subject of Sanskrit could not have in any view of the matter
become a Nurse in Medical College Hosiptal. It is this aspect of the
matter, which has all along been considered to be the impediment for
absorption of the petitioner and thus, this Court does not find any
error in refusing absorption to the petitioner namely Vidyavati Pathak.
 273. The submission of Mr. Shivendra Kishore, learned
Senior counsel appearing on behalf of the petitioner, that since in
other cases, people have been sought to be absorbed on equivalent
post, the case of the petitioner should also be considered for her
absorption on the post of Dai. , if allowed, it will ultimately mean that
a person if he/she cannot be absorbed on the post of Professor, he at
least can be absorbed on the post of Peon. That however is not the
concept of absorption under the Taking Over Act. The primal
consideration of the Government for deciding the case of absorption
would be as to on what basis the person concerned was holding the
post on the cut off date i.e. 30.6.1986 and whether for holding such
post his or her appointment was made as per prescribed procedure and
also having the requisite qualification.
 274. Applying the aforesaid test, this Court will have no

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 195
difficulty in rejecting even that part of the submission of Mr.
Shivendra Kishore that the case of the petitioner namely Vidyavati
Pathak should be considered for absorption on the post of Dai (Maid).
In the result, this writ application is dismissed.
 Re.: C.W.J.C.No. 7998 of 2008
 275. In this case there are five petitioners. Petitioner no.1
Nagendra Nath Pandey, petitioner no.2 Biplavi Kumar Upadhayay
and petitioner no.4 Raghubansh Pandey claim their absorption on the
post of Clerk, whereas petitioner no.3 Anil Kumar Mishra claims his
absorption on the post of Librarian and petitioner no.5 on the post of
Peon. For the post of Clerk that was allegedly held by petitioner no.1,
he claims to have been appointed on 1.4.1979 and his qualification is
Bachelor in Arts. Petitioner no.2 claims to have been appointed on the
post of Assistant Clerk and his qualification is only Praveshika alike
petitioner no.4 Raghubansh Pandey and their dates of appointment are
30.7.1979 and 19.8.1980. First of all their appointments were made by
the Secretary, who had no power under the Statutes to make such
appointment and secondly, the Review Committee has found that
there being no post of Assistant Clerks under the CCIM norms and
they had no knowledge or experience of typing as well as their
appointments were never approved by the Governing Body, they
would not qualify for absorption on the post of Clerk.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 196
 276. Learned counsel for the petitioners has submitted
that since the Matriculation was the qualification for the post of Clerk
in Bihar University and even under Rule 148 of the Bihar
Miscellaneous Rules, typing was not a compulsory requirement but
only the optional requirement, the reasoning given by the Review
Committee as accepted by the State Government for refusing
absorption of petitioners no. 1, 2 and 4 cannot be countenanced in
law. This Court has already indicated while dealing with similar case
of absorption of Clerk in C.W.J.C.No. 9727/2008 that there are only
three vacancies left for the post of Clerk as per sanctioned post by
Prashashi Padbarg Samiti and therefore, the cases of these three
petitioners will also have to be considered for finding out 2, 3 among
all Clerks can be absorbed for the reasons, which has already been
discussed above, in C.W.J.C.No. 9727/2008.
 277. As with regard to the case of petitioner no.5, who
claims to be appointed on the post of Peon, his case also has to be
considered with Class IV employees of C.W.J.C.No. 9727/2008
keeping in view that only ten posts of Peon were sanctioned by the
Prashashi Padbarg Samiti and all ten posts of Peon have already been
filled up by way of absorption by the order dated 17.10.2007. As a
matter of fact the Review Committee had considered 50 Class IV
employees and had absorbed 31 of them including 10 approved on the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 197
post of Peon and for remaining 19 of them the only reason given was
that the appointment was made by the Secretary prior to 25.3.1984
and not approved by the Governing Body. No individual
consideration having been made either in the case of petitioner no.5 of
this case or other Class IV employees since there are 19 posts of Class
IV employees, the case of petitioner no.5 is also fit to be considered
for absorption alike the case of petitioner no.8, 9, 10 and 15 of
C.W.J.C.No. 9727/2008.
 278. Reverting back to the case of petitioner no.3 Anil
Kumar Mishra there would be no difficulty in rejecting his case for
absorption for the simple ground that he claims to have been
appointed on the post of Librarian on the basis of his qualification of
Praveshika on 3.12.1979. It is true that subsequently he claims to have
become a graduate but he does not possess any qualification of
Library Science. Under the Statutes of Bihar University Chapter XV-B there is a qualification laid down for College Libararian prescribing
Board academic record with at least high 2nd Class Misters degree in
the subject, otherwise Library Science or 1st Class or High 2nd Class
Masters degree in Library Science. In that view of the matter, this
Court will not find any merit in the submission of the learned counsel
for the petitioners that a mere Matriculate or even a graduate after
some time of his appointment could be absorbed on the post of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 198
Librarian. Library of a Medical College cannot be manned by a
simple matriculate as petitioner no.3 was at the time of his
appointment and therefore, this Court will find no difficulty in holding
that petitioner no.3 of this case is not fit to be absorbed on the post of
Librarian.
 279. This Court, however, would fail in its duty if it does
not notice to one ancillary submission that the State Government letter
dated 8.5.1985 prescribing pay scale for the post of Librarian will give
a reprive to the petitioner because there is a provision for a person to
be given pay scale of Libararian even if he does not have the
qualification of Library Science. That Government decision with
regard to earlier agreement in no view of the matter can be said to be
qualification. In fact it is complete lack of qualification on the part of
petitioner no.3 to hold the post of Libarian at any point of time, that
will come in his way in claiming absorption on the post of Librarian.
280. With the aforesaid observation and directions, this
writ application is disposed of.
Re.: C.W.J.C.No. 7429 of 2008
 281. In this case the sole petitioner Shiv Narayan Thakur
has claimed absorption on the post of Library Assistant. According to
the petitioner, he was appointed on 14.7.1981 by the Governing Body
of the college on the post of Library Assistant. The very faint letter of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 199
appointment however will go to show that such decision was never
taken by the Governing Body of the college rather it was a decision
taken by the Secretary to the Governing Body.
 282. This Court has also gone into the staffing pattern
and has found that there is no post of Library Assistant and in fact for
Library, there is only one post of Librarian. Learned counsel for the
petitioner has submitted that if there be no post for Library Assistant,
the respondent ought to have not considered him for the post of
Libarary Assistant inasmuch as there will be a world of difference
between Libararian and Library Assistant. A Libararian who has to
possess specialized qualification of degree or diploma in Library
Science whereas a Library Assistant as today only required a clerical
work.
 283. The difficulty for this Court, however, would be that
if there is no sanctioned post of Library Assistant, this Court would
find it difficult to direct the respondent for absorbing the services of
the petitioner either on a non-existing post and on a non-sanctioned
post of Libarary Assistant or on an inferior Class IV post of Library
Attendant. As a matter of fact there is also no separate sanctioned post
of Libarary Attendant and it is only for the working strength of Class
IV employees though services of Peon has to be utilized for any
menial to be done in the Libarary.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 200
 284. Learned counsel for the petitioner in such a situation
had sought to assail the decision taken by the Review Committee
where the case of the petitioner was considered only against the post
of Assistant Librarian. Learned counsel for the petitioner explains
applying the ratio laid down by the Apex Court in the case of
Mahendra Singh Gill vs. Union of India & ors., reported in AIR 1978
SC 851, the respondent should not be allowed to take any further
defence beyond what was considered by the Review Committee.
 285. This Court would find it difficult to make the law of
Mahendra Singh Gill's case (supra) applicable to the present case.
Here the question is that whether a person can be absorbed under
section 6 of the Taking Over Act and for that there has to be firstly
availability of a sanctioned post. The Governing body may appoint
any number of persons without following or adhering the staffing
pattern but then the statute of taking over gives power to the
Government to take over services only of such employees who are
within the prescribed sanctioned strength. The Government in its case
has already considered with regard to requirement of staffs in a
Library of the Ayurvedic College and had sanctioned only one post
namely the post of Librarian and the petitioner does not fulfil the
qualification of the post of Librarian.
286. In that view of the matter, this Court would find it

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 201
difficult to direct for absorption of services of the petitioner Shiv
Narain Thakur. This writ application is, accordingly, dismissed.
Re.: C.W.J.C.No. 7360 of 2008
 287. In this case petitioners no. 1, 6, 7 and 8 claim
absorption on the post of Museum keeper while petitioners no. 2, 3
and 10 claim absorption on the post of Typist and Clerk/ Clerk,
whereas petitioner no.4 claims absorption on the post of Peon and
petitioners no. 5 and 9 on the post of Compounder.
 288. Though the post of Museum keeper is not included
in the sanctioned post of 191 post of Prashashi Padbarg Samiti,
learned counsel for the petitioners has tried to find out from the CCIM
norms that there was a provision of Museum keeper for each of the
Department but then in absence of any qualification laid down for the
post of Museum keeper it will be difficult for this Court to hold as to
whether it was a Class III or Class IV post. None-the-less as the
College had its hospital for 50 bed for teaching 100 students and
under CCIM norms there was a provison for Musiumkeeper, this
Court would direct the State Government to consider the case of
petitioners no. 1, 6, 7 and 8 for their absorption provided their
appointment was made in accordance the Statutes and they had the
requisite qualification.
 289. As with regard to petitioners no. 5 and 9 claiming

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 202
absorption for the post of Compounder, this Court would find that
their cases have not at all been considered while considering the case
of Bipin Bihari Mishra and Ravindra Kumar by the Review
Committee wherein all that was recorded is as follows:
 feJd laoxZ esa Jh fofiu fcgkjh feJ] feJd dks iwoZ dh
Ldzhuhx lfefr dh vuq'kalk ds vkyksd esa iwoZ esa gh ljdkjh lsok esa
lek;ksftr fd;k tk pqdk gSA 'ks"k 4 dfeZ;kas esa Jh jfoUnz pkscs dks
ftudh fu;qfDr ckMZ ckW; ds in ij fnukad 15-02-1982 dks dh xb Z
vkSj mDr in ij budh fu;qfDr dk 'kklh fudk; }kjk lEiqf"V dh
x;h vkSj ftUgs a ekuuh; U;k;ky; }kjk fu/kkZfjr dV&vkWQ&MsV ds
Mzslj ,oa feJd in ij izksUufr nh x;h mls u ekurs gq, Jh pkScs dks
okMZ ckW; ds in ij lek;kstu dh vuq'k alk leh{kk lfefr }kjk dh
tkuh gSA
 290. There is also nothing on record to show that what
was the qualification for the post of Compounder. Thus, the
appointment of the petitioners no. 5 and 9 firstly have to be examined
as to whether they had the requisite qualification for the post of
Compounder and if it is found that they had such qualification and
their appointment was made by the competent authority, namely,
Governing Body which alone had the power to make appointment on
Class III post to exclusion of inferior service for which the Principal
alone had the power of appointment, their cases would receive fresh
consideration on the same basis as that of Bipin Bihari Mishra.
 291. As with regard to petitioner no. 4 his case being
similar to petitioner no.5 of C.W.J.C.No. 7998/2009, for the post of
Peon the same consideration will have to be made by the
Government. In other words, for 19 vacant Class IV post the cases of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 203
this petitioner no. 4 and other Class IV employees whether Peon or
Nightguard or Wardboy etc. should be clubbed together and best
among them as per the date of their appointment and qualification
should be taken in the same manner as 31 of them have been
absorbed under the order dated 17.10.2007 against those 50 post of
Class IV employees created under the decision of the Prashashi
Padbarg Samiti.
 292. With the aforesaid observation and direction, this
writ application is disposed of.
 Re. C.W.J.C.No. 8799 of 2008
 293. In this case the sole petitioner has claimed
absorption on the post of Office Superintendent.
 294. According to this petitioner, he was appointed on
the post of Clerk on 31.7.1977 under the order of the Secretary. His
appointment letter says that he was appointed under the resolution of
the Governing Body dated 31.7.1977 on the post of Clerk in the
accounts work.
 295. This Court has, however, found from the proceeding
book, which has been produced by the Vigilance Department, that
such proceedings are only with effect from 17.6.1979. There is
nothing to show that the appointment of the petitioner was made
under the approval of the Governing Body of the College and

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 204
therefore, his story of being promoted on the post of Head clerk and
thereafter on the post of Office Superintendent is shrouded in mystery.
 296. More-over against the sanctioned post of Head
Clerk and five other Clerks under the post creation order of Prashashi
Padbarg Samiti all the six posts have been filled up and one Ram
Niwas Upadhyay has been absorbed as Head clerk. Thus, the
petitioner, who is only having qualification of Pre-University, in no
view of the matter can claim absorption in preference to six clerical
employees, namely, one Head clerk and five other Clerks already
absorbed, specially when there is no pleading that those persons, who
have been absorbed, are either junior to him or in any way
disqualified for being appointed and/or absorbed on the post of clerk.
 297. As a matter of fact there were 30 claimants for these
six posts of clerk and if therefore the Review Committee had rejected
the case of the petitioner and others on the ground of their
appointment being not as per prescribed norms and also having no
approval of the Governing Body, no flaw can be found in such
decision.
 298. The case of the petitioner Kanhaiya Chaubey for
absorption at best on a post of Clerk can be considered along with 30
other claimants as discussed in CWJC No. 9727 of 2008.
 299. With the aforesaid observation and direction, this

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 205
writ application is disposed of.
 Re. C.W.J.C.No. 9800 of 2008
300. The sole petitioner claims his absorption on the post
of Assistant Clerk. His case is that he was appointed on the post of
Assistant Clerk on 1.2.1979 under the order of the Secretary to the
College and his services were made permanent under resolution No.4
of the Governing Body dated 22.4.1981.
301. According to the petitioner, his services were
terminated on 13.10.1984 but then under the resolution of the
Governing Body dated 16.1.1985 he was reappointed on the post of
Clerk. The petitioner has also produced a letter dated 2.6.1986 to
show that the Governing Body of the College on 30.5.1986 had taken
a decision to reinstate him in service and thus, his decision for
reappointment dated 16.1.1985 was also modified.
302. Learned counsel for the petitioner has submitted
that since the petitioner had passed the Higher Secondary
Examination, he had the requisite qualification for the post of
Assistant Clerk and as such, his absorption against any post of Clerk
on account of his being in service of the College on the cut-off date
on 1.6.1986 was well permissible.
303. Mr. D.K.Sinha, learned AAG, on the other hand,
has submitted that once the petitioner admits that his services were

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 206
terminated, his reappointment in view of the alleged resolution of the
Governing Body dated 16.1.1985 or his order of reinstatement dated
2.6.1986 will have no meaning in law, specially when this Court in
the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) has already held that any
appointment or decision taken by the Governing Body after 25.3.1984
was null and void inasmuch as the President of the Governing Body
had never participated in the meeting nor had singed the resolution
and, therefore, this Court had also held that the persons appointed
after 25.3.1984 were not at all entitled for absorption. He has also
submitted that at any point of time there was no sanctioned post of
Assistant Clerk either under the CCIM norms or under the norms of
the University and this, his appointment against un-sanctioned post
will have also no validity in the eye of law.
304. As has been discussed above, the power of
appointment of non-teaching employee including a Clerk/ Assistant
clerk was vested only in the Governing Body. Admittedly that was
not done in the case of the petitioner when he was allegedly appointed
by the Secretary of the College on 1.2.1979. His appointment letter
itself goes to show that the Governing Body was to take decision with
regard to his terms and conditions of service including the scale of
pay. Thus, this Court will have no difficulty in holding that his
appointment by the Secretary would confer him no right.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 207
305. Added to it, when his services were terminated as
has been admitted by the petitioner himself on 13.10.1984, he goes
out of zone of consideration for absorption because he did not remain
a working employee on the cut-off date i.e. 1.6.1986.. His claim that
the Governing Body in the meeting dated 16.1.1985 had sought to
reappoint him or in the subsequent meeting of the Governing Body
dated 2.6.1984 he was sought to be reinstated in place of
reappointment will also make no difference inasmuch as such
decision of the Governing Body dafter the date of 25.3.1984 has
already been held to be bad and tainted with fraud in the case of Dr.
Satyadeo Pandey (supra).
306. Thus the long and short of his case is that he stood
terminated from the service of the College on the cut of date and his
order of reinstatement passed on 2.6.1986 by the Secretary of the
College will also confer him no right for being absorbed in view of
cut-off date being 1.6.1986. Added to all these, there was no
sanctioned post of Assistant Clerk under the CCIM norms or under
the University Statutes and therefore, his appointment itself was
against a non-existent post.
307. Thus, this Court does not find any merit in the case
of the petitioner for his absorption and the same is accordingly
dismissed.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 208
 Re. C.W.J.C.No. 7736 of 2008
308. In this case there are in all ten petitioners, out of
whom petitioner no.1 claims to have been appointed on the post of
Store Attendant on 15th January, 1982 under the order of the
Secretary of the governing Body and his such appointment is said to
have been made permanent by the Governing Body of the College by
the alleged Resolution No.13 dated 14th September, 1985.
309. Learned counsel for the petitioners has submitted
that there was a duly sanctioned post of Store Attendant. He has also
submitted that the Review Committee had not even considered his
case while considering the case of Ramji Pandey and Nagendra
Chaubey.
310. Learned counsel for the State, on the other hand, has
shown from the CCIM norms that there was no sanctioned post of
Store Attendant and in fact there was no post in the Chikitsalaya
(dispensary) as was mentioned in the appointment letter of the
petitioner by the Secretary of the College. In other words, he submits
that not only the Secretary to the College in violation of the Statutes
relating to power of the Governing Body, the President and its
Secretary had illegally appointed the petitioner but his appointment
was also made against an unsanctioned and non-existing post of Store
Attendant.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 209
311. Learned counsel for the petitioners, in reply, has
submitted that though it is true that there is no sanctioned post of
Store Attendant under the CCIM norms but then he has referred to a
post of Outdoor Attendant and submits that the petitioner's absorption
could be made at least on the post of Outdoor Attendant.
312. This Court fails to understand the logic of such
argument, inasmuch as once it is admitted that there was no post of
Store Attendant under the CCIM norms, the petitioner cannot claim
absorption against the post of Outdoor Attendant. Thus, his
appointment itself being against a non-existing and unsanctioned post,
his claim for absorption has been rightly rejected and this Court does
not find any infirmity in the same.
313. As with regard to the claim of absorption of
petitioners no. 2, 6, 7 and 8, namely, Nagendra Chaubey, Ramji
Chaudhary, Uma Shankar Yadav and Sugriv Singh, they claim to
have been appointed on the post of Peons. The case of petitioner no.2
Nagendra Chaubey in this regard is that he had the qualification of
passing Class VII examination and he was appointed in the College
on the post of Peon on 10th November, 1981 and his such
appointment made by the Secretary was not only approved by the
Governing Body but he was also promoted on the post of Ward
Servant on 14th September, 1985.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 210
314. This Court would find it difficult to sustain his
claim for Ward Attendant, inasmuch as his such appointment by
promotion has been made by the Governing Body after 25.3.1984.
His case, however, for absorption on the post of Peon in view of his
remaining out of service from 29.8.2003 as against his post of Peon
can also not be considered in isolation, inasmuch as all the ten posts
of Peons have already been filled up by way of absorption of ten
Peons in the order dated 17.10.2007.
315. Nonetheless since the reason given in the impugned
order as with regard to rejection of his claim for absorption on Peon
being his appointment not approved by the Governing Body and this
Court from the record of the Governing Body would find that his such
appointment on the post of Peon was approved by the Governing
Body in the meeting held on 14.2.1982, his case would require
reconsideration in the light of the observations made in C.W.J.C.No.
7998/2008, wherein the issue of absorption on the post of Peon and
other Class IV employees have been discussed at length.
316. The aforesaid reasoning and directions given above
in the case of petitioner no.2 shall also apply with full force even in
respect of petitioner no.6 Ramji Chaudhary, who too was appointed
on the post of Peon by the Secretary to the College on 10.10.1981
with effect from 1.2.1982 and his such appointment being approved

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 211
by the Governing Body in the meeting held on 14.2.1982. Thus, when
the Review Committee had also rejected his case of absorption only
on the ground that his appointment was not approved by the
Governing Body and that reason is found to be incorrect on
confirmation from the proceedings of the Governing Body, the case of
petitioner no.6 Ramji Chaudhary would also require reconsideration
for his absorption alike the case of petitioner no.2 and others in the
light of observations and direction given in CWEJC NO. 7998 of
2008..
317. The case of petitioner no.7 Uma Shankar Yadav
again is quite similar to the case of petitioners no. 2 and 6, inasmuch
as his appointment also is said to have been made on the post of Peon
on 15th August, 1979 and approval of his such appointment by the
Secretary to the College was approved by the Governing Body in the
meeting held on 14.2.1982. Thus, the case of petitioner no.7 also
would require reconsideration, inasmuch as the Review Committee
had rejected his case for absorption only on the ground that his
appointment was not approved by the Governing Body.
318. There would be, however, a difficulty in giving
similar direction for reconsideration of the case of petitioner no.8 for
his absorption on the post of Peon on which he claims to have been
appointed on 1st July, 1973. Let it be noted that petitioner no.8 did

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 212
not produce his order of appointment and the Screening Committee in
his case had found a number of anomalies as also overwriting. As a
matter of fact petitioner no.8 had produced a duplicate copy of his
appointment letter issued by the Secretary dated 14.1.1986 in which
there was a mention of his appointment on Peon being made in the
College with effect from 1.7.1973. His certificate of educational
qualification by way of School Leaving Certificate of Smt. J.K.High
School, Kewna produced by him before the Screening Committee had
contained interpolation and even in the resolution of the Governing
Body dated 23.4.1981 under which he claims his services to have
been made permanent also does not contain his name. Thus, when the
service has also stood terminated with effect from 29.8.2003 and he
has already completed the age of 60 years much before filing of the
writ application on 9.5.2008, this Court is not persuaded to remit his
case for reconsideration for his absorption on the post of Peon.
319. The case of petitioners no. 3 and 4, namely,
Janardan Mishra and Surendra Nath Singh is for absorption on the
post of Ward Boy. It is the case of petitioner no.3 that on the basis of
his qualification of Matriculation he was appointed as a Ward Boy on
10th October, 1981 with effect from 2nd January, 1982 and he was
promoted on the post of Dresser by an order dated 18.2.1983 and
again got another promotion on the post of Surgical Assistant on 14th

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 213
September, 1985. Thus, petitioner no.3 on the basis of his promotion
on the post of Surgical Assistant with effect from 14.9.1985 claims
his absorption on the post of Surgical Assistant which he was
allegedly holding on the cut-off date of take-over i.e. 1.6.1986.
320. Learned counsel for the petitioners, however, has
fairly submitted that petitioner no.3 has no technical qualification for
the post of Dresser and the Surgical Assistant, which are specialized
technical post of Operation Theater in a Medical College. In that view
of the matter, this Court while rejecting the case of petitioner no.3 for
his absorption on the post of Dresser or Surgical Assistant would find
him eligible for at least consideration of his case against a Class IV
post of Ward Boy for which Prashashi Padbarg Samiti had accorded
approval for creation of 21 posts under the category of Outdoor
Assistant, Ward Boy, Female Servant, ANM, Cook Assistant, Labour
Room Attendant and Operation Theater Attendant.
321. It has to be also kept in mind that against the post of
Ward Boy only two persons, namely, Brijnath Chaubey and Laxam
Singh were found fit for absorption by the Review Committee and
consequently have been absorbed by the State Government on
17.10.2007. Thus, there being both availability of sanctioned post the
case of petitioner no.3 can be at least considered for absorption
against the post of Ward Boy.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 214
322. As with regard to the case of petitioner no.4
Janardan Mishra, his claims is that he had a qualification of
Madhyama and his appointment was made by the Secretary to the
Governing Body of the College on the post of Ward Boy on 26th
September, 1981 and subsequently the Governing Body had promoted
him on the post of Mishrak (Compounder) with effect from 8.1.1984.
Learned counsel for the petitioners has submitted that for the post of
Compounder there was no prescribed qualification and as such, when
on the cut-off date of takeover i.e. 1.8.1986 he was working on the
post of Compounder, his absorption should be made on the post of
Compounder.
323. This Court would find it difficult to accept the
submission of the learned counsel for the petitioners for absorption of
petitioner no.4 on the post of Compounder, inasmuch as either on the
basis of educational qualification of Madhyama, which is a
qualification of Sanskrit Curriculum or on the basis of his working on
the post of Ward Boy, he cannot claim the post of Compounder. For
the post of Compounder there has to be a technical qualification and
training. Thus, while this Court while rejecting the case of absorption
of petitioner no.4 on the post of Compounder would still find him
eligible for consideration of his case for absorption on the post of
Ward Boy, inasmuch as his case stands on the same footing as that of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 215
one Ravindra Chaubey, who has been directed to be absorbed on the
post of Ward Boy. As noted above, there are vacancies on the post of
Ward Boy as per the creation of post made by Prashashi Padbarg
Samiti and therefore, alike the case of petitioner no.3 the case of
petitioner no.4 should also be considered for his absorption on the
post of Ward Boy.
324. The case of petitioner no.5 Surendra Nath Singh
again is very similar to the case of petitioner no.3, inasmuch as he
also on the basis of qualification of Matriculation was initially said to
be appointed by the Secretary to the College on 12.11.1981 on the
post of Ward Boy and was subsequently transferred on the post of
Dresser by an order dated 18.2.1983 and ultimately was promoted on
the post of Surgical Assistant on 14.5.1985. Admittedly petitioner
no.5 also has no qualification or technical training for the post of
Dresser and Surgical Assistant and therefore, this Court while
rejecting his case for absorption on the post of Dresser and Surgical
Assistant would still find him eligible for reconsideration of his case
for absorption against the post of Ward Boy in the same manner as in
the case of petitioner no.3 and petitioner no.4, specially when there
are availability of sanctioned post of Ward Boy.
325. The case of petitioner no. 9 Murlidhar Singh while
claiming absorption on the post of Pharmacist is that though his

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 216
qualification was only Praveshika (Matriculation), he was appointed
by the Secretary to the Governing Body of the College on 1011.1981
and after he had jointed the post on 22.1.1982 he was made
permanent on the post of Pharmacist on 14.9.1985. His absorption,
however, was refused by the first Screening Committee and led to his
termination of service on 29.8.2003 on the ground that he had no
qualification for the post of Pharmacist and a simply Matriculate
could not be appointed under any norms on the post of Pharmacist.
326. The Review Committee constituted under the order
of the judgment of this Court in the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey had
specifically considered the case of petitioner no.9 with four others for
the post of Pharmacist and had held that Diploma in Pharmacy is a
minimum requirement for the post of Pharmacist. Thus, this Court
had no reason to differ with the view taken by the Review Committee
and its acceptance by the State Government in rejecting the case of
petitioner no.9 for his absorption on the post of Pharmacist as he lacks
the qualification. The case of absorption of petitioner no.9 is,
accordingly, rejected.
327. The last petitioner of this writ application, petitioner
no.10, is Shashi Bhushan Singh, who claims that on the basis of his
qualification of Madhyama he was appointed as Laboratory Boy (Lab.
Boy) on 15.1.1982 and eventually got promoted on the post of

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 217
Dresser on 5.7.1986 though the Screening Committee in his case had
found certain anomaly on the ground of his transfer on the post of
Ward Boy on 3.10.1985 as also his promotion from Ward Boy to
Dresser on 2.6.1986 and the Review Committee has also rejected his
case for absorption on the post of Dresser on the ground of lack of
qualification and training. This Court would find that there are two
sanctioned post Laboratory Attendant and therefore, the case of
petitioner no.10, who still has around six years of service in view of
his date of birth being 3.2.1962 should be considered against the post
of Laboratory Attendant, inasmuch as there is no sanctioned post of
Lab Boy either under the CCIM norms or in the creation of 191 post
made by the Prashashi Padbarg Samiti.
328. To that extent while issuing such direction for
consideration of the case of petitioner no.10 for consideration of his
absorption on the post of Laboratory Attendant, this Court is mindful
that the Review Committee and the State Government under the
impugned order have also made absorption on the post of Carpenter
(Barahai), Gardener and Barber of Radhy Shyam, Uma Shankar
Chaudhary, Ramprit Gaur and late Ramashish Thakur. This in fact
was permissible in view of the provisions made under section 6(3) of
the Act but then as there is a vacancy also on the post of Laboratory
Assistant, the absorption of petitioner no.10 can be considered against

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 218
the post of Laboratory Attendant or any of 21 Class IV post clubbed
together in the category of Ward Boy, Outdoor Attendant and
Operation Theater Attendant etc.
329. With the aforesaid observation and direction, this
writ application is disposed of.
 Re. :C.W.J.C.No. 7456 of 2003
330. The three petitioners, namely, Bipin Bihari
Chaubey, Krishna Bihari Prasad and Dharmjay Prasad Lal, in this writ
application have assailed an order dated 18.11.2002, whereby and
whereunder their services were terminated by the State Government.
According to the petitioners, petitioner no.1 Bipin Bihari Chaubey
was appointed on the post of Laboratory Technician on 1.1.1982 and
his such appointment made by the Secretary was made permanent by
the Governing Body in Resolution No.7 dated 16.1.1982.
331. Similarly, petitioner no.2 has claimed that his
appointment was made in the College under the order of the Secretary
of the Governing Body on 26.2.1982 on the post of Technical
Assistant and the Governing Body in the meeting held on 14.9.1985
had made his services permanent on the post of Technical Assistant.
332. Petitioner no.3 Dharmjay Prasad Lal claims to have
been appointed on the post of Laboratory Assistant in the Department
of Sharir Rachana (Anatomy) under the order of the Secretary to the

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 219
College dated 30.1.1982 and he too claims to have been made
permanent in the meeting of the Governing Body held on 14.9.1985.
333. Thus, all the petitioners claim to be working in the
College on the cut-off date of takeover i.e. 1.6.1986 on their
respective post of Laboratory Technician, Technical Assistant and
Lab. Assistant. According to the petitioners, their services after the
takeover of the College was not taken over even as an ad hoc
employees, whereafter they had moved this Court in C.W.J.C.Nos.
929/1988 and 2963/1988 and this Court had directed the State
Government to constitute the Committee to enquire into the allegation
of the petitioners and take a decision for their absorption if they are
found to have been validly and genuinely appointed.
334. According to the petitioners, the Committee
constituted by the State Government had found them to be not validly
appointed as there was no sanctioned post available for them and they
were also not having the prescribed qualification. Thereafter they had
moved again this Court in C.W.J.C.No. 5674/1996, which was
disposed of by an order dated 3.9.1997 with a direction to the
respondents to give an opportunity to the petitioners to approach the
Secretary of the Department by demonstrating that they had the
qualification and the post on which they claim their appointment and
absorption was sanctioned. It is the case of the petitioners that

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 220
thereafter they were absorbed on the post of Laboratory Technician,
Technical Assistant and Lab. Assistant by an order dated 17.12.1997
but all of a sudden their services were terminated again by the
impugned order dated 18.11.2002 without complying the principles of
natural justice.
335. Mr. Ashok Kumar Singh, learned Senior counsel
appearing on behalf of the petitioners, had submitted that since an
interim order was already passed by this Court in this case on
22.8.2003 staying operation of their termination, the petitioners will
be deemed to be continuing in service and as such, this Court is not
required to decide anything else except validity of their order of
termination, inasmuch as they have already stood absorbed in the
services of the College.
336. This Court would find it difficult to accept such
submission for a simple reason that all such Committee's reports and
absorption were set at naught and the direction was given for
constituting a fresh Screening Committee and the State Government
to take a decision on the basis of the recommendation of the
Screening Committee. Thereafter when the order removing 223
persons were passed on 29.8.2003 the issue had again been decided
by this Court in the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra) remitting the
matter back for consideration of absorption. As a matter of fact on

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 221
account of the interim order passed in this case the decision could not
be taken in case of petitioners.
337. In this case a counter affidavit also has been filed,
wherein it has been stated that the absorption order of the petitioner
was itself a fraudulent order which was never passed by the State
Government. On the other hand, the report of the Screening
Committee has been placed on record to show that the Screening
Committee had found appointment of all the three petitioners to be
wholly illegal and against un-sanctioned post and therefore, the claim
of their absorption was itself unsustainable. Additionally it is also
stated that the petitioners were also given a show cause notice
enclosing the copy of the screening report but the petitioners had
never filed their show cause reply, which went to show that they had
admitted the allegations made against them relating to non-availability of sanctioned post as well as lack of qualification for the
post on which they were appointed.
338. This Court on perusal of the counter affidavit,
averments whereof have not been controverted by the petitioners
despite service of copy on them, would find that petitioner no.1 had
passed his Matriculation examination in 1974 and Intermediate in
Science in 1991 and therefore, as on the date of his appointment he
was simply Matriculate on the basis of which he could not have been

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 222
appointed on the post of Laboratory Technician. His passing the
Intermediate Examination in the year 1991 will also make no
difference and therefore, this Court would find that when petitioner
no.1 alike petitioners no. 2 and 3 are also not working in the College
even after passing of the interim order staying operation of their
termination order, he would hardly be entitled for any relief, much
less for quashing of the order of his termination.
339. The case of petitioner no.2 is still worse, inasmuch
as he was appointed on the post of Technical Assistant, a post which
ahs never been there in the CCIM norms. Petitioner no.2 in fact is
only an Intermediate of Arts and therefore, his claim of appointment
on the post of Technical Assistant on 25.2.1982 and the alleged
approval of his appointment by the Governing Body on 14.9.1985
will also have no meaning in the eye of law, specially when after
25.4.1984 the proceedings of the Governing Body were manipulated
which have also been not found to be worth any reliance in the
judgment of this Court in the case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra).
340. Finally, as with regard to the case of petitioner no.3
claiming appointment and absorption on the post of Laboratory
Assistant in the department of Sharir Rachana (Anatomy) it is found
that his appointment made by the Secretary of the College on
30.1.1982 was in fact never approved even by the Governing Body

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 223
till 26.3.1984 and on 14.9.1985 his services were sought to be made
permanent on the post of Laboratory Assistant. This petitioner again
has no qualification for the post, inasmuch as he is only Matriculate
of the year 1974 and therefore, this Court would not find any
difficulty in either approving the report of the Screening Committee
which was made the basis for termination of the service of the
petitioner and that too after following the principles of natural justice
by way of show cause notice.
341. This Court in order to be sure that whether the
petitioner's case would require reconsideration had specifically asked
the learned Senior counsel appearing for the petitioners as to whether
the petitioners were still continuing in service but Mr. Singh very
fairly had said that he was not aware of the present status of the
petitioner and in that background this Court had directed the
respondents to file supplementary counter affidavit which was sworn
and filed on 30.9.2015 by none-else but the Director, Indigenous
System of Medicine, himself wherein it was clearly stated that all the
three petitioners were not in service of the College as per written
information given to him by the Principal of the College.
342. Thus, if the petitioners even after the interim order
of stay of their order of termination have not been in service for more
than 12 to 13 years, there would be no question of issuing any

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 224
direction for their absorption. It appears to the Court that the
petitioners have abandoned their claim.
343. That being so, this writ application must be and is
hereby dismissed.
Re.: C.W.J.C.No. 12064 of 2008
344. In this case all the three petitioners are claiming
absorption on the post of Laboratory Technician. According to the
petitioners, petitioner no.1 was appointed on the post of Laboratory
Technician on 9.11.1981 in the department of Ras Shastra, whereas
petitioner no.2 was appointed on the post of Laboratory Technician in
Sharir Kriya and petitioner no.3 Awadesh Chaubey on the post of
Laboratory Technician in Maulik Sidhyant. According to the
petitioners, their qualification of Intermediate in Science would be
good enough for their appointment and absorption on the post of
Laboratory Technician.
345. Let it be kept in mind that the cases of petitioners
had earlier also been considered in the batch case of Dr. Satyadeo
Pandey, wherein it was held as follows:
"In C.W.J.C.No. 19830 of 2008 the sole petitioner Ganeshji
Pandey was denied absorption on the ground that he did not
fulfil the qualifications laid down by the State Government
for the post of Laboratory Technician. The petitioner has
shown on the basis of Annexure 9 that persons having
qualification of I.Sc. which petitioner has, are working as
Laboratory Technician in Magadh University, petitioner was
appointed in the year 1981 whereas Annexure 9 is of a later
date. According to the counter affidavit of respondent no.4
essential qualification for medical Laboratory Technician
has been fixed by the State Governemnt because the CCIM

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 225
does not fix qualifications for non-teaching staff. It has been
asserted that the necessary qualifcaiton for the post is I.Sc.
with two years training as Lab. Technician. It further
appears that petitioner was a party to C.W.J.C.No.11673 of
1995 which was disposed of by remitting their case for
decision by the Health Commissioner but ultimately the
same was rejected by the State Government on the report of
the Screening Committee. Neither the report of Screening
Committee nor the counter affidavit discloses when the State
Government prescribed the qualification claimed by the
State. It is also not clear how the State Government could
prescribe qualification for the college when ordinarily it
should have been done by the Bihar University to which the
college was affiliated. Petitioner has a good case on the
basis of order of Magadh University contained in Annexure
9 but since the Bihar University is not a party to the writ
petition, there is nothing on record to show whether Bihar
University had laid down qualification for the post of Lab
Technician for affiliated Colleges or not. If the Bihar
University at the relevant time had laid down qualification
for the post in question the same should be made the
yardsticks for deciding the claim of Laboratory Technician
otherwise the State Government will be at liberty to apply
the qualification laid down by it if such decision was in
existence at the time of appointment of the petitioner in the
year 1981 as Laboratory Technician. This may be treated as
a further direction for deciding claims of Laboratory
Technicians in addition to general directions even by this
Court earlier in this order."
346. Pursuant thereto, the petitioners have tried to
emphasize that the Bihar University had given in writing on 2.6.2008
that the qualification of Laboratory Technician was only Intermediate
in Science. Learned counsel for the petitioners thus had submitted that
all the three petitioners would be deemed to have been qualified on
account of their being Intermediate in Science and thus, fit for being
absorbed. Since this stand of the Bihar University was contested by
the learned counsel for the State this Court had directed the Bihar
University to file a fresh affidavit and the Bihar University in its latest
affidavit dated 10.11.2013 had clarified as follows:
"2. That it is stated that the then Registrar BRA Bihar University

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 226
had issued two letters bearing no. B/270 and B/2148 dated
21.1.2011 and 11.10.2014 respectively wherein it was stated that
the requisite qualification for Laboratory Technician was "I.Sc."
3. That it is stated that the basis of the said statement, submitted
to State Government, was a previous letter issued by the State
Government bearing letter No. 9690 dated 17th August, 1979.
4. That it is further stated that the University proceeded on the
basis of qualification prescribed, in the said letter bearing letter
no. 9690 dated 17th August, 1979, for Laboratory In-charge to
state that the required qualification for Laboratory Technician
was "I.Sc."
5. That it is further stated that the University has not prescribed
any qualification or framed any statute with regard to
qualification required for appointment in Class III and IV as they
were merely assigned the task to conduct examinations in the
Ayurvedic Colleges and nothing more."
347. It, thus, became clear that the Bihar University had
fixed no norms for appointment on the post of Laboratory Technician.
As per the judgment and directions of this Court in the case of Dr.
Satyadeo Pandey (supra) in absence of qualification laid down by the
Bihar University the qualification fixed by the State Government had
to be followed. The State Government in the year 1981 had already
fixed the qualification for the post of Laboratory Technician, wherein
it was prescribed as follows:

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 227
348. Thus, from the reading of the aforementioned letter
produced by Mr.D.K.Sinha, learned AAG, it becomes very clear that
the Review Committee had committed no error in relying on the
Government decision with regard to qualification of MLT to be a
condition precedent for appointment on the post of Laboratory
Technician.
349. Judged in this background this Court will not find
any merit in the case of the petitioners for their absorption on the post
of Laboratory Technician, inasmuch as none of them in their
appointment made in the year 1981 had undergone or completed even
one year MLT course which now for the present has been enhanced to
two years DMLT course.
350. The petitioners in fact are bound by the inter-party
judgment, wherein it was made clear that in absence of qualification
prescribed by the Bihar University for the post of Laboratory
Technician, the qualification laid down by the State Government
could be made applicable for examining their cases of absorption.
That having been done this Court cannot now take any different view
in view of the aforesaid Government decision becoming final and
conclusive between the parties.
351. In the result, this writ application fails and is,
accordingly, dismissed.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 228
Re.: C.W.J.C.No. 15019 of 2008
352. The case of the two petitioners Ganesh Ji Pandey
and Akhileshwar Kumar Pathak for their absorption on the post of
Laboratory Technician is almost exactly the same as that of the
petitioner of C.W.J.C.No. 12064/2008. In their cases also their
appointment on the post of Laboratory Technician on the basis of
their qualification of Intermediate in Science was made on 12.11.1981
and 30.1.1982 without either of them having the prescribed
qualification of one year training MLT, as discussed earlier in
C.W.J.C.No. 12064/2008.
353. Thus, for the reasons given in C.W.J.C.No.
12064/2008, this application also must fail and is, accordingly,
dismissed.
Re.: C.W.J.C.No. 11003 of 2008
354. In this case the petitioner Usha Devi is actually
claiming absorption of her husband Balmiki Pandey, who was
appointed as an Assistant Clerk on 2.4.1982. Balmiki Pandey was
said to be having qualification upto Intermediate and he is said to
have died on 16.9.2004. His writ application filed against the earlier
order of his termination dated 29.8.2003 was remanded alongwith
other batch case of Dr. Satyadeo Pandey (supra).
355. This Court would find that there is no sanctioned

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 229
post of Assistant Clerk under any norms either under CCIM or under
the University Statutes. Therefore, this Court would direct that the
case of the husband of the petitioner may be considered along with
similarly situated 30 other clerks against three remaining vacancies as
per direction given in CWJC No. 9727 of 2008.
356. With the aforesaid observation and direction, this
writ application is disposed of.
Re.: C.W.J.C. No. 12366 of 2008
357. In this case again the petitioner Most Rajkishori
Devi, the widow of late Rabindra Kumar Pandey claims absorption of
the services of her husband, who is said to have been appointed on the
post of Clerk cum Typist by the Secretary of the Governing Body of
the College.
358. In the case of the husband of the petitioner, it had,
however, got revealed that he had no qualification for the post on the
date of his appointment on 9.11.1981, inasmuch as he had passed
even his Intermediate Examination in the year 1984 and acquired
training in typing in the year 1991. Thus, on the date when the
husband of the petitioner claims to have been appointed on the post of
Clerk cum Typist he had neither the qualification of Intermediate,
which was a minimum qualification for such post nor he had
knowledge of typing.

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 230
359. Such a person can never be absorbed, specially
when there are large number of qualified person on the post of Clerk,
who have not been absorbed on account of lack of vacancies. Thus,
this Court will have no difficulty in holding that the appointment
made by the Secretary to the Managing Committee in respect of the
husband of the petitioner was a fraudulent exercise because he had
appointed the husband of the petitioner on the post of Clerk cum
Typist when the latter was not having any training of typing which on
his own showing, he had acquired in the year 1991.
360. The case of the petitioner can also not be equated
with those of the Clerks who are claiming for them knowledge of
typing as desirable and not essential. Here in this case the
appointment letter of the husband of the petitioner shows that he was
sought to be appointed on the post of Clerk cum typist but if he never
knew typing, his appointment made by the Secretary to the Managing
Committee would only amount to a fraud which was again
perpetuated by the Governing Body which is said to have approved
the appointment of the husband of the petitioner on 31.7.1985 i.e.
after cut-off date of 25.3.1984. Thus, all these aspects considered in
isolation or collectively would definitely disentitle the petitioner to
claim absorption of her husband late Ravindra Kumar Pandey.
361. In the result, this writ application fails and is

Patna High Court CWJC No.7246 of 2008 231
accordingly dismissed.
362. Having decided the cases of all the petitioners
individually, this Court must clarify that wherever direction has been
issued for reconsideration of the concerned petitioners in batch of
those writ petitions and eventually if they are absorbed on such post,
they will not be entitled for any back wages from the date they have
been removed from service i.e. 29.8.2003 and till the date of their
absorption and if in the meantime they have attained the age of
superannuation , their services shall be computed from the date of
takeover of the college for the purpose of retirement benefit only.
Those non-teaching persons, who have not completed the age of 60
years and upon reconsideration can be absorbed on any Class IV post
shall be entitled for their payment of salary from the date the
Government decides to absorb them in service with of course
continuity of service for the period they had remained out of service
on account of their termination of order dated 29.8.2003.
Surendra/Rishi/Sujit/
Ranjan
 (Mihir Kumar Jha, J)
